# Changelog

Published artifacts and authoritative release notes are in [GitHub Releases](https://github.com/trina-app/trina-ax/releases).
This file summarizes user-visible changes; it is not a list of every commit.

## Unreleased

## 0.5.3 — 2026-11-13

- Renaming a Trina chat, moving it to a project, changing its AI model, deleting it or bringing it back from Deleted history now shows at once, and so do creating, renaming and deleting a conversation folder and moving a conversation into or out of one. Before, each waited for the server and the row could jump when it answered. If the change fails, it goes back and a short message says why.
- **Slack: Trina reads the thread you ask in, with the Slack app's own permissions.** Mention @Trina in a thread and it reads the thread so far; ask in the app about a Slack channel or thread and it reads channels it was invited to that you are in too (`/invite @Trina` first). It no longer reads Slack as you, has no Slack search, and nobody has to "Allow reading Slack". Nothing of Slack's messages is stored beyond the chat's answer.
- Admin › Connected apps › Slack shows **Update Slack permissions** when the Slack app does not have the two reading permissions yet. A team's own app: add `channels:history` and `groups:history` as Bot Token Scopes in Slack, reinstall the app, then press **I updated them**. The official app: **Add to Slack** again.
- An answer that used Slack messages from another channel or a DM still asks first before **Post to channel**.
- The AI no longer offers a "connect card" for Slack; it says how Slack is reached instead.

- **Activity** on a computer selects only the notice you opened. Two notices that open the same page (two people who joined one project) were both shown selected, filled and in bold, while that page was open.
- **A message's hover bar** under the first message of a conversation stays on top of the next message's bar where they meet, so its buttons can be clicked while that message is hovered.

- **Activity** has its **DMs** tab again, between All and Mentions: direct and group chats' notices are under it, and **Mark all as read** works there as on the other tabs. The 멘션·스레드 page opened from a phone's 대화 tab still has no DMs tab.
- **Activity** has **Mark all as read** for the tab you are on: All reads every notice, DMs, Mentions and Threads only theirs, including ones not loaded yet. A conversation is read up to its newest message, as opening it does, so the room's unread count, the Activity count and the phone app's icon number go down at once, on your other devices too. The button shows only while the tab has something unread.
- **Add an app for the team** (Admin › Connectors) now starts with a list of known apps, with their icons and a search; picking one fills in its name, address and sign-in, so you only confirm and sign in. **Custom app (enter an address)** is the last choice.
- The add-app dialog reads like Team settings: each field with one short line and its box, select or switch on the right, normal-size text, and no red errors on a fresh form until you save or leave a field you typed in. Who it is for and the sign-in note share one quiet line, **Change** opens who can use it right there (it is no longer repeated inside Advanced), and **Advanced** is just the word, with plain selects instead of big option buttons.
- Adding a listed app shows its icon next to "Add Notion" and its address as one small line.
- Admin: Team settings and System now sit in the middle of the page on wide screens instead of at the left edge.
- **Tests**: the admin browser test reads the Folder permissions Save button's current wording ("2명 열 수 있게 됨" / "2 can now open it", from #346) instead of the old "권한 늘어남", so it passes again locally and in the feature map.
- **Always allow on every card**: every confirmation card Trina shows now offers **Always allow** on the first try: changes after reading conversations, remembering, saving where more people can see, posting to a conversation, reading in a shared chat, moving files, skills, deleting files, project members, AI videos and very large downloads. Pick it once and Trina stops asking about that kind of change. Your choices are listed in Settings › Allowed for Trina, where you can delete any of them. Admins can turn the option off for the team.
- **Connectors**: "Always allow" on a connector now holds after Trina has read web pages, files or conversations in the chat too.
- **Desktop browser**: "Allow all websites" also opens every public website in the desktop app's browser, and the browser's own card offers it on the first site.
- **Ask a member to connect** (Admin › Connected apps): pick the member from a list with their picture, name and id, and search it by name. The popup is shorter: one line on top, short notes, and no red message before you press Send request.
- Operators of the relay and the push gateway can get error reports and cron check-ins in their own Sentry project: set `RELAY_SENTRY_DSN` or `PUSH_GATEWAY_SENTRY_DSN` on the host. Nothing is sent without it. The push gateway reports when Apple or Google refuse its own credentials, not when one phone's token is dead. The relay's nightly backup, firewall-range refresh and a new 15-minute Litestream check report each run as `relay-registry-backup`, `relay-cf-ips-refresh` and `relay-litestream`.
- **Rooms**: an empty message box keeps its hint on one line (cut at the end) instead of growing, at the largest text size or with a wide system font; the hover bar's ⋯ and the message box's + no longer sit 1 px off with such a font.
- A connector's page for Notion, Higgsfield, Figma and the other listed services, and for a connection added by its address, now looks like the Google connector pages: the name with its one status and ⋯ on the right, About, what the AI may do, the tools as chips and a small details block. Check again, Edit and Delete are in ⋯, the tools' switches under "Turn tools on or off", and an error shows once with what to do next.
- A listed service's page shows all the tools it lists with **Show all**, in place. The link to the service's own page stays.
- A member's request for a service stays on its page ("Request sent" and **Cancel request**) while it is open, also when the team has added the service but not finished connecting it.
- An open request always says "Request sent", also after the page is opened again.
- **Connectors** moved from the sidebar into **Settings › Connectors**, like Claude's. It shows the same list, and a connector's page opens inside Settings with a way back to the list. Old links and bookmarks still open it, and every hint and notice now says Settings › Connectors.
- Intro decks: in the brand-colour look, the big numbers, the footer and the contact labels on the coloured slides are easy to read again. The lighter shades in the coloured background are now kept as dark as the brand colour, and small labels reach 4.5:1.
- On a computer, going back to Projects or Files now shows the list at once and updates it in the background. Before, each visit showed a loading placeholder and loaded the list again. The placeholder shows only the first time.
- On a computer the text is larger and easier to read, at the same sizes as on a phone: lists, menus, buttons, fields, tables, times and hints are a step bigger, and chat answers and room messages too. The smallest text (tags, notes under the message box) is now as large as other small text. Phones are unchanged, but for two small notes that were too small there as well.
- Team settings > Dev environment: the messages that appear when a change cannot be saved (the server cannot run it, a site address that is not allowed, too many sites, an unknown group or person, the warning to accept first) now come in the person's own language instead of English.
- Dev environment: on a Mac team server the folder check reads the path of the opened file from the system, so a folder swapped for a link during the check can no longer slip a file from outside into the file history. Linux is unchanged.
- Team settings › Dev environment: when the server refuses to turn it on, the switch goes back off instead of staying on until the page is reloaded, and the "cannot run" note uses the same friendly Korean as the error.
- Dev environment: on a Mac team server, a file reached through a link to a folder outside the project is no longer read into the file history (the check that was Linux-only now runs on every system).
- A team server on a Mac (`scripts/install-macos.sh`, a team's own Mac mini, one team) now has the dev environment: the AI's commands run in srt's macOS sandbox (Seatbelt), each run as its own dev account (`_axdev1` ... `_axdev8`), never as the server account. The installer turns it on by default; `--no-dev-env` keeps it off and removes it, and `uninstall-macos.sh` removes it too. A failure is a warning: the server runs without it. Office mode (no sudo) stays without it.
- On a Mac a dev server must listen on `$PORT` (each run has its own port; no other localhost port works). The server reaches it there directly, for a later preview link.
- Weaker than Linux on a Mac: memory is watched (RSS once a second) instead of a cgroup limit, there is no CPU quota (runs get a lower priority), and a run can see the names of the folders above the ones it may open.
- Settings › Security & devices: after you change your password, the list of signed-in devices shows only this device at once (the other devices were already signed out). Signing out a device that is already gone just removes its row instead of showing "Not found or no access."
- Tests: the browser tests follow the navigation of #683 (프로젝트 as its own place, the phone 트리나 tab as before).
- **Tests**: the last feature-map failures after the navigation rail: the invite step's one intro, English skill tabs as a picker in a wide font, room links opened from the keyboard, a project dialog closed before the next page, one more dragover before a real drop.
- **Tests**: browser tests now cover requesting account deletion, the password reset link, demo mode, intro deck slides and downloads, promo video MP4s, the team data export and the browser's long-poll fallback, so these features no longer show as only partly tested in the feature map.
- **Tests**: web e2e specs follow screens changed on purpose (pinned chats first in the chat menu, the room name's screen-reader words, phone pages without the computer's buttons, the offline page in the device's language, Mark all as read) and no longer depend on the system font or browser timing (the phone bar's back label, the IME check, a theme switch before an accessibility check, a sheet closed by a fling).
- **Rooms**: a press on a reaction under a message closes an open emoji picker, as a press anywhere else outside it does.
- Tests: the wide-window column check measures the phone width once the page has laid out (it failed on a loaded machine).
- For contributors: the feature map's e2e coverage now also counts endpoints a spec answers in the browser (`page.route`, the rooms mock), so room features such as thread replies no longer show Untested. The dropped PC runner is no longer a feature; the setup's folder plan and skills made from a form file are new ones.
- **CI:** the Feature map workflow now ends red when a web e2e test fails, and lists the failed tests in the run summary. The coverage files and the Features tab are still written first. It stays a non-required check.
- **CI**: the feature map's e2e shards install pandoc, bubblewrap and fontconfig and turn on converter isolation as the main CI job does, so the Hangul page previews and the docx/pptx samples run there as they do locally.
- For contributors: every API endpoint and web screen now belongs to a named feature in `docs/features.ts`, and `bun run features:check` (in CI) says which feature a new one should join. The e2e run records which endpoints it reached, for the QA sheet's new Features tab (docs/FEATURES.md).
- The file list is always on: Admin › Team settings › File list no longer has an off switch, only its status and **Run now**. A team that had turned it off gets it back, and its files are read again over the next nights. Only the server's host can still turn it off (`server.json`).
- The in-app AI is told the file list is up to a night old: a file added or changed today may be missing or described as it was, so the AI searches names and contents before it says a file is not there, and answers from the opened file.
- A form skill's card lists only the (at most three) questions teammates see; the rest stay under "더 자세히 정하기".
- When a Hangul form cannot be filled, the AI now says to turn on the Hangul (HWP) plugin in Admin › Plugins, not the dev environment. A form skill saves its file once, so a chat that read a room asks only once.
- **Move**: a folder you ✕ no longer shows under "Recent" for those files, and ✕ no longer moves weak folders up into "Suggested".
- Gmail, Google Calendar, Google Docs and Google Sheets can now be shared with the team: an owner or admin connects one Google account once (Admin › Connectors › the app › Team settings › **Shared Google account**) and picks who may use it, and those people use it in their chats without connecting their own. Only owners and admins can connect, change or remove it; members see "Shared by an admin" on the app's page and can still connect their own account. Changes such as sending a mail still ask first.
- **Scheduled task cards** call your own folder "내 파일" (My files), as the app does, instead of "내 폴더".
- **The AI's project-members card** no longer shows a character count ("24자") under the project, the people added and the people removed: they are names, not text that goes out.
- **대화 on a computer**: when the conversation list cannot be read, its panel says so calmly with **다시 시도** (it was empty, with no way to try again), as 트리나's list does.
- **A message's hover bar** sits over the message's top edge, not over its first line, so a link at the end of that line can be clicked with the mouse.
- **트리나에게 묻기** (and ⇧⌘O) while Home is already open puts the cursor in the message box, as from any other page.
- **Phone files**: the team folder's ‹ says "파일", and the trash opened from the team folder says "‹ 팀 폴더", where it goes back to.
- **Phone 대화 list**: a message's preview is plain words, without Markdown marks such as `**` or `` ` ``.
- **Trina history is grouped by project**, like the Claude Code desktop app: each project's chats sit together under its name, the newest work first, and chats in no project come last under "Other chats". Each project shows three chats ("N more" for the rest). ▾ folds a project (remembered), and a folded project still shows "?" when a chat inside waits for you. + at the right of a project's header asks Trina in that project. Without any chat in a project the list looks as before.
- **Search (⌘K) › Needs attention** now shows the same marks and words as your Trina list: "?" when Trina is waiting for your answer (it said "Answering now"), and a dot when a new answer is there that you have not seen (it was missing).
- **Search › History**: each chat says, in grey on the right, when it was last active and which project it is in. Opened on a project's page or in a project's chat, History lists that project's chats first.
- **Pin to top** is also in the chat's own title menu.
- **File version history**: a version saved by the AI has **Open the chat**, which opens the chat that saved it, when you can open that chat.
- A file dropped anywhere on Home or on a project's page goes into the new-chat box, as on an open chat: the same overlay while it is dragged, then a chip in the box. Before, only a drop right on the box worked there.
- After the AI moves files to the trash, the day it tells you they can be restored until is the team's day, the same one the card and the trash list show. Near midnight it could say one day earlier.
- A self-install on Debian 13 turns the dev environment (srt) on by itself: the installer runs `install-srt.sh` once the server answers. You no longer run it by hand. `--no-dev-env` keeps it off (and removes it on a repair); `--dev-env` stops the install when this system cannot have it. Ubuntu 24.04, Debian 12, `--minimal` and `--no-systemd` stay without it, and the installer says why.
- The server kit carries the srt install files (root/dev-env-srt/). `mycel-ctl update` runs the new `install-srt.sh` when srt is on and a release changes them, so a new pinned srt or node comes with the update.
- `uninstall` removes the dev environment too (`uninstall-srt.sh`; `--purge` also deletes its run data).
- Invite people: one line on what to do, the invite link on one line (shortened in the middle) with a Copy button, and the message to send below it in smaller, quieter text.
- In dark mode, the team page no longer starts white for a moment before its colours load, also when you chose Dark while your phone or computer is light.
- iPhone app: swiping back from the left edge to the top of a tab now slides over that screen instead of a white flash, which was blinding in dark mode. This part comes with the next app update.
- Invite people › Manage link and the team invite link popup on Members read more cleanly: the link is one short line with Copy link and a QR code button beside it, and the details are a tidy list (joined, works until, groups with Change, joins as member, seats left). "Copy link only" is now "Copy link".
- Phone app (iPhone and Android): a photo or video picked with 사진 보관함 now reaches the message box. The app's own photo picker opened, but after picking nothing was attached and nothing was said. If a pick is still lost, the box now says 사진을 가져오지 못했어요.
- Phone app 1.0.5: 사진 보관함 in a message box's attach sheet opens the phone's own photo picker right away. On the iPhone the small menu (Photo Library / Take Photo / Choose File) no longer comes first. Pick several photos or videos; a HEIC photo arrives as a JPEG. A file over 50 MB is left out with a short message. Older app versions keep the previous picker.
- Phone app: a dropped connection or a server hiccup no longer shows the team address and "다른 팀에 연결" under a page you are signed in to; the app also checks again when it comes back to the foreground.
- **Move** suggests where a file goes: up to three folders whose names and files fit the file's name show on top as "Suggested" (추천), then the folders you recently moved or saved into ("Recent", 최근), then all folders. Only folders you can save into; no AI is used. ✕ stops suggesting that folder for files named like it.
- **Trina list**: a chat started or changed while the list was off screen (a phone's new-chat page, a chat) is in the list when you come back, without a reload.
- **Tests**: web e2e specs follow the rail navigation; the feature map keeps a failed shard's screenshots and traces.
- **Projects have their own place**: 프로젝트 is a rail item on computers and a tab on phones (트리나 · 대화 · 프로젝트 · 파일 · 나). Its list has **팀 프로젝트** and **내 프로젝트**, each project with who sees it, when it was last used and how many chats it holds; a project opens on its page with its chats.
- **새 프로젝트 asks only the name**: a new project is yours alone, and people can be added later from its page with **멤버 추가** (a confirm says what that means). A chat's **프로젝트로 옮기기** can make a new project for it in one step, and an empty project page offers **기존 대화 가져오기**. Sharing a personal project now moves its files instead of leaving a second copy (the old folder goes to your trash).
- **트리나's list** on computers: 트리나에게 묻기, 검색, 예약 작업 and 팀 스킬 stay on top; below them **고정** and **최근 기록**, where a project's chat shows its project's name. The list's own folders are gone (대화's folders stay).
- **Clearer lists**: section names are small and grey and fold with a ▸; a folder's rows are indented under a faint line.
- **Phones**: the 트리나 tab is back to how it was, the new-chat screen with the history behind ☰.
- **New navigation.** On a computer, a narrow bar on the left has four places, each with its name: **Trina** (your chats with the AI), **Chats** (team rooms, group conversations and direct messages), **Files** and **Activity**. The list of the place you pick shows beside it, one list at a time. Your photo at the bottom of the bar has your status, Settings and Sign out; for owners and admins, the team's logo at the top has Invite people and Admin.
- On a phone the tabs are now **Trina · Chats · Files · Me**. The ☰ menu is gone: your Trina chats are the Trina tab, with **Ask Trina** at the bottom right. Projects are in the Trina list; the team folder is under Files; Activity is in Me.
- The Trina list shows your pinned chats, your folders, the projects you used lately (more behind **All projects**) and your other chats, then Scheduled, Team skills and Deleted history. There is no five-chat limit any more.
- **Folders** for your own lists: make one with **+**, then move a chat or a conversation in from its **···** menu or by dragging it. Only you see your folders; they change nothing about who can see a chat. **Trina folders** hold Trina chats and **Chat folders** hold conversations; each list says which kind it shows.
- When a Trina chat waits for your answer, **Waiting for you** sits on top of the Trina list, and **Trina** on the bar opens that chat. Scheduled, Team skills and Deleted history stay under the list on a computer.
- **Switch team…** under the team's logo in the desktop app, and in **Me** in the phone app.
- **Activity** holds the app's own notices (scheduled task results, things that wait for you); mentions and thread replies are under Chats. The Chats list shows every conversation, by kind, in the same order on a phone, and the Chats number is the same on both: how many conversations have something unread.
- Your usage is now under **Settings › Usage**. "My folder" is now called **My files**.
- **Notifications**: in an older desktop app (such as the Mac App Store 0.4.0), a Trina window covered by other windows no longer counts as away at once, so your phone stays quiet while you work at the computer; it rings again 10 minutes after your last input in Trina (an older app cannot see input in other apps).
- Pinning or unpinning a Trina chat or a conversation now shows at once. Before, the row moved only after the server answered, and a pinned chat could disappear from Recent for a moment before it showed under Pinned. If the pin fails, it goes back and a short message says why.
- Getting started › 팀 파일: the ✕ on a file in the folder plan now reads "'<file name>' 그대로 두기" in Korean, with no particle glued to the file name.
- Chat on a phone: while an answer runs, the empty message box's hint ("Sends after this answer", or "Choose above first" when a card waits for a choice) fits on one line. It took up to 3 lines and made the box grow.
- Settings › AI accounts: adding your own API key now advises a key of your own (not "a key just for this team in the company's console"), and a provider's refusal of the previous key goes away when you change the key.
- Settings › AI accounts: connecting your own ChatGPT account now says "Sign in with your own ChatGPT account and approve." instead of asking for the account the team will use.
- The lists beside the rail (**Trina**, **Chats**, **Projects**, **Files**, **Activity**) are easier to read: rows and section headers are larger (15 px), headers stand out in full colour, rows sit indented under them, unread rows are bold while read ones are softer, and the panel is a little wider on large screens.
- On phones, the **Projects**, **Files** and **Me** tabs and the history drawer now look like the **Chats** tab: larger names and an icon in a round tile on every row.
- Phones: the 트리나 tab shows Trina's star in its orange, as in rooms and on Home (dimmed while another tab is open).
- The phone app's icon now shows how many things wait for you (the same number as the Mac Dock and the browser tab: AI chats that need you and conversations that want you), and clears when you have read them. It comes with the next phone app; on an iPhone that already allowed notifications, turn on Badges in Settings › Notifications if no number shows.
- Phones (Android): after opening 나, Back on another tab's first screen goes to 트리나 again instead of closing the app.
- Android phone app: the app no longer puts its own number on the icon. On Samsung phones that number stayed after everything was read or after signing out, and clearing it removed every notification from the tray. The launcher's own count of the notifications in the tray stays; the iPhone app is unchanged.
- **Admin › Plugins** says plugins can come from Trina AX or the community and how each one is checked before install, and has a **Community plugins** line with **Build a plugin** and **Share your plugin** links. There is no community list yet: community plugins still install only with Install from file, and only on test servers for now.
- New docs page [Build a plugin](https://ax.trina.app/docs/develop/plugins/) in the Developer guide: what the community can build today (skill packs), a small example that installs, how to try it, how the server checks a plugin, and how to share it. The old Plugins page links to it.
- **Projects:** a project made from the Trina list's **+** or **새 프로젝트** (or Search's "New project") now opens its page after **Save**, as the Projects page's own button does. Before, the save went back to the project list, which did not show the new project until a reload. The same link also opens the form when the project list is already on screen.
- **Admin › AI accounts is simpler.** Each provider and account shows its logo (Claude, OpenAI, or a plug for the API engine), and the page is as quiet as Team settings: a name, one state and the switch per provider; a name, plan and one state per account, with a short "Replace the key" line when something is wrong. Models, check times, tokens and last use open under the name; Check again and Move up / down are in the ⋯ menu; the order note is behind "How order works".
- Your phone and browser now get a notification when Trina finishes an answer you asked for in your own chat, when the answer fails, or when Trina waits for you (a question or a yes/no card), unless you are looking at that chat. Tapping it opens the chat. In a shared project chat only the person who asked is told. Turn it off in Settings › Notifications › **AI chat answers**.
- **Notifications**: while you're using Trina on your computer, your phone no longer rings for the same message; it gets a notification only when you're away. Settings › Notifications › Phone: turn it off to get them on both.
- A file's preview no longer shows the pencil (edit) in a folder you can only read. Tapping it only said "read-only folder"; now it is left out there, on a phone's ⋯ menu too, as **Save as PDF** already turns into a download there.
- **Home**: an owner whose setup steps are unfinished now sees the "someone joined with your lead link" card on the first Home visit of a new tab, instead of being taken to the steps first. The steps come back on the next visit after the card is closed.
- **System › Connection** on a server without a relay (office network only) shows the team address, as Settings › Apps does, and no longer shows a red "Off" badge.
- **Rooms**: the old "@트리나" in a room (off since 2026-11-09) is gone from the code. Ask Trina in the ✦ side chat beside a room and send the answer with "Send to room". AI answers already posted in rooms stay as they are; old answers that were never posted are no longer shown.
- Room AI: an answer that read only your own file no longer says, when you post it, that it used content from the web or a connected service.
- Going back to a room, group chat or 1:1 chat you just had open now shows it at once and only loads the messages sent since. Before, it showed a loading placeholder and loaded its messages again each time. If nothing new came, it opens where you left off; otherwise at the newest message. An AI chat you go back to also opens where you left off when nothing new came.
- **Code comment**: the plan.json `maxActiveMembers` comment in the cloud contract now gives the member caps the owner set on 2026-11-13 (Free 3, Starter 5, Team 15, Business 40) instead of the old 5 / 12 / 20. No behaviour change.
- Invites when the plan's member limit is reached: the invite page now says the team is full (and to tell whoever invited you) instead of showing a sign-up form that is refused afterwards. A person accepting a last seat that someone else just took sees the same words, no longer the admin's "disable an account or change the plan" message with the billing link.
- Team invite link: when no seat is left, the panel says so above the ready-made message, so an admin does not send a link nobody can join with.
- Setup's **팀 파일** step now sorts the files you upload: AI reads up to 20 of them and proposes folders in **전사 공유** (at most 8, one level, existing folders first). Change a file's folder, take a file out or rename a new folder, then press **이대로 정리**. Without AI, files are sorted by their names. The step says that AI reads the files.
- When several files belong to one piece of work, the step asks once whether to make them a project, which the whole team can see.
- Admins who join later get **팀 파일 정리하기** on their welcome card until someone has sorted the team's files.
- After setup's **팀 파일** step sorts your files, it asks whether the Excel, Word and Hangul forms it put in **양식** should become team skills ("견적서 양식, 주간보고 양식을 스킬로 만들어둘까요?"). Press **만들기** to see each skill's name, what teammates are asked and the form it uses, then **팀에 공유**. When a teammate asks for one (for example "견적서 만들어줘"), the AI fills a copy of the team's newest form and saves it as a new file. The form itself is never changed.
- Files whose names include a customer's or company's name are not offered as forms.
- The dev environment now comes with the Python libraries for Excel and Word files (openpyxl, python-docx).
- Team skills › Recent changes no longer calls a skill's extra files (such as `reference.md` beside its `SKILL.md`) "team rule". Only a `skills/*.md` file without a skill header is a team rule; the save card the AI shows uses the same rule.
- Team admins can now manage Slack in **Admin › Connected apps**, like the owner: see it, add it with **Add to Slack**, turn it off and on, reconnect and remove it. Setting up or changing your team's own Slack app (pasting its keys) is still for the team's owner only.
- Docs site: a new **Slack** guide (Use Trina AX › Work with the AI), with screenshots: how the team's owner adds Trina AX to Slack with **Add to Slack**, how each person links their Slack account once and asks in a channel or a DM, what to do when **Add to Slack** isn't there, and how to remove or add it again.
- **Slack** is no longer an Admin menu item of its own. The owner finds it in **Admin › Connected apps**, like Google Drive: under **Connected** once it is added (with On, Off, a problem or Reconnect on its row), else under **Discover**. Its page has the same Add to Slack, steps, status, Turn off and Remove as before. Old links to Admin › Slack, and the way back from Slack after **Add to Slack**, open it there.
- **Slack**: after the app was removed in Slack, its page also offers **Remove Slack**, so it can be taken out of the list instead of waiting there to be reconnected.
- Docs site: the Slack guide's screenshots are now of the English app in the light theme, showing Slack in Connected apps.
- **Slack: one sign-in links your account and lets the AI read Slack as you.** "Sign in with Slack" now also lets Trina read the threads, channels and messages you can see in Slack, so "summarize this thread" works when you ask from Slack. A question from Slack knows where it was asked and reads the thread so far. People linked before see **Allow reading Slack** in Settings › Connectors › Slack, and once in Slack next to "working…". Unlinking stops it.
- An answer that used Slack messages from another channel or a DM asks first before **Post to channel**.
- The AI no longer offers a "connect card" for Slack; it says how to link Slack instead.
- Admin › Connected apps › Slack shows **Update Slack permissions** when the Slack app does not have the permissions for reading yet. A team's own Slack app: add the listed permissions in Slack, reinstall the app, then press **I updated them**; until then Sign in with Slack links the account as before.
- The dev environment has a **Preview** button again (Linux servers with a preview domain). When the AI starts a dev server, its step in the chat shows "Dev server on port N · Preview": the button opens the app in a new tab, on a separate preview domain. Only the person who started the dev server can open it, and the link stops working when the dev server stops.
- On Ubuntu 24.04 the installer and `install-srt.sh` now give the real reason why the dev environment stays off: the system's systemd 255 cannot limit the team server to its own run scopes. AppArmor is not the reason.
- The dev environment now works on Ubuntu 24.04 servers. The installer turns it on there as on Debian 13. On a server installed before this version, run `sudo ./install-linux.sh update --from <kit>` once to turn it on.
- Each dev environment run is now a systemd unit `trina-dev@<run id>.service` of a template that the install writes as root. The limits and the sandbox stay the same. The first update with this change stops runs that are still going (jobs and dev servers end as lost); start them again after the update.
- **Store apps show no plan links.** In the Mac App Store and Microsoft Store desktop apps and the iPhone and Android app, Admin › System › Plan has no **Manage plan** link, and a full plan says only that the seats are taken and that the team owner manages the plan on the web. The web and the downloaded desktop apps are unchanged.
- Admin › Members: the team invite link says which groups people join ("Joins: Leads" or "Joins no groups"), and an admin can change those groups on the same link without breaking the address already sent. Only people who join after the change get the new groups. The group checks link to Groups to make a new one.
- **Rooms**: a thread where you once asked the old room "@트리나" and never opened its private answer no longer stays unread after you read it.
- **Allow all websites**: the address card no longer points phones to a "Browser" row they don't have. The card now says "turn it off in Settings", and the note after choosing it names the right place: Settings › Browser on a computer, Settings › Allowed for Trina on a phone.

## 0.5.2 — 2026-11-12

- **Admin › Connectors** now looks like the Connectors page. The list has **Connected** (the Google connectors that are on, the team's connections, templates and requests, one line each) and **Discover** (what is not on yet). A Google connector's page is the same simple page members see, with **Team settings** at the end, laid out like Admin › Team settings: who can use it, and for Google Drive the Drives shared with the team. **Set up** is the main button until it is set up; after that **Change setup** is in ⋯. A team connection opens its own page.
- On a server whose Google sign-in comes from the hosting service, each Google connector's admin page says so in one line and has nothing to set up. Team settings are still there.
- There is no "Members can connect their own …" switch any more. Once Google Drive, Gmail, Google Calendar, Google Docs or Google Sheets is set up, everyone in the team can connect their own account. **Members' existing connections that an admin had turned off start being used again** (Google Drive and each Google app). This includes teams made before the Drive switch existed, which had it off without anyone choosing it.
- The Google apps' tool names and what each one does are shown in your language.
- Chat: when the AI service is busy (a rate limit) and the AI tries again, the answer now shows "The AI service is busy. Still trying…" until it continues. It showed only the thinking dots, sometimes for minutes.
- Settings › Apps now shows the team address for a server you run yourself without the relay, such as an office server reached at `https://192.168.1.2:19300` or a Tailscale name with a port. It is the address the server is reached at, port included, so people can type it into the desktop or phone app. A plain `http://` address is shown with a note that the desktop and phone apps need a secure address (https). A server on the relay still shows only its relay address.
- In the phone app, Settings › Apps › "Send the link to a computer" opens the phone's share sheet when the app offers one (the Android app's own sheet in the next app release), instead of only copying the link.
- A new Atlassian connection uses Atlassian's current address (v2). Connections made at the older address keep working and still show as Atlassian. Canva's newer documented address is also recognised as Canva.
- Figma's page lists its Weave tools too.
- When an admin adds a team connection, the dialog says who it is for ("For: Everyone in the team") without opening Advanced, with **Change** to pick groups or people.
- More look-alike names for known services are caught, such as capital Greek letters or a Latin word with one Cyrillic letter. The chat's connect card then shows the address, not the service's name. A connection saved under a known name at another address before this check now gets a card titled by its address when you sign in to it again.
- When the AI offers to connect a service you know, such as Notion, the card in the chat says "Official Notion address" only when it is exactly Notion's official address. Any other address, such as your company's own GitLab or an address the AI made up, is shown under its own address with a warning, never under the service's name or as official. Look-alike names ("Nоtion", "노션") are treated the same way.
- Asking the AI to connect a service you already have no longer makes a second connection: if it works, the AI says so; if it needs you to sign in again or a new key, the card fixes the connection you have ("Sign in to Notion again").
- Clearer words when you connect your own account to a service your team set up, and for the official address of a known service, in all five languages.
- When the AI asks to connect a known service, such as Notion, at an address that is not the service's own, the line above the card now shows that address too, like the card does. The card says plainly that this is not Notion's official address and shows the official one (mcp.notion.com).
- **Safer connected apps:** once a chat has read something from outside (a web page, someone else's file, a service's answer), the AI asks before it changes or sends anything through any connected app, even one set to "Run without asking" or "Always allow". The card says why. Reading is unchanged.
- The approval card for every connected app now offers **Don't send**, **Send once** and **Always allow**. Always allow is your own choice: for a team's app it applies to you only, and "Ask every time" on the app's row turns it back.
- When your own connected app needs a new sign-in or key, the chat shows a **Sign in again** card instead of only text, and the AI carries on after you sign in.
- A connector's page works better without a mouse. A tool's description can now be reached with the keyboard and is read out by screen readers. The privacy and terms links say what they open ("Google privacy policy"). After you close a Google app's **Settings** from ⋯, the keyboard focus goes back to ⋯.
- A connector's page (Google Drive, Gmail, Google Calendar, Google Docs, Google Sheets) is simpler, like Claude's: the header with **Connected** (or **Connect**) and ⋯, the About text, one line on what the AI may do, every tool at once, and a small details block. The examples, "Try in chat" and the boxed sections are gone; a Google app's **Settings** is in ⋯.
- In the desktop app, **Connect** (or **Sign in**) on Google Drive, a Google app or another service such as Notion opens its sign-in page in your browser at once. Before, it only showed a link you had to click.
- Connectors: a service your team set up as a template shows once, in **Your connectors**, not again in Discover. Discover starts with Google Drive, Gmail, Google Calendar, Google Docs and Google Sheets, also when one is not set up yet. A Google page's name wraps on a phone instead of being cut.
- A team connection's page says it uses the team's shared account, not "your own account".
- Admin › Connectors, a listed service's page: the main button is **Connect for the team**. Its ⌄ has **As a template** and **Just for me**. Before it is connected, the page shows the address it signs in at.
- Admin › Connectors: **Connect for the team** is for the whole team unless you choose groups or people under Advanced. Before, only you were ticked.
- A connection whose last try failed says when it was last tried, not "Not used yet".
- Admin › Connectors › Google Drive describes what the team gets, not "only you use this connection".
- Connecting a team template with a sign-in now says you sign in on the service's own page and the app keeps the sign-in, not your password. The words about a stored key stay for services that use a key.
- A listed service's page (Notion, Linear, Atlassian, Figma, Canva, Stripe, Supabase) shows its privacy, terms and help pages before you connect it. Notion, Figma, Stripe and Supabase also show the tools they list on their own pages.
- Linux servers: the AI engine now runs in its own network space. It can reach only Trina's model connection, no longer the local services some Linux systems offer to programs on the same machine (for example storage or disk helpers). Nothing changes in how answers work.
- Linux servers: before every answer, Trina now checks that the AI engine's network protection is really in place, not only that it was turned on earlier. If another program removed it (for example a firewall or Docker reload), the answer does not start and the chat says "The server's network protection is off" instead of "The answer stopped partway". The protection is put back automatically within 30 seconds (it was 2 minutes).
- Admin › System › Health shows the firewall as a problem while the protection is off, and `sudo mycel-ctl status` checks the rules the server holds right now.
- Linux servers: Trina now checks the AI engine's network protection before every answer. It trusted a good check for 3 seconds, so an answer that started right after one could run while another program had just removed the protection.
- Admin › System › Health shows why the firewall is off in one plain line, without the firewall tool's raw error text.
- **Go back** in Admin › System › Updates is no longer offered for a version that cannot read the team's database after the update changed it (0.4 after the update to 0.5, for example). The row says so and that going back needs the backup made before the update; the steps are in the backup and restore guide ([Going back to the version before an update](docs/BACKUP-RESTORE.md#going-back-to-the-version-before-an-update-linux)). Before, Go back to 0.4 looked like it worked, but the server came straight back to 0.5.
- An update or a Go back says **"finished"** only once the server runs the new version. When it comes back on the version it started from, it says so ("Could not go back to 0.4.0; still on 0.5.0") and why, and Admin › System › Health says it in plain words too.
- After an update where the new version first missed its health check and then started fine, `mycel-ctl status` no longer says "HELD: release … was rolled back" while that version runs. The next `mycel-ctl status` or update check clears that hold and says so in one line.
- Starting an older Docker image tag (or an older Linux kit) on a server whose database a newer version has changed now says that the older version cannot read it and points to the restore steps (in Docker: a copy of the whole volume from before the update), instead of suggesting Go back.
- A self-installed server can sign in to Google (Drive, Gmail, Calendar, Docs and Sheets) through the hosting service, like a hosted team, once the hosting service has registered it: no Google client of its own and no Google review. The operator sets `MYCEL_GOOGLE_DRIVE_BROKER_URL`, removes the server's own Google client and runs `admin.js google-broker-key` for the key and address to register. Until the hosting service serves it, the admin page still lets an admin set a Google client of its own. A server with its own client is unchanged.
- Linux: `install-linux.sh update` (and `repair`) checks the free disk space before it changes anything. When there is not enough, it says how much it needs and how much is free, and the server keeps running. The new version is copied while the server still runs. If the update fails after the server stopped, the installer puts the old files back and starts the server again, so the team is never left with a stopped server. `sudo mycel-ctl update` also says how much space it needs before it downloads.
- Admin › System › Diagnostics no longer says "System packages: Not yet brought to this version's list" after a successful sync on Debian and Ubuntu.
- People's messages in rooms, DMs and thread replies now show Markdown like the AI's answers: **bold**, *italic*, `code`, lists, code blocks and [links](https://example.com). Line breaks stay, @mentions still stand out and web addresses are still links. HTML stays text, `javascript:` links stay text, and pictures from other sites are not loaded by themselves. Quotes of a message, a message being sent, previews and notifications stay plain text. A link whose words do not show where it goes (`[paypal.com](https://…)`, `[here](…)`, an e-mail address) shows the real site or address in brackets next to it. Lists and quotes nest at most 6 deep, and a message Markdown cannot draw (or would read only very slowly) shows as plain text instead.
- A message (or an AI answer) stuffed with footnote marks such as `[^1]:` no longer freezes a room for seconds: people's messages show footnote marks as typed, and so does an answer whose footnotes would read that slowly. Very long messages of very short lines, and other text Markdown would read slowly, show as plain text.
- Connectors: a connection made from a team's sign-in template that you never signed in to now says "로그인 필요" on its line in Your connectors, like your own connections and its page, not "다시 연결".
- In a group room, a typed **@Trina** no longer looks like a mention when the server does not let rooms call Trina (the default). It stays plain text, in the room and in threads, the same as the "@" list that does not offer Trina.
- After the update to members-only projects, the owner and admins who could open team projects before it get one card on Admin › Home: how many and which, **Add me to these** (a member of all of them at once; their members are told) and a dismiss. Members see nothing.
- A scheduled task paused because only a project's members can use it now says so, and how to start it again: once its owner adds themselves to the project, it runs again on its own.
- Hidden text in a file you uploaded to your own folder, or in a skill someone else wrote (a group or team skill run with "/name"), can no longer save a memory without asking. After the AI reads such a file or skill, "remember" shows its card with the text first, as it already did for files in shared folders. Files the same chat wrote and your own personal skills still count as your own words.
- The AI is told to say in one short line when a file or page held instructions it did not follow.
- Admin › System › Updates now says when the publisher has paused a release ("Updates are paused by Trina AX for now"), the same as Admin › Home, and offers no Install update button for it. Before, it showed "New version" with a button the server then refused.
- On the Scheduled page, a task paused because its owner is no longer a member of a project from before the update now says "Only for project members now" with how to start it again, instead of "Project gone". Once the owner adds themselves to the project, the task no longer shows "Project gone".
- Phones, Admin: the page's main action is the bar's + (Members & invites: invite; Groups: new group; Folder permissions: add folder; Usage: usage limits; Team settings › Usage limits: add limit; Company profile: create it, then Edit), and the other ways (Add directly, Team invite link, Download CSV) are under the bar's ⋯. No button block over the page any more. Computers keep their buttons.
- Phones, Admin: Members & invites and Activity log have one search field across the screen with the Filters icon in it (with the number of filters set); Folder permissions has By folder / By person on a row of their own and Filters in the bar; Usage has the range across the row with the Filters icon at its end.
- Phones, Admin › Members & invites: each row says the person's role in words; the owner changes it on the person's page (or ⋯ › Edit).
- Phones, Admin › System › Backups: each backup is a tidy row, its date and size on one line, its kind under it and Restore on the right. The date was squeezed into one word per line.
- Phones, Admin › AI apps: with AI app access off on the server, the page says Off and "Set this up on a computer" instead of the installer flag, server.json line and environment variable to copy.
- Phones: the back button names the page it returns to on every page under Admin, also beside a long title (an AI engine's page under "Team AI accounts", Team settings' "Members and AI" and "Company profile"). It showed ‹ alone.
- Phones, Admin › Plugins: a plugin's page shows the plugin only, without the plugin search and the upload button.
- Phones, Admin › System: a list of its parts (storage, version and updates, health, connection, connected folders, backups, export; an admin: storage, and the automatic updates in office mode), each with its state on the right and its own page under "‹ System"; ‹ and the phone's Back return to the list. Admin's attention rows, the backup step, the office update banner and Drive's public address link open that part. Computers keep the one page.
- Phones, Admin › System: connecting a folder by its path on the server, changing the connection mode and an update done by the server's own command say "Set this up on a computer" instead of offering those forms.
- Phones: every message box (Trina's chat, a room, a thread and the side chat) opens one attach sheet with **사진 보관함**, **사진 찍기**, **파일** and **팀 파일**. Each opens one picker; on an iPhone 파일 goes straight to Files.
- Rooms take team files: pick one with 팀 파일 and it is sent as a copy that the room's people can open. The box says how many more people will see it.
- Phones, keyboard up: a room's files stay in one sideways row, the "@" list floats over the newest messages, and the text field and Send stay in sight. Trina's chat hides the AI picker row and "나만 봐요" while you type.
- Phones: the AI picker and "다른 모델로 다시 시도" open as a sheet that closes when you tap outside it. On a computer their lists now also close on a click outside or Escape.
- Phones: a setting with choices is now one row, "Name   value ›". A tap opens a sheet from the bottom with ✓ on the current choice, as in the phone's own Settings. This covers Team settings (language, time zone, presentation length, speech to text, default engine and model), Admin › Usage (Range, Group by), Me › Usage (Period), your own default AI in Profile, and a muted room's level in Notifications. "Let members pick the engine" is three ✓ rows instead of a segmented box. Number fields (Keeping data, answers at the same time) open the number keyboard and have no ▲▼ arrows. Back closes the sheet. Computers look the same as before.
- Phones, keyboard up: a room's whole message box (files, text and buttons) stays within about a third of the screen, so more of the conversation stays in sight while you type.
- Phones: on Connectors the line at the top, the search box and "Add custom connector" now scroll away with the list instead of staying pinned, and "Show all", "Show less", "Set up" and Admin › Connectors' "Show all" are easier to tap.
- Phone app: Google Drive's page, and Gmail's, Google Calendar's, Google Docs' and Google Sheets', no longer start a Google sign-in. They show whether it is connected, Disconnect, and one line to connect it on a computer.
- Phones: Home looks like an AI app. Trina's star and the greeting sit in the middle of the screen. The message box is one card above the tab bar, with the text on top and +, the AI picker, the mic and send in a row inside it. The separate "Default AI" line under the box is gone. The keyboard comes up only when you tap the box, so the tabs stay in sight until then. Computers are unchanged.
- Phones: Home has the message box at the bottom, like the ChatGPT and Claude apps; the greeting and the suggestions sit right above it, and the box stays in sight while the rest scrolls. The AI's chats are only in ☰ now, not listed a second time on Home. The suggestions (what's ready in your team, or what your group often asks) are one row of chips that scrolls sideways; each chip is one tap. Computers are unchanged.
- Phones: Projects has "+" in the top bar instead of an intro and a button in the page; a card's "···" is easy to tap. A project's page has one bar, "‹ Projects · name · ···", with the project's menu behind "···". Its Files row opens the project's files in the Trina tab, and Back returns to the project; the folder button in the bar opens the same folder on the Files screen. The new-project form has larger choices and no resize corner on phones.
- Phones, Admin › Members & invites: the team invite link is a tidy row, its name with Copy link only and ⋯ on the right and its groups, uses and end date under the name. The details were squeezed into one word per line.
- Phones, Admin › Team settings: Upload logo (with the logo now on it) and Back to the default icon in General, and Run now in File list, are rows across the screen like the Settings list, not small buttons beside the text.
- Phone app: switching to another team while a switch is still finishing no longer closes the question as if it had worked. It now says "Still switching teams. Try again in a moment." and keeps the link, so **Try again** goes on.
- Phone app: opening a team link on a team you left earlier, whose sign-out had not gone through yet, now still lands on the join page after the phone signs out of it. It could lose the link and show the sign-in page.
- Phone app: a team switch that could not save the new address no longer forgets an earlier sign-out the phone still owed to the old team.
- Phones: 멘션·스레드 on the 대화 tab shows your mentions and thread replies even when many newer notices came after them. Activity no longer says there is nothing while older notices are still to be loaded. It loads them by itself.
- Phones: a mention in a room you muted later is counted on 나, not shown as a dot on 대화's 멘션·스레드.
- Phone app: Back follows the app's screens. At a tab's root the iPhone's edge swipe does nothing, and Android's Back goes to the start tab, then leaves the app; with a sheet or dialog open, Back closes it first. Needs the new app release (nav-v1); older apps and older servers keep working as before.
- Phone app: a notification tapped while the app is open opens its conversation without reloading the app, and Back returns to the screen you were on. Pulling down at the top of a room no longer reloads the app.
- Phones: Projects is a list of rows (name, who sees it, ···, ›), not cards with "No instructions."; ··· or a long press opens a project's menu. A project's Files no longer shows the folder path.
- Phones: Profile has the photo in the middle (tap it to change it), the name, job title and email as rows, and Save in the top bar once something changed. Change password has one full-width button.
- **Phones: a 프로젝트 tab in place of 파일.** The tabs are now **트리나 · 대화 · 프로젝트 · 나**. 프로젝트 opens the projects list in one tap, and a project's chats in two. The team folder is the list's last row, **팀 폴더**, with its Trash inside as before, so a file is 프로젝트 › 팀 폴더 › …. With no projects yet, the tab says what a project is and has a **새 프로젝트** button above the 팀 폴더 row. 프로젝트 is no longer in the 트리나 tab's ☰ menu. Computers are unchanged.
- On a phone, a chat opened from a project goes back to that project. A chat's new-chat button and **Delete** show 트리나's home. A chat opened from a link or a notification opens under 트리나, and a team file opened from a notification opens under 프로젝트, with Back going up to the team folder and the projects list.
- The 트리나 tab shows Trina's own star, the same mark as the ✦ in a room's header.
- In the phone app, Back and the swipe now work on a page opened from a notification. Before, they could stop on it as if nothing were under it.
- Phones: on Home's "팀에 준비된 것", a skill's "써 보기" no longer touches the end of its chip.
- On a phone, Getting started is now a full-screen flow: no tab bar, and a bar of its own with ‹, the step ("2/4 스킬") and "나중에", which leaves the steps for now. Back (‹, the iPhone swipe, Android's back button) goes to the step before, and from the first step back to Home. The count covers only the steps a phone shows (4, not 5).
- On a phone the team invite opens the phone's share sheet ("메시지 보내기": KakaoTalk, Slack, a text message); copying the message is still one press under it, and where no share sheet opens it copies. Android needs the next app release for its share sheet; until then it copies.
- On a phone the skill checkboxes in Getting started and Team skills take taps from a larger area.
- On Linux servers, a team folder named `Projects` (capital P) is an ordinary folder again: only the lowercase `projects` folder is for project members only, so the owner and admins can open folders in `Projects` as before.
- Taking someone out of a group on their own page in Admin, or disabling their account, now also ends their own **Always allow** on the team's connected apps that group gave them. Given access again, they are asked again.
- Phone push (Android): a phone could miss a notification when Google's push service (FCM) stalled for more than about 40 seconds. The push gateway now stops waiting for FCM after 8 seconds, so the team server's retry sends the notification.
- A page on one team address could leave a pile of cookies on the shared parent domain, and from then on every team address answered a blank "400 Bad Request" in that browser until the person cleared cookies by hand. The relay now reads request heads up to 128 KB and passes on only the cookies a team server uses (its own `__Host-` sign-in cookie), so such cookies no longer reach or break the team. When there are even more of them, the address shows a page in the team's language that explains what happened, with a button that removes the extra cookies (the sign-in stays) and steps for the browser settings if the page comes back.
- A release tag now has to be on main: the release stops before it reads any secret when the tagged commit did not come through a reviewed main merge.
- The Microsoft Store package builds only from a release tag on main, and only after the full CI tests passed on that commit in the same run.
- Dev environment: the podman box runner is removed, and srt is the only runner. The Linux kit no longer has `root/mycel-boxd`. On a server where `scripts/dev-env/install-trial.sh` ran, the update stops and removes `mycel-boxd.service` and the box trial's drop-in, so the dev environment is off until `install-srt.sh` runs. The box state stays, and the update says where it is. `docs/INSTALL-LINUX.md` "Removed: the box runner" says how to remove it.
- The project endpoints of the box are gone: the per-project dev environment setting and the preview link. The stop of all runs of a project is now `POST /api/projects/:id/dev-env/stop`.
- Linux docs: step-by-step ways to go back to the version before an update (turn automatic updates off, put back the database copy from just before the update, then `sudo mycel-ctl update --version <old version>`; a plain restart started the new version again; how to cancel when that update does not finish) and to bring a team back on a new server from its archive (which file goes where, owner and modes, the database's `-wal`/`-shm` files, reconnecting AI accounts).
- `sudo mycel-ctl status` says "AN UPDATE DID NOT FINISH" and to run the update again when some root files differ from those of the version in `root/VERSION` (a reset or power cut while an update replaced them). It said only "root files 0.4.0".
- A bad release can now be paused before it reaches more servers and office Macs, without a new version: `bun scripts/release/hosting.ts hold stable --reason "…"` re-signs only the channel's small head files, and `hosting.ts resume stable` lets the staged roll-out go on where it stopped (docs/RELEASE-CI.md 6 "배포 멈추기"). Servers and offices not on it yet stay on their version, and Admin › Home says "Updates are paused" for it; a server already on it keeps running it. A copy of the head from before the pause, served again, does not lift it.
- In rooms where people ask **@Trina** directly, posting an answer that only you could see now always shows one card first, the same card as **Send to room** from the side chat. When the answer used files, connected services or another conversation that some people in the room can't see, the card says how many people would newly see it and where it came from. Nothing is posted until you say yes.
- Rooms, 1:1s and group chats: a quiet line above the message box now says when someone else is writing ("Alice is typing…", "Alice and Carol are typing…", or "Several people are typing…"). A thread's box shows only who writes in that thread. Nothing of it is saved, and only people who can read the conversation see it.
- **Slack with one button.** Where the hosting service serves your server, Admin › Slack shows **Add to Slack**: choose your workspace in Slack, allow it, and it is on. No app to make and no token to copy. A Slack workspace admin or owner has to allow it. People link their own Slack account as before ("Sign in with Slack" works through it too). Servers without it keep the four-step setup with your own Slack app, unchanged.
- Linux servers: this release's installer (`sudo ./install-linux.sh update --from .`) replaces `mycel-ctl` before the other root programs right away, and so does `sudo mycel-ctl update` from this release's `mycel-ctl` on. After a reset or power cut in the middle of such an update, `sudo mycel-ctl status` says "AN UPDATE DID NOT FINISH". Before, the bootstrap, the launcher and the sandbox helper could already be new while the old `mycel-ctl` had no such check. The update from 0.4.x through `mycel-ctl update` or the update timer still runs 0.4's `mycel-ctl` and its order; if that one stops halfway, `sudo mycel-ctl update` still finishes it, because the feed's release is newer.
- That message now says to run the same command again: `sudo ./install-linux.sh update --from .` in the kit's folder after an update from a kit, otherwise `sudo mycel-ctl update`. A plain `sudo mycel-ctl update` used to be named first and answered "already at" when the kit was newer than the feed; it now says that it cannot finish the update and why, instead of "nothing changed".
- Backup guide: going back to the version before an update no longer says that 0.4.0 shows the restored backup in Admin › System › Backups and the activity log (that comes with 0.5.0); cancelling the staged copy also removes `restore-staged.db`; on a new server, `mycel-ctl status` right after the start can show "connection refused" for a few seconds.
- An answer that an update cuts off now says "This answer stopped because Trina was updating. Send it again." instead of "The AI engine stopped unexpectedly". Its chat is free again at once (before, the turn slot was held for a while after `mycel-ctl update` restarted the engine launcher). Messages waiting in the queue, and new ones sent during the update, wait for it and then run in order. This works from the first update made from this version on: the update to it still runs the earlier launcher and server.
- `sudo mycel-ctl update` says how many running answers it waits for. The answers still running when the wait ends are stopped by the engine launcher itself just before it restarts, so the server knows they ended.
- Getting started's "도구 연결" shows the same cards as Connectors: Google Drive, then Notion, Linear, Atlassian, Canva and Figma. One 연결 connects your own account in one click, with nothing to type. Google Drive on a server without it says "아직 설정 안 됨" instead of disappearing. The address form stays as the last choice.
- Windows: uninstalling the app now also removes its trina-ax:// link handler, so links no longer point at the removed app (only when the handler still points into the install folder; updates keep it).
- Members can now ask an admin to add **any connector in the list** (Notion, Linear and the rest), not only the Google apps. "Request sent" has **Cancel request** to take it back.
- **Admin › Connectors** shows what members asked for, with **Add** (the usual "Connect for the team") and **Dismiss**. Members are told when they can use it.

## 0.5.1 — 2026-11-12

The tag v0.5.0 was not published: the release's Linux attack suite failed on a test fixture (its admin was not a member of the project her runs started in). 0.5.1 is the same code with that test fixed.

- The Inbox is now **Activity**, a page of its own (`/activity`) instead of a list inside Settings: tabs for All, DMs, Mentions and Threads, an **Unreads** filter, a search, and the notices grouped by day. Settings › Notifications keeps the notification settings.
- **Activity**: switching to Mentions no longer moves the page sideways, and a tab's focus ring shows whole.
- **Signing in with your own app**: when the service refuses the app's Client ID or Secret, the connection and the sign-in window now say so and where to check it, instead of "exchange". Other failed sign-ins also say what happened in plain words, and after a sign-in that worked the connection shows as connected without a reload.
- Admin › AI apps shows your own connections: "✓ Connected" on Claude and Claude Code when you connected them, the other apps' names (Codex, …) on the "Other MCP apps" row, and one line "You have 3 connections: …" with **Manage**, which opens Settings › AI apps. It showed only Connect, also for apps already connected. Settings › AI apps marks its rows the same way.
- Admin › Home lists only what an admin can act on: an untested AI engine version, a failed engine lockdown test and server internals such as socket paths are shown in Admin › System › Diagnostics only. Admin › Providers shows such an engine as Connected, without the version detail.
- Settings › Browser is in the desktop app only. On the web and phones it is there only while your own "Allow all websites" is on, so you can turn it off.
- **Settings › AI apps** and **Admin › AI apps** have a **Codex** row with its logo and its own steps: the `codex mcp add` command and, if no browser opens, the `codex mcp login` command, each with Copy. Codex was only under "Other MCP apps".
- **Claude Code** shows "✓ Connected" on its own row when it is connected. Claude Code names its connection "Claude Code (<server name>)", and that name was listed under "Other MCP apps".
- Admin › Team settings › AI: saving the AI defaults no longer throws away the people and groups ticked under who may pick the engine but not saved yet.
- Admin › Team settings › AI defaults: the default AI list shows only the AIs that are switched on, by the names the rest of the app uses (Claude, ChatGPT). A saved default that is off now is shown as off, with a line saying to pick another one.
- Asked to delete a folder that holds a project, group or protected folder you cannot open, the AI no longer says which kind it is: it says it holds a group, project or rule folder, so it does not tell you what is in a place you cannot see.
- When the AI cannot start, the chat says "Trina isn't ready yet." A member gets one button, [Tell an admin], which sends the owner and admins an Inbox notice ("<name> tried to use Trina but it isn't set up yet", opening Admin › AI accounts) and turns into "Told" (at most one notice an hour per person). The owner and admins get [Connect AI], which opens Admin › AI accounts. "Try again" stays.
- "Trina isn't ready yet": after a member presses [Tell an admin], the button still reads "Told" after a reload, until the hour in which the server sends no second notice has passed. It is read from the server (the same record that limits the notices), only for that member; the owner and admins still get [Connect AI].
- **Allow all websites now covers the AI's web reads.** Turn it on in Settings › Browser, or pick "Allow all websites (turn off in Settings)" on an address card. When you ask, the AI opens public websites without asking, in your chats and in rooms. It is always your own switch, never another member's. Scheduled tasks still ask. Addresses with long hard-to-read strings, long names or long queries still ask too. Both places say what it risks: a hidden instruction in a document, page or room message can make the AI send anything it can read to any site. In the desktop app the same switch covers the in-app browser, which asks once more in its own window.
- Admins decide with a new line in Team settings, "Also for web pages the AI opens", under "Members can turn on 'Allow all websites'". It is on by default. Turning it off turns everyone's switch off, and the activity log records it.
- An address card about a link that repeats what the AI sent now asks even on a site you allowed for the conversation.
- Phone app: when a team link can't reach the server (no internet for a moment), the question now offers **Try again** instead of only Cancel, and says to check the connection rather than the address or HTTPS certificate.
- Settings › Apps: the Mac row offers the **App Store** first, also when the team server has its own Mac installer. The App Store app opens without the macOS security warning and updates by itself. The Mac App Store build is unchanged and offers no installer.
- An artifact that tries to open another page now always shows "The artifact tried to open another page, so it was stopped." In Chrome and the desktop app it could turn into an empty grey box with no notice when its frame loaded slowly.
- A member who opens Google Drive, Gmail, Google Calendar, Google Docs or Google Sheets before the team has set it up can tap **Ask admin to add it** on its page. The owner and the admins get one notice in Activity ("Alice and 2 others asked to add Gmail") that opens the app's setup, and once it is added each member who asked gets a quiet notice that it is ready. A request is sent once; asking again says "Already asked". Members never see who the admins are.
- A protected publish (Safe PowerPoint) no longer fails with "changed" just because someone's connection renewed its sign-in token or a connection call failed meanwhile. Changing a connection's key, sign-in, address or rules still stops it.
- When two people replace the same file at the same moment, File history now keeps each version with its own content, and restoring the one in between brings it back. Before, that version could hold the other upload's content and restoring it did nothing.
- Linux server: the first-run setup code (`setup/code`) is deleted once the team has its owner, at the next repair or update, or by an uninstall that keeps the data. It stayed on the disk before.
- Chats: the version arrows (1 / 2) wait while an answer is being written, also when it is another member's answer in a shared chat. Before, switching versions then dropped the live answer off the screen and the next message continued from the other version; the server now answers "An answer is still being written. You can switch versions when it is done."
- A used-up usage limit now stops an answer while it is still working (before its next step), not only before it starts: the answer ends with the usual "You have used up … AI usage limit" message. This also covers a Claude Code sign-in and the team's API engine, which until now only counted their use when the answer ended. An answer that has already finished is never turned into an error: its use is counted and the next question is refused. A Codex sign-in reports its use only when its answer ends, and a dollar-only limit on a Claude Code sign-in gets its price only then, so for those the next attempt or the next question is refused.
- Several answers of one person at the same time now see each other's use, so together they stop near the limit instead of each going on past it. An answer that is cut off keeps the use it already had.
- An answer stopped at the usage limit also closes the questions it was waiting on: an answer you already gave that was waiting for a free place is dropped, like when you press Stop.
- When the connection drops, a list or section that could not load now says so calmly ("Can't connect right now. Reconnecting…" with **Try again**) instead of a red error, tries again by itself and shows its content as soon as the connection is back, without a click. This covers Home, the sidebar's chat history (it kept spinning), rooms, a room's messages, files, a chat, the side chat, most Admin screens and Settings › Devices and Memory.
- A first message on Home that cannot be sent because the connection is down stays in the box with a calm line and **Try again**; it is never sent by itself. The connection banner at the top is no longer amber or red and says what is happening in plain words.
- Confirmation cards are easier to read: a list of files to move to the trash, move or attach no longer shows a character count ("23 characters" counted the letters of the path), and a one-time scheduled task's card says its day in your language ("Nov 10, 2026") instead of "2026-11-10". Text that leaves (a message, an instruction) keeps its count.
- While the AI asks whether to remember something, its step under the answer says it is asking ("기억할지 묻는 중: …") instead of already saying it remembered.
- A question card that ran out without an answer no longer shows a green check on its step. It gets a quiet mark with "답하지 않음" next to the line. A "no" to "기억할까요?" no longer reads as unanswered.
- Contributors: CI now runs `bun run changelog:check` (in `check:docs`), and on a pull request it fails when the PR adds a line under "## Unreleased" in CHANGELOG.md instead of a file `changes/<topic>.md`; the message says where the note goes. A fix to a note already there passes. No product change.
- Pressing **Stop** while your message is still sending now stops the answer as soon as it starts. Before, the stop was lost and the answer ran anyway.
- If **Undo** after deleting a chat fails, a message now says so (the chat is still in Deleted history). Before, nothing happened.
- Opening or refreshing a chat while its answer is being written no longer makes the text jump in chunks or reload the chat again and again: text that arrives while the chat loads is kept and added once it is in.
- A question you just sent (or a regenerated answer, or another branch you picked) no longer disappears when a slow reload of the chat finishes after it.
- Sending a message no longer reloads the whole chat twice (which also took away older messages you had scrolled up to see).
- A step that already finished no longer shows as running again after the chat reloads.
- Admin › AI providers says **Connected** right after a Claude or ChatGPT sign-in finishes in the browser. Before, the engine kept saying "Sign-in needed" for up to 10 minutes, even after a reload. When an account is added, checked or removed, every open Admin › AI providers window now updates at once.
- Connecting a service from the chat card no longer means asking again: after you connect it (a key or a sign-in on the card), the same answer goes on and uses the new service right away. With Codex the service still comes with your next message.
- **Connected folders** are checked again at every sync, not only when they are connected. If the connected folder is later replaced by a link, or now leads to a different place, the sync stops and the owner sees "This folder was not synced" with what to do. A file or folder inside it that is a link is never copied, even one swapped in while a sync is running, so a sync can no longer copy the server's own files or a closed folder into connected/.
- A connection you signed in to (OAuth) no longer asks to sign in again when an admin switched one of its tools off, renamed it or changed its settings while its access token was being renewed. The renewed sign-in is now kept.
- AI apps (Claude, ChatGPT) connected to the team server keep working when someone floods the server's sign-in pages: their tool calls, token refreshes and upload links no longer share a limit with requests that carry no valid sign-in.
- Registered apps that never finished connecting are removed a week after their last use, and a full list makes room from them instead of refusing every new app. One address can register at most 12 apps a minute and 240 an hour, and start at most 50 sign-ins for one app and 200 in all in 10 minutes, so an office behind one address can connect a whole team. A sign-in link of a removed app shows a short page that says to start the connection again.
- **AI apps (Claude, ChatGPT)**: a few addresses sending many requests can no longer stop everyone else from connecting for minutes on end. When the server is busy, only the networks that flood are slowed down; a person connecting gets through.
- **AI apps**: the page where you allow an app (Claude, ChatGPT) now scrolls, and **Allow and continue** / **Deny** stay at the bottom of the window. On a laptop window of 1280×720 or a phone the buttons were out of reach.
- **AI apps (Claude, ChatGPT)**: one large IPv6 address block can no longer stop new connections by sending from many sub-networks. While the server is busy, such a block (an IPv6 /32) is slowed down as one network, and a person connecting from elsewhere gets through. Someone holding several sub-networks inside your provider's block can still slow your connection during such a flood.
- **AI apps**: the server does less work for requests from apps that are not connected yet during a flood (at most 6,000 a minute, was 30,000). Connected apps are not affected. A flood from very many networks at once (about 120 IPv4 /24s) can therefore stop new connections for the rest of that minute.
- **Connectors** (was "Connected apps") is simpler: one **Your connectors** list, one line each with one status (Connected, Reconnect, Needs attention, or Connect for something your team shared), and **Discover** with only what you have not connected yet. Errors, "Run without asking" and the other settings are on each connector's own page. It is in the sidebar only: the Connectors tab in Settings is gone, and old Settings links still open the page.
- An app your team has not set up yet still shows at the end of Discover for members, as "Not set up yet" ("Asked" once you asked an admin from its page). The AI's answers, scheduled-task notices and the Slack link message now send you to **Connectors** instead of "Settings › Connected apps".
