- Desktop app: a page at the team address that runs sandboxed (like the artifact frame opened on its own) no longer gets the app's desktop functions (notifications, the Dock badge, the browser pane).
- Desktop app: while Trina uses the in-app browser, the signal is calmer: a small pulsing dot, "Trina is using the browser" and a solid Stop button on the toolbar's own colour, with a thin accent line along the top of the page, in place of the orange bar and the orange frame around the page. Before a new tab's first page arrives, the browser shows the light or dark background with "Opening the page…" instead of a flash of solid orange or an empty white tab; an empty tab is called "New tab", not "about:blank". Ships with the next desktop release.
- Desktop app: drag the edge between the team page and the browser to make the browser wider or narrower, as with a thread or a file preview: double-click the edge for the usual width, or focus it and use the arrow keys. Both sides always keep enough room, the width is remembered after a restart and follows the window's size, and typing stays where it was after a drag.
- Desktop app: Trina's browser keeps working after you sign out, or when someone else signs in to the same team on the computer. At that moment the app clears the browser's data of the person before (their site logins, cookies and saved site data). On a very busy Mac that clearing could hang, and then Trina could not use the browser again and removing a team from the app's team list never finished. The app now clears only the kinds of data a site keeps for a login, which takes a second or two, and if clearing ever takes more than 10 seconds, Trina carries on. A removed team now leaves the list at once. Ships with the next desktop release. No API or data change.
- Codex on a Mac (owner's own Codex sign-in): a turn can no longer rename or replace its Codex settings folder to slip files past the lock. The lock also covers `.env`, `AGENTS.override.md`, `rules/` and `prompts/`, and the server deletes every locked file (config, model list, hooks, plugins, skills and these) before and after each turn.
- Desktop app: a second start (for example a `trina-ax://` link on Windows) hands over to the running app and exits at once, without setting anything up.
- Docker install: the image builds with the latest stable release (0.4.0) and other releases before 0.5.0-beta.1 again. They stopped the build with "this Dockerfile needs a kit …".
- Docker install: the guide, `env.example` and the test script name the latest stable release (0.4.0) instead of 0.3.0, 0.4.0-beta.10 or a 0.5 beta.
- Docker install: the guide lists the tested hosts; Colima (an Ubuntu 24.04 VM) needs the Ubuntu host setting and is not tested with it. When the engine sandbox or the firewall cannot start, the container now says so once and waits, instead of restarting again and again.
- The AI connection page is called by the name the app shows, Admin › Connections › Providers, in the install guides and in the messages that point to it.
- Docker install: tested on Ubuntu 24.04 hosts (amd64 and arm64). The guide now gives the one host setting Ubuntu 24.04 needs.
- Messages and guides now send people to Admin › Connections › AI accounts (관리자 › 연결 › AI 계정), the page's real name, instead of "Providers".
- Files: the folder panel is now **Who can open it**, laid out like Google Drive's sharing window. Admins see the people and groups who can open the folder, each with **Can view** or **Can edit**, and one **General access** setting (only people added here, everyone in the team, or the same as the folder above). They change it right there: add people or groups, change or remove them, and save. Before saving, one line names who will gain or lose access, and giving more people access asks first. People and groups that a folder above lets in show greyed, with a way to change them in that folder. The list of everyone who cannot open the folder, the reasons under each name and the counts line are gone. A team project's folder is shown, not changed, here: its members are changed in the project. Members see what they saw before.
- Admin › Folder permissions: the Save button and the summary of a change keep its outcomes apart: "can no longer open it", "can no longer edit", "can now open it" and "can now edit". Someone who goes from read & write to read was counted as losing access; they can still open the folder.
- Google Drive on a server without a public internet address is no longer a dead end. In Admin › Connected apps › Google Drive the owner reads what the address is for, who turns it on and where it shows (with a link to System); an admin is told to ask the owner. In Connected apps an owner or admin no longer reads "Your admin needs to turn this on" for Drive that is not set up: they get **Set it up**, which opens its page in Admin.
- Setting up Google Drive in Admin › Connected apps now lists what to do in Google Cloud, in order, each step with a link to its Google Cloud page: a project, the Drive API, the consent screen, a web client with this server's address, and the JSON file to upload. The fields name Google's own labels, so an admin can finish without other help.
- **Files:** a file link (the one the AI hands out, `/files/view?path=…`) now opens the file in the preview on a phone too. At phone width it showed only the file's folder.
- Admin › Team settings › File list: Run now on a screen from before the file list was turned off now says "The file list is turned off. Turn it on in Team settings to run it." instead of the generic conflict message, and the screen reads the status again so Run now goes away.
- **File list.** Every night (02:00–05:00 in the team's time zone) the server has the team's AI read new and changed files in shared, project, group and connected folders, and keeps one English line per file saying what it really is (type, names, date, a short phrase). The in-app AI sees these lines as a `_FILES.md` in each folder and searches them first, so a scan named `temp.pdf` or a photo named `IMG_2031.jpg` is found by what it is. Each person sees only the lines of files they can open; nothing is written into the team folder, and no file is ever moved, renamed or changed.
- The reading uses the team's own AI account (Admin › AI accounts; a person's own account is never used) on its smallest model, and counts against that account's limit: **after this update, a team's file contents go to its AI provider on the first night**, a capped amount per night. Personal folders are not read.
- On by default. An admin can see the status (files listed, last and next run, which AI account it uses) and turn it off in Admin › Team settings › File list; off deletes the stored lines. A server's host can turn it off in `server.json` (`fileList.enabled: false`).
- When two people save the same text file, each hears about the conflict copy once, with the folder named as on the Files screen (전사 공유 › Notes); the person who saved is told "your version was saved as a separate copy".
- An upload that ended says why: nothing arrived for 24 hours, or it passed the 7 days an upload may take.
- Search (⌘K) finds folders by name too, and opens them.
- **Files:** on a folder only an admin may change (a group's or a project's folder, 전사 공유, or a folder holding one) a member's ⋯ menu now shows 이름 바꾸기, 옮기기 and 휴지통으로 turned off, with one line saying why; the person's own folder says it cannot be renamed, moved or deleted. Selected, such folders are counted on the selection bar and left out of 옮기기 and 휴지통으로. Before, the actions were offered and failed with "only an admin can" after pressing.
- **Files:** opening a subfolder while **더 보기** is still loading now shows only that subfolder's files. On a slow network the parent folder's next 200 files were added to it.
- The Files search finds files and folders by name first ("로고" finds 로고.png, a quotation PDF by its file name, a Korean name saved on a Mac too), then the lines that contain the text. It searched only inside files.
- A line found in an HTML file shows its text, not its markup (`<!doctype html>…`); a line where the word is only in a tag, an attribute, a comment, a script or a style is left out (a page's title and text it hides are still found). While new words are being searched, the names found for the previous words are dimmed and cannot be clicked.
- Asked for a file that is already in the team folders (or given only its name), the AI searches file and folder names in other words and both languages, searches inside files and opens likely ones, pictures and PDFs too, before it answers. It no longer writes a new file in place of the one you asked for unless you ask for a new one. When nothing matches, it says so and lists the closest files with their paths, and only then asks where the file is. It does not search the web for team files.
- Admin › Folder permissions now uses the same words as **Who can open it** in Files: **Can view**, **Can edit** and **No access**, "Everyone can view" and "Everyone can edit", "Can edit" in the counts line, and "Cannot open" for someone who cannot open a folder. It said "Read", "Read & write" and "Cannot see". Only the words changed.
- Files › **Who can open it**: after **Cancel** › **Discard**, the next change can be saved again. Save did nothing until the panel was closed and opened again. The same happened when Cancel was pressed while Save was still checking.
- Files › **Who can open it**: when you give access to groups nobody is in yet, the question before saving says for each group whether its new people will be able to edit or only open the folder. It said "open or edit" for every group when only one of them could edit.
- After the AI read a conversation, the card that asks before it reads from a connected service now says it wants to read something and asks to allow it, and shows "What it reads". It said "changes are asked first" and "What it sends".
- The user guide's Mac install section starts with the Mac App Store; the installer file (.dmg) is the second way.
- Shared project chats say **members**, as the project's own screens do: "3 members can see this chat", **Share with project members**. They said "people in the project".
- **A low or full disk is said plainly.** Admin › System's storage card now agrees with the disk check below it (on a small disk it no longer says "plenty of room" while the check says space is almost gone). A backup that failed for lack of space says so in your language. When the server's disk is full, sign-in, sending and saving answer "the server's storage is full; an admin has to free up space" instead of "something went wrong on the server", and an answer that stopped for it says the same instead of "the answer stopped partway".
- **Gmail, Google Calendar, Google Docs and Google Sheets** are connectors now, next to Google Drive. Open **Connected apps** in the sidebar, pick one and press **Connect**: Google's sign-in opens at once, and after you confirm the account the AI can use it in chats. Reading is free; sending an email, changing an event, a Doc or a Sheet asks on a card first, and **Always allow** on that card (or **Settings** on the app's page) stops asking for that app. Each app is one sign-in of your own; disconnecting one leaves the others. In a team chat room @Trina can use the asker's own Google apps, and what it finds is shown only to the asker until they share it. Gmail attachments (PDF, HWP, Office files, pictures) are read like Drive files. Linux servers run the open-source Google Workspace MCP server (2.4.1, pinned by hash) on their own machine for this; it keeps nothing, and each call carries the person's own sign-in. Not yet in the Mac app.
- **Connected apps** has its own place in the sidebar: search, what you have connected as a row of icons, and Popular with a + on each. Each connector has its own page with example prompts that start a chat, what the AI can do, information and all its tools; once connected, **Try in chat**, **Settings** and ⋯ (Reconnect, Disconnect). Settings › Connected apps opens the same page. Adding a service by its address is for owners and admins; a member reads "Not listed? Ask an admin to add it."
- Admins set up each Google app in Admin › Connected apps: **Use the same Google app as Google Drive** finishes it in one step, with the list of Google APIs to switch on. Members may connect their own account right away (a switch per app, on by default). Google Drive's members switch is now on by default for new teams; existing teams keep theirs.
- Hosted teams sign in through the hosting service, once it offers these apps. Database migration 341 (add-only).
- The new chat screen no longer shows the **Frequently used skills** card or **My frequent questions**. In their place, under the box and without a frame: what people in your group often ask (or your team, if your groups have no list), three short questions with **Show more** for up to 10. A click puts the question in the box; it never sends. Each one is a general sentence the AI writes once a day from what 3 or more people of that group or team asked in a similar way, with no names, numbers or file names, and nobody can see who asked or how often. Only questions people type in their own AI chats count (never project chats, rooms, side chats, Slack or scheduled tasks), and only from this update on. A group or team shows a list once 5 or more of its people asked something. It uses the team's AI account once a day, never anyone's own. Admins can turn it off in Admin › Team settings › Members and AI ("Show what the team often asks", on by default).
- For newcomers, the team skills in **Ready in your team** now step aside after 5 chats even if no team skill was run.
- A team's internet address on the relay no longer tells signed-out visitors which version, database schema and uptime the server has; it only says that it is up. Signed-in people, the server itself and the office network still see them.
- Desktop app: picking your whole home folder (or another folder with system files) in a team page's folder window now says why it can't be used and offers **Pick another folder**. It was refused without a word, as if you had pressed Cancel.
- Getting started: the invite step no longer has "This person will be the owner"; one team link invites many people, so ownership is handed over after people join (Admin › Members, a person's "…").
- Home: no mic in the message box while voice input can't be used on this server (owners and admins included), and no AI picker when there is only one choice.
- Home: the team goal card invites you to make documents with colleagues, naming a few of the team's own skills; the 0/3 count is smaller.
- Chats started from a skill are called by the skill's name (e.g. "Promo cards"), not "/card-news".
- When Trina hosting copies your team's AI sign-in key for safekeeping, or makes a full export of your team's files and data, the owner now gets a notice in Activity and the team's activity log shows it. The daily offsite backup shows one line per backup in the activity log, without a notice.
- If your team address is moved to another server, owners and admins now get one notice in Activity saying so, with a pointer to hosting support. Before, the server only kept retrying quietly.
- **HWP preview:** when someone replaces a Hangul document while another person has its preview open, the next page turned starts again at page 1 of the new version and says "문서가 바뀌어서 첫 쪽부터 다시 보여 드려요." It showed a page of the new file right after pages of the old one.
- An invite link or team link opened in a phone's browser, where the app is not installed, now leads with **Copy invite & get the app**: one tap copies the link and opens the App Store (iPhone) or Google Play (Android). After installing, paste the link on the app's first screen. Safari on iPhone also shows Apple's app banner on these pages. The join form is still there under **Continue in the browser**. Computers see the page as before.
- An invite link opened while you are signed in (in a browser or in the phone app) now says who is signed in and offers to sign out and accept the invite, or to keep using your account. Before, it opened Home and the invite was lost without a word.
- Phones: the "Join your team" screen no longer leaves a stray dot after "I don't know the address" when the two help links wrap onto two lines.
- A team invite link opened on a weak connection no longer turns into "this link doesn't work". The page says it couldn't reach the team server and offers **Try again**, and a refresh of the same tab still has the link.
- Signing in with a temporary password from a link that needed sign-in (a Slack "link your account" link, a connector approval) now goes on to that page after the new password is set, instead of Home.
- An update no longer fails and rolls back on a server with a large database. While the new version copies and updates its database (minutes on several GB), it answers "updating its database" and the update waits for it, up to an hour. Retries no longer leave another full-size `pre-migrate-*.db` copy each time.
- Signed out, through the team address on the internet (the relay or the office internet address), `/licenses/manifest.json` and `/licenses/README.txt` now answer 404: they gave away the exact version and package versions, which `/api/health` already hides there. Signed in, on the server and in the office network they are there as before; the licence texts and the source-code link stay public.
- **First setup removes its code.** Once the owner account is made, the server deletes the setup code the installer left for the administrator (`<install folder>/setup/code`), not only its own copy. Running the installer again removes a code an older server left behind.
- **A link only this computer can open says so.** An invite, password reset or team link made while the app is open at this computer's own address (for example `http://127.0.0.1:47820` through an SSH tunnel) now shows that other computers can't open it, and to set the team address in Admin › System › Connection first.
- The install guides name the AI page as the app does: Admin › AI accounts (관리자 › AI 계정).
- The Linux installer works on a minimal Debian 13 again. It stopped with "/etc/sysctl.d/60-mycel.conf.tmp: No such file or directory" because such a system has no `procps`. The installer now installs `procps` (with `--skip-apt` it must be installed first), makes the folders it writes into, and `check` says when `sysctl` is missing.
- Welcome steps, team files (desktop app on a Mac): a folder macOS keeps Trina out of (Documents, Desktop or Downloads after Don't Allow, or Trina off in Files and Folders) no longer reads as an empty folder; the step says macOS blocked it, where to turn Trina on, and offers "Pick another folder" (QA TC-049).
- Settings › Apps sends Mac users to the Mac App Store only: when the edition has a Mac App Store listing, the Mac row no longer offers the team server's unsigned installer file (no "Download the installer" fold), also inside the desktop app installed from such a file. Windows, iPhone and Android are unchanged; a build without a Mac App Store listing still offers the server's installer.
- A team's address on the relay no longer tells signed-out visitors whether the team has finished first-run setup: `GET /api/setup` says setup is needed there only to a visitor with the setup token (the setup link sends it), and wrong tokens count toward the same per-address limit as on `POST /api/setup`. This server and the office network see the real answer as before.
- The user guide's Mac install section now describes the App Store app.
- Through the relay, `POST /api/setup` checks the setup token before it looks at whether the team exists: a made-up token gets the same counted 403 before and after setup (it used to get a 409 "already set up" afterwards). This server and the office network keep the 409.
- Settings › Apps: on an office Mac server with a Mac App Store listing, the owner's hint names only the Windows installer to add.
- The desktop app installed from a team server `.dmg` now says, when a new version is out, to get the Mac App Store app from Settings › Apps (it updates by itself), in builds with a Mac App Store listing.
- When someone joins the team (with an invite or the team link), the Everyone room shows "… joined the team", so the team can say hello. It is not a notification and adds no unread count. The owners and whoever sent the invite also see it in Activity, now for single-use invites too.
- **A welcome for people you invite.** Someone who joins with a team link, a single-use invite or **Add directly** now sees one card at the top of Home: "Welcome to {team}", who invited them and how many people are here, and four small steps: the conversations they are in, **👋 Hi** (one tap says hello in Everyone), a profile photo (optional) and **Ask Trina what you can do here** (one tap; Trina lists the team skills, folders and conversations they can use, in plain words). Without an AI the last step is how to get one (connect their own, or tell the owner). It follows them across devices and goes away when done or hidden.
- Someone an admin adds with **Add directly** is now announced like an invite or team link join, when they first sign in: "{name} joined the team" in Everyone, and the owners see it in Activity (not the owner who added them).
- The join page says who invited you ("Alex invited you to Acme"), for team links and single-use invites.
- A new member's first Activity item is no longer a connector to set up: those wait until they open Connected apps, where they are listed.
- Rooms say once what ✦ does: "✦ Ask Trina about this room. Only you see it." Close it or use ✦ and it never comes back.
- Opening a team link while already signed in says "You're already in {team}". Files no longer calls **Personal** read-only (your own folder is inside), and a greyed New folder / Upload says why.
- "Who sees it" leads with the folder's own people. A folder's chip in Files says "3명" (read aloud with "팀장·관리자 2명도 볼 수 있어요"; its panel is unchanged). Someone who is both a member and an admin counts once, as a member. Nobody's name is shown for the owner and admins. The API sends the count apart (`split`, `audienceSplit`: `direct` and `byRole`) to the owner and admins only. (For projects, see "A project's files and chats are now for its members only": their counts are members only.)
- Settings › Memory now says when the AI uses only your newest memories (those that fit what it is given) and marks the older ones it does not use now as **Not used**. Before, they were listed like the others and the AI could not recall them, with no word.
- Files: in the folder picker (Move, Save to), a folder you only pass through to reach your projects or groups now says "Only some folders", as on the Files screen. It said "No access", although it opens.
- Files: moving a file or folder out of your personal folder to a place where more people can see it (a project, a group or a shared folder) now asks first and says how many more people will see it. Moving several at once asks once. Before, it moved without a question.
- Connectors: a connection you added but never signed in to is listed under "로그인 필요", not under "연결됨" with "다시 연결 필요". Its own page already said "로그인 필요".
- Closing the sign-in window no longer leaves "새 창에서 로그인을 마쳐 주세요" on the row. When you come back before the sign-in finished, it says so, with 다시 시도 and 취소.
- New folders, renamed or moved files and new text files keep Korean names in composed form, like uploads did already, so a name typed on a Mac no longer shows as split letters on Windows. A download's file name is composed too, also for names saved the old way; renaming such a folder or file to its own name fixes it on disk.
- The office Mac's automatic update no longer rolls back when the new version has to update a large database first: it waits while the server says it is updating its database (up to an hour, as a Linux server does), and `teamax status` says so. Meanwhile desks on the office address (`https://<Mac IP>:8443`) get "connection refused"; docs/OFFICE.md says what to expect. The first update from a version without this waits only a few minutes for it (the old version's updater runs it): for a very large database run the new zip's installer by hand.
- `mycel-ctl update` waits for a large database update as long as the server's bootstrap does (its `--migrate-timeout` in the server unit), not a fixed hour.
- When a backup or a download of the whole team cannot copy the database, the error now says why (for example "file is not a database") instead of a missing-file message.
- Scrolling up in an AI chat, a team chat room or a thread until older messages load no longer jumps the view down by a page. The message you were reading stays where it was, on computers and phones alike.
- The setup's skills step now shows only the skills Trina prepared. It no longer asks for a Claude Code or Codex folder (a hidden folder most people do not have) or for a line to paste into Claude Code. Owners and admins still bring their own skills from **Team skills › Bring my skills**. When every prepared skill is already in the team, the step says so and goes on. The step is called **Skills** in the progress bar (it was **My skills**).
- The end of Getting started now lists the steps you put off with **Do it now** next to each: the step opens right there and you come back to the list after it. Before, the end screen said only that you were ready, and the steps put off could not be found again from there.
- A new team starts with one chat room for everyone. The team's own group no longer gets a second room with the same people. On a team that has that second room and nobody ever wrote in it, it is archived; one with messages stays.
- Message box: with only one AI engine switched on (for example only Claude), there is no AI picker under the box, however many models that engine has. The model for new chats is still chosen in Settings › "AI for new chats" (people who may pick) and Admin › Team settings › AI defaults.
- Only the team owner can now change how long the activity log, file history, trash and deleted chats are kept, and the update channel. Admins see these settings read-only with a note that only the owner can change them.
- **Activity tells you when a password reset link is made for your account**: on the server (the account or server command) or by an owner or admin. It says who made it, and while the link still works, that changing your password in Settings › Security & devices stops it. Before, only the activity log had a line.
- On a phone, a pinch on a PDF now keeps the part of the page under your fingers in place. It zoomed the right amount but moved the view down, by more than half a screen at 2x and further at 2.5x.
- Phones: Admin opens on a list, like a phone's Settings: what needs attention first, then each page as a row with its state ("2", "Claude Code", "1 to fix"). A row opens its page under "‹ Admin", and Back (the phone's too) returns to the list where it was, also for a page opened from a notice or a link. The sideways strip of tabs is gone.
- Phones: in Settings (the Me tab), the phone's own Back from a section now returns to the list instead of leaving Settings. The Connections sections show in the list without "More".
- Tablets: Admin's menu is a column beside the page instead of a sideways strip (a narrow rail with small names from 768 px, the full menu from 1024 px).
- Phones, Admin: a connector, a plugin, a folder and its rule editor, a person in Folder permissions › By person and an AI engine are pages with their own address. ‹ and the iPhone's swipe go one level up (the editor to the folder, the folder to the tree), and the bar names the page; the second "← back" link under the bar is gone.
- Phones, Admin: a row in Members & invites or Groups opens the person's or the group's page (role, groups, folders, actions with delete last). Row ⋯ menus and the ⌄ of page buttons (Invite ⌄, Add ⌄, Usage limits ⌄) are bottom sheets.
- Phones, Admin: Plugins are flat rows; an Activity log row opens its record; filters open in a sheet; Usage's breakdown is a list; AI apps are rows to tap; page intros are no longer cut beside their button; "Applies to everyone in …" is said once, on the Admin list, which names the team's AI accounts, connected apps and AI apps "Team …". Team settings › General: the logo in phone words and the update window as two time pickers.
- Phones, Admin: setting up Slack, adding a service by its address, an engine's server sign-in and Claude Code or Codex say "Set this up on a computer" instead of offering computer forms.
- Inside the phone app, a blocked microphone now says where to turn it on in the phone's Settings (iPhone: Settings › Trina AX; Android: Apps › Trina AX › Permissions) and offers an **Open Settings** button, instead of pointing to "the browser settings". An outside image in an answer says it opens in your browser, not "in a new tab". The browser and the desktop app keep their words.
- A folder's ⋯ menu in Files now says **Start a chat with this folder** (it said "this file").
- Phones: Back (the iPhone's swipe from the edge, Android's Back) now closes the open sheet, menu or dialog first and stays on the page: a message's sheet, a room's ⋯, 🔔, members and @ list, Files' Move and Version history, search, the side chat's chat list, the new-chat picker, a chat's title menu, Admin's invite dialog and Activity log's Filters. One layer closes per Back, and a page opened from one of them goes back to the page it was over.
- On a phone, Android's Back closes a room's ✦ side chat and the room stays, as Back already closes a thread and the image viewer. It went to the Chats list. Back from a picture opened in the side chat closes only the picture.
- On a phone (and in the Android app), Back now closes the open side menu and a full-screen file preview, artifact or browser panel, and stays on the page. Before, Back with the menu open left the app, and Back with a file preview open changed the folder behind the preview instead of closing it.
- After you open a page from the side menu, one Back now goes to the page before it. One press could do nothing.
- **Phones: Back goes to the screen above, and the tabs don't pile up history.** In Files, Back from a folder is always the folder above it (a breadcrumb goes back to that folder; the Team folder/Trash switch adds no step). Tapping a tab adds no Back step: each tab keeps its own place, tapping the tab you're on goes to its top, and Back at a tab's top no longer walks into another tab. A chat's project link goes back to the project when the chat was opened from it. 나 › 연결한 서비스 stays in 나, and every ‹ says where it goes ("‹ 설정", "‹ 트리나"). A page opened from a link or a notification has the pages above it under it. Computers are unchanged.
- Phones: the 대화 tab has search in its top bar (🔍). It opens search on 대화: rooms, people and messages; the other kinds stay one tap away in its type list.
- Phones: the 대화 list starts with 멘션·스레드, the mentions and thread replies from Activity, as a page of the 대화 tab (‹ and the phone's Back return to the list). A dot shows while one is unread. Activity in 나 stays as it was.
- Phones: each unread item is counted once across the tabs. Mentions and thread replies count on 대화 (their rooms already count them); 나 and its 활동 row count only the app's own notices. Computers keep the whole count.
- On a phone, only Markdown and `.txt` files offer **고치기** in a file's ⋯ sheet. Code and settings files such as `.json` or `.yml` offered it too. Computers keep the pencil for every file kind they could edit.
- A Hangul (HWP) file whose preview cannot work here no longer shows **다시 시도**, only the download: no HWP renderer or no safe isolation on this server, or a locked, distribution-only, damaged, unsupported, too large or lossy document. Trying again could only fail again. A timeout, a busy or cancelled preview, a changed file, a preview that is turned off and a lost connection still offer it. The same in Files and in a room's file viewer.
- **Phones: Files looks like an app screen.** One bar on top: ‹ with the name of the folder above, the folder's name, and ⊕ for 새 폴더 and 올리기 (a sheet from the bottom, only where the folder takes new files). The search box and the folder's "who sees it" note are the top of the list and scroll away with it; the Team folder / Trash switch and the breadcrumbs are gone on phones. Each row shows the name, then the date and the size. 휴지통 is a row at the end of the team folder (Back from it returns to the team folder); inside, items say the folder they were in by its name (부서 › leads, not `groups/leads`), the note is one line, and 되돌리기 says where the item went back. Two or more finished uploads fold into one line ("3개 올렸어요") so the list shows what is still going up. Computers and tablets look as before.
- **Files: each row's ⋯ is the app's menu.** On a phone it is a sheet from the bottom with large rows that never hides under the tab bar, with 선택 to start choosing several items (their 옮기기 / 공유 / 휴지통으로 sit at the bottom). Items that can't be used stay in the menu, turned off, with the reason under them. On a touch tablet ⋯ shows without hovering.
- Phones: Admin › Team settings opens on a short list (General, Keeping data, Members and AI, Voice input, AI engine, Usage limits, File list, Company profile) instead of one 6,700 px page. Each opens under "‹ Team settings", and Back returns to the list. A link to one of them (Usage's "Usage limits", a usage limit notice) opens that one. Unsaved changes stay when you go back to the list or leave Team settings, with Save at the bottom. Computers keep the one page.
- Phones: switches in Team settings sit on the right of their name, as in Notifications; Admin › System shows each value on the right of its label; buttons on these pages and in Settings › Profile, Security, Memory and the like are 44 px tall. The lists in Memory, Allowed for Trina and Browser have no frame around them on a phone.
- Phones: opening the keyboard at the bottom of a room, DM, thread, side chat or Trina chat keeps the newest message right above the message box (in the iPhone app it stayed where it was and the newest messages went behind the box); scrolled up, the message you are reading stays in place. A long press on a message while typing now takes the keyboard down and shows its sheet, with your draft kept. While you type, the message box sits right on the keyboard (the tab bar steps aside). Sheets with a text field, such as "Set your status", stay above the keyboard with their buttons in sight, and "Set your status" no longer opens the keyboard by itself on a phone.
- Phone app: moving the phone to another team now signs you out of the team you leave. Before, switching back opened that team still signed in, without a password. The question before the switch says so. Comes with the next phone app build.
- Phones: Admin's list pages are shorter. Groups have one line per group, Providers one row per engine, with the rest a tap away; Connectors' Discover is a list instead of tiles; Usage keeps the range in sight with one Filters button for the rest, and Group by beside the breakdown; Activity log keeps only the search and a Filters button at the top, with the kinds inside. Slack and Usage lose their frames, and small buttons and links are 44 px tall.
- Phones: in Settings, Providers is named in the bar, and Usage puts the period under its line and its numbers on one row.
- **Phones: menus open as one kind of bottom sheet.** A chat's or project's name ⌄, a Team skills row's ⋯, the new scheduled task "+", the drawer's filter and sort, and a chat row's ⋯ in search (or a long press on it) now open the same sheet from the bottom, with the name on top, rows of 44 px or more and Delete last. Scheduled tasks has "+" and, on a task, "Edit" in the top bar and a red Delete row at the end of the page. Computers keep their menus.
- **Phones: tabs fit the width.** Team skills, Folder permissions and Activity show every tab in one row of equal parts, 44 px tall, with no sideways scroll or scrollbar (shorter words where needed). Where the words still do not fit, the row becomes one picker that opens a sheet; search's seven types are such a picker. Activity's search moved into the top bar.
- Phones: in the phone app and the installed web app a pinch or a double tap no longer zooms the whole page (the bar and ✕ went off the screen). A picture still zooms inside its viewer, and a PDF inside its preview. A phone's browser tab keeps the browser's own zoom; in the app, Settings › Profile › Text size makes text larger.
- On a phone an empty folder now says "No files yet. Tap Upload to add some" instead of "Drop files here", and the page shown when the team server can't be reached uses the phone's language when nothing else was chosen on it yet.
- Phones: Admin › Members & invites is shorter. One "Invite to team" button with the other ways under its ⌄, the search and a Filters button on one row, and the role on the right of each member's name, with a second line only for the username or a status. The page's name is in the bar only, and only the search row stays at the top while you scroll.
- Phones: Admin › Connectors and Settings › AI apps no longer show their name twice.
- When the AI asks a question, its card no longer has a second text box: type your own answer in the message box, as the card now says. The card keeps its choices and its Answer and Skip buttons.
- In a room's Trina side chat on a phone, hold a message to copy, retry or edit it, as in the room. The icons under each message are gone there; "스레드에 보내기" / "대화방에 보내기" stays under the answer.
- Phones and the dark theme: a Mermaid diagram in an answer is drawn dark in the dark theme (it was a white box); an amount with its unit in an answer table ("1,234,567원", "12,345건") stays on one line; the page behind a file preview or the file viewer can no longer be tapped, scrolled or reached with the keyboard; counters such as "2 / 6" never wrap; the usage filters show one ⌄ instead of two; the empty conversation's box has a shorter line ("Message · ask @Trina") that fits on one line; and the room's ✦ side chat on a phone, already full screen, no longer offers "Open full screen".
- On a phone, an HTML file wider than the screen (a quotation drawn as a fixed A4 page) is now fitted to the width, with **실제 크기** / **화면에 맞추기** above it. It was cut off on the right. Computers (a narrow window with a mouse too) show the page at its own size as before.
- Pinching or double-tapping a PDF or an HTML file now zooms the document inside the viewer, not the whole app. On phones the PDF zoom buttons are gone (pinch instead); computers keep them.
- On a phone only Markdown and .txt files offer **고치기**, from the ⋯ menu of the preview. Editing a page's or a table's raw source (.html, .csv, .svg, .xml) stays on computers, which keep the pencil in a narrow window too.
- Phones: a chat's header shows only ‹, the name with the member count under it, ✦ and ⋯, so a long name has room. Notifications are in ⋯ › 알림 설정 (it says the setting now; a crossed-out bell after the name when they are off), threads with new replies are the first item in ⋯ (a dot on ⋯ says there are some), and 이름 바꾸기 is in the chat's info (tap the name) instead of twice.
- Phones: the picture and file viewer shows the file's whole name (two lines when long) and who sent it on top, and its buttons in a bar at the bottom: "2 / 3", download, ✦, 💬 and ✕. No arrows where a swipe moves to the next file, and no zoom buttons where a pinch zooms. Computers are unchanged.
- Phones: Settings sections are rows and switches like a phone's own Settings. **Memory** has its + in the bar, a tap on a memory opens it to correct or forget (forgetting asks first) and **Forget all** is a red row. **Security & devices** opens **Change password** and the other devices as pages (a device shows a phone icon when it is one). **Notifications** has a switch for this device and a separate red **Turn off on all devices** that asks first, and the messages to be notified about are checkmark rows. Language and theme are checkmark lists, **AI accounts** end with an **Add account** row, and signing out asks first.
- Phone app: what only a computer can do is no longer offered. Adding a service says to connect it on a computer (no form), **Use in other AI apps** shows the connected apps and one line to set them up on a computer, and **Apps** sends the Apps page to a computer instead of offering the Mac and Windows files.
- Phones: Notifications no longer has an **Open Activity** box (Activity's gear led back to it in a loop), **Browser** and **Dev environment sites** are parts of **Allowed for Trina**, the "Just for you" line is gone from each section, and paused scheduled tasks show as one row above Trina's pages.
- The owner's hint for a server without installers names `office-install.sh` only, not the office folder's command.
- Phone sign-in and join pages: the eye button is inside the password field, and the button of the sign-in, invite, team link, setup and forced password change forms stays above the keyboard, with the field being typed in still in sight. Inside the phone app these pages no longer show the website's footer (source code link, language select). The setup page asks for a **setup code** with a plain hint, and a wrong code says "setup code" too.
- A member who opens an /admin link sees "Only admins can see this" with a link Home, instead of landing on Home without a word.
- The personal invite page (/invite/…) shows why its details did not load and a **Try again**, like the team link.
- Phones: the controls the first round missed are 44 px to tap too: the side chat's starter chips, a Files row's ⋯ menu items and checkbox, the Files search results, the Files search box's ✕ (its tap area no longer reaches New folder), Settings' theme buttons and text-size slider, My usage's period and "Everyone's usage", the invite page's "Copy invite & get the app" and "Continue in the browser", Admin's "Learn more", Members' ⌄ beside Invite, and the file picker's ✕. A PDF zoomed with a pinch or a double tap no longer drifts sideways (16 px at 2x). Computers look the same.
- Phones: the message box's ＋ menu items, the team file picker's "Team folder" and folder rows, and the "Go to Home" link on the page-not-found screen take taps from 44 px. Computers unchanged.
- Phones: the shared buttons, icon buttons, menu buttons, copy buttons, tabs and text fields are at least 44 px to tap (Apple's minimum) on every screen, not only the settings forms: Team skills' tabs and ⋯, a room's bell, Files' path, search, New folder and Upload, the join page's "Sign in", "Open in the app" and language. Text fields show 16 px text at phone widths, so an iPhone no longer zooms in when one is tapped. Computers and tablets look the same.
- Admin › Google Drive lists its tools in words ("Google Drive 검색", "폴더 보기", "파일 읽기"), not as code names.
- The connector dialog's closed 고급 line no longer says "MCP 서버" or "(OAuth)": it reads "로그인 · 매번 묻기".
- Google apps the team's server cannot offer yet say "이 팀 서버에서는 아직 못 써요" instead of blaming your computer.
- The activity log names the outside app (Claude, ChatGPT…) that was connected or disconnected, instead of an id.
- Plain words in chat errors: the red box under a failed answer says what happened and who to tell ("지금은 AI가 답할 수 없어요. 팀장에게 알려 주세요."), never "AI 엔진", "모델 프록시", "API 주소" or "(HTTP 502)". The owner and admins read where to fix it instead, in the admin menu's own words ("관리자 › AI 계정에서 연결해 주세요", the owner also "관리자 › 시스템 › 상태 점검"); the old "관리 › AI 연결" and "관리자 > 시스템 > 진단" pointed at menus that no longer exist. Refusals about picking an AI ("어떤 AI로 답할지는 관리자가 정해요") and safe operations ("이 팀의 보안 설정 때문에…") say it plainly too. Errors saved before still follow the language on screen.
- The AI's confirmation card before it sends to a connected service names the action by the service's own title ("AI가 Tasks에 이 내용을 보내려고 해요: 할 일 만들기", not `create_task`) and shows every value as a labelled line (받는 사람, 제목, 마감일 …), where it goes first. The exact content is one click away under **보내는 내용 그대로 보기** (Show exactly what is sent), with its size; long random-looking parts are highlighted in both. When the lines could not show all of it, the card shows the content open, as before. Sending and not sending work as before.
- The card before the AI opens a new web address speaks plainly ("AI가 이 대화에 없던 주소를 열려고 해요. 주소에 내 정보가 들어 있지 않은지 확인해 주세요."). The card before saving where more people can see it names the place in words ("전사 공유 › 영업 › 보고서.pptx") and no longer shows a SHA-256 line (the file's hash stays in the activity log).
- Cards that name team folders say them as the Files screen does: moving or copying, moving to the trash, changes after reading rooms, saving a team skill, the places a chat read ("내 폴더 › 메모.txt", "Bob님의 폴더", "팀 스킬 › 회의록 › SKILL.md"). "내 폴더에 저장해 달라고 하기" no longer puts your user id into your message.
- The steps under an answer read plainly: no engine names or model ids ("AI가 답하지 않아 다른 AI로 다시 답해요"), a connected service's step by its tool's title without the AI's arguments ("Notion · Create page", as the service names it), a tool without words of its own as words ("Todo write", not `TodoWrite`), no ids of schedules or Drive files, "오래 걸리는 작업" for a dev-environment job instead of "(job)", and plainer verbs (화면 만들기, 자료 지도에서 찾기, 페이지 오류 보기). Steps saved before are shown in the new words.
- Plain words on the screens members see. Settings and Admin call the three connection pages **AI accounts**, **Connected apps** and **Use from other AI apps** (was Providers, Connectors, AI apps), and Codex is called ChatGPT in the app. "MCP", "engine" and "connector" are gone from Settings' tabs, Connected apps, Search, onboarding and the notices; the setup steps for Claude Code and other apps keep their own terms. Pointers to the old "Settings › MCP" now name the real page.
- Members add services from the list in **Connected apps**; a service that is not listed reads "Ask an admin to add it." Owners and admins still add any service by its address.
- The model id under each answer is gone. People who choose the AI see which one answered ("Claude Sonnet") on a small info button.
- Files says "Only some folders" for a folder you can only go through and "separate copy" for a conflict copy; Settings › Usage shows answers and an estimate, without tokens.
- Android: invite and team links now open the app for people who installed it from Google Play too. The team server's app-link file lists Play's app signing key next to the upload key.
- A plugin is trusted only while this server's settings still trust it: a package taken in while unsigned or test-signed packages were allowed, or signed with a key that was later removed, is turned off at start, its skills leave `skills/`, and Admin › Plugins says why. Turning it on or rolling back to it is refused.
- Placing a skill pack's skills never follows a link in the team folder: a link at `skills/`, `skills/<plugin>` or below blocks install, turn on, update and roll back instead of writing outside the team folder.
- **Admin › Plugins** is laid out like Connected apps: a search box in the title row, installed plugins and the official list as cards, and a page for each plugin with what it will do, where it runs and its licences. Installing still goes through the same permission prompt.
- Getting started: after "홈페이지가 없어요" and the three typed answers, the review is titled "입력한 내용을 확인해 주세요" instead of "이렇게 이해했어요", which suggested something had been read.
- **Projects**: the card of a team project you manage but are not a member of now says "Only members can see what's inside", with a small lock. It said "No instructions." even when the project had instructions.
- On a phone, a chat's **···** on a project page opens a full bottom sheet again (it showed as a narrow strip), and it has the same items as the chat's menu in the sidebar: pin, copy link, rename, move to another project and delete, each only for people who may do it.
- **A project's files and chats are now for its members only.** The owner and admins no longer open a team project just because of their position. They still see every project in Projects (its name, who made it, how many members it has), change its members, rename it, delete it and bring it back. To open one, an admin adds themselves with **Add me**; the project's members see that they joined, and it is in the activity log. Folders like Shared, department folders and team skills stay as they were.
- Projects made before 2026-11-09 outside the Projects folder are not changed: the owner and admins can still see them, and their page says so.
- Deleting a team project now moves it to the trash. Until the owner or an admin restores it from its page, its members can't open its files or shared chats. The owner or an admin can also delete it for good, with its files.
- Member counts of projects count members only ("Members · 3"), without the owner and admins.
- Admin › Folder permissions shows project folders as **Members only**; their members are changed on the project's page.
- When someone joins a project (also by being added to one of its departments), its members get a quiet notice in their inbox.
- Admins: the activity log and the skill changes no longer show the names of files inside projects you are not a member of. The owner's export leaves those projects out. The server's own backups still hold everything, as before.
- A scheduled task whose instruction has a line that starts with `>` no longer stops every save of its run as if the run had read a team chat room.
- A disabled account can no longer be added to a department. Enabling an account again is treated like joining: the members of the projects it can now open are told.
- Projects list: the cards in a row are one height. A project without instructions says "No instructions." in its card, which no longer comes out shorter than its neighbours. Under the name a card says who sees the project, in the words of its page's 멤버 line ("멤버 3명", "나만 봐요"), instead of its folder path. The line under the page title reads "Keep the files, instructions and chats for one piece of work in one place."
- A **Daily file index** or **Weekly summary** task that reads a team project's folder you are no longer a member of (for example the owner or an admin after the update from 0.4.0, now that a project's files are for its members only) is paused at its next run, and your inbox tells you why, as for a task whose project you no longer see. It used to stay on and fail every day. Add yourself to the project, or change the task's folder, then turn it on again.
- The copy of the database that a restore replaces is now removed after the same period as backups (the newest copy always stays). Several restores slowly filled the disk until nightly backups stopped for lack of space.
- Another member can no longer register your phone for their notifications: a phone's notifications stay with the account signed in on it until that account signs out. Turning them on for another account first says to sign out there.
- Asked to delete a project's folder, the AI now says the project is deleted on the Projects screen. It said skill folders are deleted on the Files screen or the Skills page, also for a folder with no skills in it. A group's folder, your own folder and a folder with its own permissions each get their own reason too, and the AI says whether you can do it yourself; it still never puts any of them in the trash.
- A Trina answer sent to a thread or a room no longer shows doubled quote marks around a quote in it.
- In the steps under an answer, every save after a card's **Allow once** shows its place in words ("Company-wide › …"), like the first one. They showed the raw path (shared/…).
- After the AI moves files into a new folder, the file chip under the answer no longer counts the new folder as a file, and it marks the moved files **Moved**. It called them "Created".
- The dialog for sharing an existing chat with the project's people counts everyone who will see it, you included, like the line under the chat box and the copy-link message. It counted one person fewer.
- When the connection drops and you open a page that was not loaded yet (Files, for example), it now says it loads by itself once the connection is back, and it does: the page reloads by itself as soon as nothing you typed, are uploading or are waiting for would be lost (a reply typed in a conversation counts too). It waited for a click on **Reload**. The team goal card on Home also comes back by itself after a dropped connection; it stayed away until the page was reloaded.
- In a folder's **Who can open it** panel, the confirmation says people who join later (or people switched off) "will be able to open it" when the change only lets them view; "open or edit" is kept for a change that lets them edit. In Korean the panel's lines no longer show "이(가)" or "은(는)" after a name.
- After the AI has read a conversation, the "Please check" card for a call to a connected app says what it does in plain words ("Read from Tasks", "Change something in Tasks") and where it goes. It showed "Api request" and the raw call (`connection: team_…`, `method: GET`). The exact request is one click away under **Show exactly what is sent**. On these cards and on the card before the AI sends something to a team connection, members see the connection's address as the Connected apps list shows it, without settings an admin put at its end.
- An admin who picks an AI that is switched off for the team is told where to turn it on (Admin › AI accounts). Members still read that their admin turned it off.
- A member who sees a broken team connection in Connected apps is told to tell an admin. It said to check the address or enter the key again, which only an admin can do.
- The "Allow all websites" line on the address card and in Settings uses the same plain words as the line beside it ("long hard-to-read strings", no "tokens"), in every language.
- A team template's connect window and an admin's request to connect a service no longer name the admin who set it up: they say "A service your team set up for you" and "An admin asked you…".
- A scheduled task's confirm card lists rooms as **Conversations it may read**. It said "may read and send", but a scheduled run never posts to a room.
- When an admin changes or deletes a team template, a member's open **Connectors** page drops the old connection at once. Its "Try again" used to say "not found or no access" until the page was reloaded.
- The Mac app's update notice in Korean now points to 설정 > 앱 (it said 팀 설정 > 앱), and the install guide names the AI page as the app does (Admin → AI accounts).
- A link inside a quoted message is no longer treated as one you typed. When you "Ask Trina about this" on someone else's room message, Trina now asks you before opening a link from that message, as it does for any link you did not write. A link you type or paste yourself, outside the quote, still opens without asking.
- A person's name can no longer hold a line break or invisible characters (the same rule as room names). Saving such a name says so in plain words. Names saved before still show; where they are quoted, they are put on one line.
- The relay now refuses a signed operator or team call that is sent a second time, even when its signature is written differently. Before, a copied team call (status, heartbeat, rename, key change, release) could be sent again within a minute, and an operator call could be sent again with its signature re-spelled.
- The relay refuses an operator key list that names the same public key twice (under two key ids), so a copied operator call cannot be sent again under the other id. A list like that now stops all operator calls until it is fixed, so check operator-keys.json before updating a relay.
- **Relay:** one visitor can no longer make a team unreachable by opening many connections and not reading the answers. One address (IPv6: one /64) can hold at most 256 of a team's streams at once (`RELAY_MAX_STREAMS_PER_IP`), and an answer the visitor stopped reading is cut after 30 seconds (`RELAY_CLIENT_STALL_MS`). Before, 1,100 such connections took every stream and nobody else could open the team's address.
- CI only: the dev → main release pull request no longer fails `changelog:check`. It is compared with dev, not main, so the notes dev gathered since the last release no longer count as lines the PR added.
- The knowledge graph is removed. Trina's AI finds your files the way it did before: it lists folders, matches file names, searches the words inside files and reads them. In tests it found the same files without the knowledge graph, and it no longer quotes text that was already deleted from a file or waits for files to be indexed after a restart. Admin › Team settings no longer has a Knowledge graph section, and the Files search box lists the lines that contain your words. The older search by meaning tool (`search`, on only where `MYCEL_SEMANTIC=on` was set) is removed too, and `MYCEL_SEMANTIC` does nothing now. On the first start after the update, the server deletes from its data folder the old index, the old `semantic.db` and the downloaded search model (about 120 MB). Admins: a `knowledgeGraph` entry in `server.json` is now ignored (the log says so once), so you can delete it.
- A password reset link now stops working as soon as the password changes another way: the person changes it themselves, or an admin gives them a temporary password. Disabling an account or changing its role also ends its open reset links, and enabling it again does not bring them back. Before, a link pasted in the wrong place kept working for its full day.
- **Restoring a backup in Admin › System › Backups keeps the folder permissions as they are now.** The team folder is not restored, so its permissions are not either: a folder made or closed after the backup (for example an HR folder only the owner can open) stays closed. Before, it took the permissions of the folder above it again and every member could open its files. The restore dialog says so.
- After restoring a backup, Admin › System › Backups and the activity log say which backup was put back (when it was made), when, by whom, and whether it worked. Before, nothing said so once the server had restarted.
- Admin › System: a wrong password when restoring a backup or installing an update keeps the dialog open, with the message under the password field. It closed the dialog, so you had to start over.
- With the AI turned on in team chat rooms, a question whose answer was cut by a server restart now shows "Trina couldn't answer" with the reason under it for the person who asked, also after reconnecting or reloading. Before, the question looked ignored. Closing the line keeps it closed in that browser.
- What you type in a conversation's message box is now kept on your device, like AI chat drafts. Closing the app or pressing **Reload** for a new version no longer loses it; the box shows it again when you come back.
- A message that hadn't gone out when you closed the app is still there when you open the conversation again, marked "Not sure this was sent. Trying again won't send it twice." with **Try again** and **Discard**. Drafts and unsent messages belong to the person who wrote them, are shared between that person's tabs, are removed when they sign out or leave the conversation, and are kept for 30 days at most.
- Opening a team chat room no longer marks messages as read that you were never shown: not while the first messages are still loading (for example a room opened from a notification), and not when more messages are unread than the room loads at once. The room is read up to the newest message you scrolled into view.
- A reply you send after jumping to an older message from search now lands at the newest messages with your reply in view, instead of out of sight with the "Jump to latest" button gone.
- A team chat message that reached the server just as the connection dropped no longer shows twice (as sent, and again as not sent with **Retry** and **Discard**). The not-sent copy goes as soon as the message comes back through the room, also after reconnecting.
- A team chat message that arrived while the room was still loading (for example a room opened from a notification) no longer disappears, and the room is no longer marked read up to a message you never saw.
- A message you send after jumping to an older message from search now stays in the room when it goes back to the newest messages.
- Edits, reactions and thread replies that come in while a room or thread is loading now show.
- When someone joins or leaves a room, people who have it open keep their place instead of being sent back to the newest messages.
- Rooms: after the connection comes back, every room's member list is read again, so people added or removed while you were away show in the @ list and the member dialog. Hiding the open conversation goes back to the list only once it worked; if it fails you stay in it. A reaction that fails undoes only your own tap, so other people's reactions that came in meanwhile stay.
- A scheduled task no longer says "Last run finished with problems" when the only things that went wrong were pages it could not open, searches that found nothing or files it could not read, and it still finished its answer. It says so when a save, a send or another change was refused or failed, or when the answer stopped before the end.
- A scheduled task that saves a file and then sends its summary to a connection no longer has the send refused because the summary names the file (for example `weekly-report_2.md`).
- A scheduled task that Trina made from a chat shows a conversation it names by its name (#영업팀, or the person for a 1:1 chat) on the Scheduled page and in each run's question, instead of a raw id ("room_id 01a1…"). Someone who is not in that conversation reads "a conversation you're not in", never its name. Runs still read it by its id.
- A scheduled task's **Last run** says it once: under the heading, how it went and when ("Succeeded · 2 minutes ago"), or "Running" while a run is on its way. **Run now** shows that run, and the block follows it to its result without reloading the page.
- ⌘K's chat search is faster when a word is in most messages (one letter such as "a" or "요", which ⌘K searches as soon as it is typed): it reads only the chats the list can show, each until its first message with the word. It read every message of the team and could hold the server up for a second or more.
- **⌘K finds conversations and people by name**, first on the All tab: part of a name, or a Korean name's initial consonants ("ㄱㅈ" finds "견적"). A conversation opens; a person opens your 1:1 chat, or the new-chat list with them ticked when you have none yet.
- **⌘K finds files sent in conversations** by name, after your team's files: the file, who sent it, where and when. Only files of messages you can see are found; choosing one opens the conversation at that message.
- ⌘K matches **every word in any order** in messages, chats and file names (team files and files sent in conversations; the chat's team-file picker too): "견적서 보내" now finds "견적서를 보내드렸어요", and "budget q4" finds "Q4 Budget.xlsx". It needed the words as one exact run.
- ⌘K: pressing Enter right after changing the text could open a chat found for the text before. Results now always belong to the text in the box.
- ⌘K: the highlighted row no longer jumps to another row when more results arrive above it.
- Settings: a text size that can't be saved (offline) goes back to the account's size at once, instead of staying until the next reload.
- Settings › Notifications: when a change can't be saved, the switches go back to what the server has; a change saved just before it no longer flips back.
- Team settings › AI: the two switches for members' own AI accounts wait for each save, so two quick changes can't end on the wrong value.
- In the sidebar, **Show more** under Conversations now lists the rest by kind — team rooms (the everyone room first, then by name), group chats, direct messages — below the pins and the latest five, which stay in place. A conversation you open from there stays where it is.
- The sidebar's section titles, **Conversations** and **Trina history**, are now as large as the rows below them and bold, so it is clear where one list ends and the next starts (on the phone drawer too). The labels inside the lists (Team rooms, Group chats, Direct messages, Today…) and Show more / Less stay small and grey.
- A member downloading a big file slowly from a team server that runs on a Mac (for example through the office public address) no longer freezes the server for everyone. Linux servers and downloads through the team address were not affected.
- The Mac app's update notice no longer repeats its title and reads "새 버전(45.0.0)이 나왔어요. 지금 버전: 44.7.2".
- Admin home's Hangul documents to-do names the plugin as Admin › Plugins lists it ("한글 문서 미리보기").
- Asked to open a website on the web or a phone, the AI now says the desktop app has a browser that can do it, and reads public pages meanwhile, instead of only saying it cannot.
- When many answers can start at the same moment (a raised "answers at the same time" setting), their AI engines now launch three at a time, the next three 0.2 s later. On a small server, 15 answers starting together no longer freeze the app for up to a second and a half. Queue order, places in line and the per-person limits are unchanged.
- Searching team files by name on a very large folder no longer pauses the app for everyone while it runs. A broad search now looks at up to 1,000 matching files (was 5,000), shows the newest of them and says there are more.
- Dev environment (Linux): a command now reads only its own folders, the person's team folders, the system programs and Trina's tools. Before, it could read other places on the server, such as the original folder behind a connected folder (sub-folders the person may not open included), other apps' files and service settings.
- Dev environment (srt): a command in a project chat no longer copies a file into the project folder from a folder that some people of the project may not see (your own folder, a group folder) with no card. The run now reads only the team folders that everyone who sees the project may read too; a chat with no project keeps your whole read access.
- Voice input no longer waits forever when the first download of the speech model stops halfway. After 2 minutes without data (or when the download takes far too long) it stops with a message to check the internet connection and try again, and other people's voice input can go ahead.
- An update whose download stops halfway now fails after 2 minutes with a message to check the internet connection. Until now it kept backup, restore, export and update answering "Another operation is already running" until the server restarted.
- A theme picked in Settings that cannot be saved (no connection) now says so, and the screen goes back to the theme you had. Before, the screen changed with no message while your account kept the old theme, so the next reload or another tab flipped it back.
- An answer cut off when the AI's launcher restarted no longer keeps its chat "running" and its place in the answer queue until the server restarts. Its place comes back once the AI's process is gone (or, at the latest, a few minutes after the answer's time limit), and the chat takes new questions again.
- An unexpected error while starting one answer now fails only that answer. Before, every later question was refused until the server restarted.
- An error while ending an answer no longer leaves the answer streaming and its queue place taken.
- An answer that waits for you (a question, a confirmation, saving to a room) no longer takes one of the team's answer places while it waits. Questions queued behind it start at once; when you answer, it goes on as soon as a place is free, before anything queued after it, and the chat says "Waiting for a free place" meanwhile.
- A chat whose earlier answer is still being ended now says "Waiting for the earlier answer to finish. Other chats work as usual." instead of refusing a new message without a word.
- A backup cut short by a restart or power loss no longer stays in the backups folder for ever: what it left is removed at the next backup and when the server starts. Finished backups are never touched, not even when the data folder was replaced or restored from an older copy.
- An update (the installer's and the one in Admin) now writes the new version to the disk before switching to it, so a power loss right after an update can no longer leave the server stopped on a damaged version.
- The server keeps answering while a backup checks its copy of a large database.
- Linux: `install-linux.sh update` and `repair` now download and install the headless browser, the Google Workspace library and the engines named with `--engine` while the team server keeps running, and stop it only to switch versions. Before, the server was down while they downloaded. In a test update from 0.4.0 the server was down for 22 seconds before the change and 3 seconds after it, on a fast network. On a slow one the wait was longer, because the browser download alone may take up to 8 minutes. The install guide now says how long an update can take.
- When an update could not start and the server stopped, the message now names the newest database copy made before the update and says plainly that what was recorded in the app since the update (messages, settings) is lost if it is put back; the files in the team folder stay. Admin › System › Health no longer says the server "went back" to the version it stopped at: it says what happened and that nothing else is needed now that it runs again.
- After an update that failed once and then started on the second try, Admin › System › Health says so next to the version.
- When an administrator points the server at another version after such a stop, `state.json` names that version as the current one.
- Admin › Plugins no longer offers "Roll back to …" when this server no longer trusts that earlier version (it was only refused).
- Turning a skill pack off no longer removes empty folders outside the team folder when `skills/` was replaced by a link.
- A failed update can no longer leave the server down for good. When the older version cannot open the database the new version already updated, the server starts the new version again instead of restarting the old one forever. If no version can start, it stops trying and says why in `mycel-ctl status` and the update history.
- Large uploads no longer exhaust the team server's memory: the server reads at most 3 upload chunks at once (others wait), frees memory after each chunk, and asks the app to retry a little later while memory is high. Before, three 200 MB uploads at once slowed every page to 8-12 seconds and could stop the server.
- A large upload that pauses (the laptop slept overnight) now continues where it stopped: an unfinished upload is kept for 24 hours after the last part arrived, instead of 24 hours after it started, and at most 7 days in all.
- A voice recording refused because too many transcriptions were waiting is now removed at once, not a day later.
- **Uploads:** a file going up when the connection drops now waits for it ("연결을 기다리는 중") and goes on by itself when it is back, from what the team server already has, for up to 10 minutes. It used to fail after about 15 seconds (a small file at once) and stay failed. A file that still fails has **다시 시도**, also in a folder upload on a phone.
- Your own upload sent again into the same folder within a day (a retry whose answer was lost, or photos picked again) is kept once: no more "IMG_0001 (2).jpg" copies of it. A file someone else uploaded, or an older one, still gets a copy of your own, as before.
- The hint under a usage limit's amount (USD) now says that answers through a login add up too. It said only API key connections did.
- Asking from Home while your usage limit is used up no longer leaves an empty, untitled chat behind. Your question stays in the box with the reason.
- When a usage limit can be used again, the time is in 24-hour form ("11월 12일 0시", "Nov 12 at 00:00"), in the message and in Settings › Usage. "오전 12:00" could read as noon.
- "A new version is ready" shows once after the server is updated, not once more every time the connection comes back (a phone showed four stacked).
- A file's version history says when its folder is in the trash ("Restore the folder from the trash first"), and restoring a version there is refused instead of making the folder again outside the trash. This was already so for folders with their own permissions; now it is for every folder.
- A voice message that waits for another one to be transcribed first now says **Waiting for another voice input to finish…** until its turn comes, instead of "Transcribing…".
- Long jobs (updates, backups, restores, exports, voice input) no longer keep showing an old step while they are on the next one. A step that came less than half a second after the previous one was dropped, so a stalled update download showed "Backing up the database" for two minutes.
- Using a password reset link in a browser where someone else was still signed in now starts clean for the new person: the previous person's chats, rooms and live updates no longer stay on screen until a reload.
- The AI picker's list is forgotten at sign-out, and a list that failed to load is asked for again instead of staying empty until the page is reloaded.
- The Windows app now installs an update only when it matches Trina's signed release. If an update can't be checked, it isn't installed and the app says so once ("This update couldn't be checked, so it wasn't installed."). Team servers keep the signed release info next to the Windows installer, so offices without internet still update.
- Web Push works with a push key whose private part is 31 bytes. A key made with Node's `ECDH.getPrivateKey()` loses a leading zero byte (1 key in 256); the server accepted it from `secrets/push.json`, then every send failed with "Vapid private key should be 32 bytes long". The key is padded back to 32 bytes when it is read. No API or data change.
- "트리나에게 묻기" has Trina's ✦ now, the mark of a room's header and of "Ask Trina about this". On the sidebar's first row it replaces the orange speech bubble with a plus that read like an error or a notice, drawn about as tall as the icons below it; in ⌘K and on the new-chat button of a phone's AI chat it replaces the grey speech bubble. Where the product is not named after its AI it is the plain sparkle, in the row's colour. No text, API or data change.
- Trina can add people to a project or take them out when you ask ("김소라, 영업 부서 넣고 이민재 빼줘"), instead of saying it has no tool. One card shows the project, every person and group in full (a group with its number of people, "같은 이름 2명" when two accounts share a name), how many people start and stop seeing it, what opens to them (files, shared chats with their history, project skills) and who is told; nothing changes until you press **멤버 바꾸기**. A name you never wrote yourself (one taken from a quoted room message, for example) is marked in red, "내 메시지에 없는 이름이에요", and a chat that read rooms says so first. Only the project's creator or an admin can do it, in their own chat; two people of one name, a look-alike name or an older project folder are never guessed: Trina asks, or sends you to the project's members panel.
- Tests only: the unit test "successive human waits share one absolute deadline" no longer fails now and then on a busy CI runner. Its turn had a 90 ms deadline and its first card a 20 ms wait, so an event loop held up for more than ~70 ms ended the turn before the second card opened; the turn now has 500 ms, and the test checks the same things. No app, API or data change.
- A server that updates many times a day no longer keeps a day of old versions. The old-version prune kept every version folder changed in the last 24 hours, so the dev test server (about 40 updates a day) kept about 40 folders, 22 GB. Now an older version folder goes once it is an hour old; a newer one (an update about to switch to it) still waits a day, and the current, previous and newest downloaded versions stay as before. The hour covers a roll-back in progress (`admin.js switch`, Admin › Roll back, `mycel-ctl update --version <older>`). An older kit that the installer only staged goes an hour later too. No API or data change.
- A file name the AI chose after reading a room also keeps counting as outside content when another chat only renames it a little (adds an extension, changes its case), and when its folder comes back from the trash under a new name. A listing too long to trace in one go counts as outside, so it no longer slows the server. No API or data change.
- 설정 › 앱에 모든 앱이 보여요. Settings › Apps now lists every app with one way to get it: the team address on top, then **Phone app** (iPhone on the App Store, Android from the download page until Google Play is live; on a computer one small QR code of the download page, on a phone only that phone's store, in the phone app "This app") and **Desktop app** (Mac on the App Store, Windows from the team server; the app you are in is marked "This app" with how it updates; a team owner whose server has no installer yet still sees where it comes from). The Mac App Store build shows no Android row (App Store rules) and still no installer file. "Install as an app" moves under the apps. The links are the edition's (`editions/trina/branding/apps.json`); a build without them shows only what its team server has. In all five languages. No API or data change.
- Desktop app: coming back to the window (Cmd-Tab, a dock click, a notification's click, a start or the relaunch after an update) puts the keyboard back where you were, so you can type at once: the team page's message box keeps its focus, its draft and its caret, and the in-app browser gets it back if you were typing there; a notification's click puts it in the team page. Before, typing did nothing until you clicked into the page. A page loading in the in-app browser (Trina browsing, a site redirecting) no longer takes the keyboard from the message box while you type; it goes to the browser only when you click it, use its address bar or open it yourself. The keyboard is never taken from a dialog, a menu or another part of the window you are using, and a lost connection no longer brings the app in front of the one you are in. Ships with the next desktop release. No API or data change.
- 누가 보는지 늘 한 줄로 말해요. Every project chat's box and every open chat now say who sees the chat, in one quiet line under the box, on phones too. "나만 봐요" is for a chat only you see: a chat in a project that does not share its chats, a personal project's chat, a chat outside any project, or any new chat while safe operations are on. "나와 관리자만 봐요" is for a shared chat that, besides you, only the team lead and admins see (shown to people who may change the project's members). Otherwise the line reads "프로젝트 사람 N명이 볼 수 있어요", as before. Before, only a shared chat said anything, so a private one left you guessing. In a shared chat, if you can change the project's members, the line opens the members dialog, and after a save it shows the new count.
