New routes `POST /api/onboarding/files/rank` and `POST /api/onboarding/files/landed`; migration 272 (add-only). In all five languages.
- Online status, like Slack: a dot on a person's picture says whether they are online (a filled green dot: the app is open on a computer, in a browser or on a phone, and in use), away (a hollow dot: open but no input for 10 minutes on a computer, the phone app in the background, or they set themselves away) or offline (no dot: the app is not open anywhere; a reload or a short reconnect does not count). Several devices count as the most online one. The dots are on the DM rows of the sidebar and of the conversations page (the phone's 대화 tab), in a DM's header (on the picture on a computer, before the name on a phone), on each person in a room's member list and on your own picture at the bottom of the sidebar; not on message avatars, team rooms, the everyone room or group chats, and @트리나 has none. Pointing at a picture says "접속 중" or "자리 비움"; a screen reader hears it after the name. The account menu has one new item, **자리 비움으로 표시** (then **접속 중으로 표시**): you stay away until you change it, on every device. Only the state is shared, only with the people of your team: no device, no time and no "last seen". It comes over the connection the app already keeps (`presence`, a new WebSocket control frame each way), and `PUT /api/me/away` sets yourself away. No database migration, no change to the phone or desktop apps. In all five languages.
- Mac desktop app: notifications now get turned on. macOS asks an app only once whether it may send notifications, and the app used to ask with its first notification, which only comes while its window is in the back, so the question went unseen (the Mac App Store app showed none); if the app quit before an answer, notifications stayed off and macOS never asked again. The app now asks as soon as a team page opens with the window in front. If notifications are already off, turn them on in System Settings › Notifications › Trina AX. On a Mac, Settings › Notifications › Desktop app notifications › Test notification now says "Test notification sent. If it does not show, turn notifications on in System Settings › Notifications › Trina AX." instead of reporting it as shown, because macOS accepts a notification it will not show without an error.
- Folder permissions show faces. In Admin › Folder permissions, each person in the edit panel, in its people and group search and in a folder's list of people now has their profile picture (their initial without one), and each group a grey square tile with the group mark, in place of the small person and group icons; the Team skills page's "who can edit" window does the same. Admin › Groups shows up to 3 members' pictures overlapping, then "+N" (2 on a phone), before the member count and names. Names stay as text; the pictures are decorative. No API or database change.
- Desktop app (Mac download and Windows): a folder picked in the team page's folder window now reaches the page. Before, picking a skills folder (내 스킬 찾기, or a member's 올리기) ended as if the window had been closed, and a pick of the home folder, Desktop, Documents or Downloads itself never answered. The page may read the folder you pick and what is in it, never write there; other sites, the in-app browser and frames inside the page (artifacts, previews) get nothing, and the home folder itself and folders such as ~/Library and ~/.ssh stay refused (picking home would open ~/.config, ~/.aws and other places that hold keys; a folder inside it, such as .claude, can be picked), also when Desktop, Documents or Downloads is moved to the home folder or above it or is a link to it, and when another drive or link leads to it. On Windows a network folder written as \\server\share cannot be picked (pick its drive letter), nor a network drive that gives no file numbers. The folders you picked are forgotten when you sign out or someone else signs in. The Mac App Store app keeps its own window. No API or data change.
- A project page's chat list shows who is in each shared chat: the profile pictures of the people who wrote there, overlapping, the most recent first (at most 3 and then "+2"; 2 on a phone), in place of the creator's name. A chat only one person wrote in shows that face and the name ("Mina · 1분 전"). On a computer, pointing at the faces or reaching the row with the keyboard lists the names; a screen reader reads "Mina, Sam 참여". Your private chats there are unchanged. `GET /api/chats?projectId=` gives each shared chat `shared.people` (up to 3) and `shared.peopleCount`. No database migration. In all five languages.
- Only skills with a known origin ship, and none is added by itself. Four skills remain, in Korean and English: 회사 소개서 (Company profile), 카드뉴스 (Promo cards) and 홍보 영상 (Promo video) and 자연스러운 글 (Natural writing, the English one adapted from the open-source "humanizer" skill, MIT). Deleted: 할 일 정리, 핵심 정리, the marketing pack (7 skills and its four shared folders), six everyday work skills and the Korean HR pack, with the "마케팅 스킬 7개 넣기" and "인사 서류 스킬 3개 넣기" lines, the welcome steps' marketing pack route (`POST /api/onboarding/welcome/pack`) and the pack ids `marketing` and `hr`. Without 할 일 정리 the invite link opens Home unless the owner shared a skill of their own in 내 스킬 가져오기, and the end screen shows 홈으로; an invite link an earlier version made to the intro deck opens that shared skill, else Home, after the update. The 7-day goal never counts the intro deck (the owner's own first run): not on the card, not toward the goal, not in the owners' first-run notices. The team link's starter (meeting notes with "내 할 일 정리해 줘") is gone from the API and the app. Existing teams keep every skill file they already have. An older version run again after this one adds none of the removed skills either. No database migration. In all five languages.
- Share a chat you already have with the project's people. A chat made before a project shared its chats, or moved into such a project, now gets **프로젝트 사람들과 공유**: a switch in the move dialog (from the chat's title or its row's "…" menu; off by default) and **프로젝트 사람들과 공유…** in the chat's title menu. Only the person who made the chat can share it. First the server checks what the chat used on every branch: when its answers include content from material some of the project's people cannot see (another group's folder, your own folder, a connected service, the browser, a conversation) or may have used your personal memories, it asks "이 채팅을 프로젝트 사람 N명과 공유할까요?", says that the whole conversation becomes visible and that sharing cannot be undone, and names only places you may look yourself, never people. When nothing is narrower, the move dialog's switch shares at once and the title menu still asks once. An answer that ran dev environment commands counts as not fully known, here and in 대화방에 보내기. Cancel keeps the chat (moved, if you just moved it) private. After sharing, the chat has the shared mark and the line under the message box, your next answer starts a fresh AI session without your memories, and the steps under earlier answers show only their action to the others. Not for personal projects, confidential chats or chats made by a scheduled task. No database migration. In all five languages.
- @트리나 in rooms now shows Trina's star, the orange four-point star of trina.app, instead of the small sparkles: beside its answers, on its "답하는 중" line, in the "@" menu, in the "트리나 답변" head of an answer shared into a room and on the Inbox row of its answer. It sits on the same neutral circle as a person without a photo, with the same orange in the light and the dark look. The other AI sparkles (the phone's AI tab, Files' "AI에게 묻기", skills) are actions and stay, and an edition whose AI is not named after the product keeps the sparkle. No API or data change.
- A project's page no longer has the 개발 환경 (dev environment) line. The dev environment itself is unchanged: on the srt runner (what `install-srt.sh` sets up), admins turn it on or off, choose who may use it, the network and the limits in Team settings › Dev environment, and a chat shows each of its runs in place ("Waiting for the dev environment… #2 in line" with Cancel, and the command's step). On the older box runner, a project's dev environment and its box are now turned on, off, stopped or restarted only through the API (`PUT /api/projects/:id/dev-env`, `POST /api/projects/:id/dev-env/box`). No API or database change.
- Projects list: each card has a "…" menu. **이름 바꾸기** and **삭제** are there for people who may edit the project (the same delete confirm as inside it, with the line that says its shared chats go too), and **링크 복사** copies the project's address for everyone who sees the card. A chat moves to a project from its row in the sidebar's 트리나 기록 too: **프로젝트로 옮기기…** (**프로젝트 바꾸기…** in a project) in the row's "…" opens the same dialog as the chat's title menu; a shared project chat has no move there. The row's "…" is now 24 px on a computer. No API or database change.
- Shared project chats show who asked. Every question now has the sender's profile picture and name above it, your own questions included, so a screenshot passed around still says who asked; before, only other people's questions had a name and there was no picture. A person without a photo gets their initial; a question whose author is unknown says "알 수 없는 사용자". It is one quiet line, on the right like the question. Chats of one person are unchanged. No API change, no database migration.
- Files in rooms open with a click. A file under a message opens in the preview panel beside the room (a full-screen sheet on phones), as files do elsewhere: PDFs, pictures, Markdown and text, Word, Excel and PowerPoint, and HTML pages, which run in the same closed frame as other previews and cannot reach the app. Download stays, as a small icon on the file and in the panel; a file the panel cannot show (a zip, a Hangul file, a file past the preview's size) still downloads with a click. In an answer someone sent with **첨부해서 보내기**, a link to a file you cannot open now opens the attached copy instead of showing a lock; someone who can open the file gets the file itself, and the lock stays only when there is neither. Answers sent before this version keep their locks. A file on safe operations' download-only list is only downloaded. Security: a file shown inline (a room file, a team file's inline address) that is an XML document a browser would show as a page (RSS, Atom, XSLT, MathML, XUL, WML) now comes as plain text, like HTML. No database migration. In all five languages.
- Who can edit team skills is now on the Team skills page instead of in two admin tables. The owner and admins see one line under the heading, "고칠 수 있는 사람: 팀장·관리자, 영업팀 3명, Bob 님 [바꾸기]". It names the same people Folder permissions names for `skills/`, so it includes people who can edit through a group, an everyone rule or a rule on the whole team folder. Folders inside it that have editors of their own (anyone given write there: a person, a group or everyone) are counted at the end ("폴더별로 맡긴 곳 2개"). If that part cannot load, the line still names who it knows, without "only"; if the line itself cannot load, it says so with a retry. **바꾸기** opens one window. It lists the people and groups who can edit (with the folder when their rule is on a subfolder) and lets you remove one. You can also find a person or group, read the warning, and let them edit all of `skills/`. To hand over one folder only, use Folder permissions. The "팀 스킬 관리" column is gone from Admin › Members and Admin › Groups. Permissions do not change: earlier grants stay, and members do not see the line.
- One list of system packages for every install path. A self-hosted Linux server installed with `install-linux.sh` now gets the same packages as a hosted server: besides what it installed before, CJK fonts, the dev environment's tools (python3, git, build tools, Debian's chromium …; nothing is turned on, `install-srt.sh` still does that) and a pinned headless browser for HTML to PDF (Chrome for Testing `chrome-headless-shell` 157.0.8103.38, SHA-256 checked) in `/opt/trina/chrome-headless-shell`, named by `MYCEL_HTML_PDF_BROWSER` in `/etc/default/mycel`. `--minimal` installs only what it installed before (kept for repair and update; `--no-minimal` turns it back). An office Mac downloads the same browser into `~/TeamAX/browser` (about 105 MB, once per browser version). The list is `scripts/system/packages.json`, shipped in the kit as `root/packages.json` (its Linux part only: a change of only the office Mac's download is no root update): a release that changes it is a root update, and after the root files and the app `mycel-ctl update` starts a sync in a unit of its own (`<id>-packages-sync.service`; the update returns at once, so a slow download never counts in its time or fails it) that installs what is missing and moves the browser to the new version once it starts (the one before stays until the next switch). The sync record names the system (`/etc/os-release` and the machine-id), so a server reimaged with its data disk kept syncs again; every server syncs once after this change, because older records have no system name. `sudo mycel-ctl status` and Admin › System › Diagnostics ("System packages") show where that stands. A self-hosted server installed before this release is not changed by apt unattended: it shows what is missing until `sudo ./install-linux.sh repair`. A hosted server (`"hosted": true` in its install.json) may run apt unattended. A bad browser pin can be rolled back: the list pins the good version again and names the bad one in `"revoked"`, and the sync switches back even though the bad one is newer. CI warns when the pin is behind Chrome for Testing Stable and fails when it is two or more major versions behind; the trina-ax maintainers bump it. If the fonts, tools or browser cannot be installed, the installer warns and goes on; on an office Mac the browser is downloaded before the server stops, and a failure never fails or rolls back an update. The Docker image keeps the packages it had (its installer runs with `--minimal`), now read from the kit's list; `--build-arg TRINA_AX_PACKAGE_GROUPS="fonts browser"` adds the CJK fonts and the browser for HTML to PDF. The image builds stop when the kit's list is not the checkout's. `scripts/linux/deps.txt` is gone (its minimum versions are in the list).
- @트리나 in a room reads shared project chats. "@트리나 견적 프로젝트 채팅에서 뭐 정했는지 요약해 줘" finds the shared project chats the person asking can see and reads one. When everyone in the room can see that project, the answer goes to the room; otherwise only the person who asked sees it (the same private answer as for a file only some can open), and the step names the chat only when everyone can see it. Chats in the trash, confidential chats and chats of a project in the trash are never read, and the chats' messages are information for the AI, never instructions.
- Sidebar: the section titles 대화 and 트리나 기록 now read as titles. They were small grey text, quieter than "더 보기 (2)" under the list, so the rooms and the AI's history ran together; now they are in the main text colour and semibold (still a step smaller than the rows, so they do not look like one more row), with more room before 트리나 기록 (about as much as above 대화; the owner's setup row, while it shows, gets the same room after it), and in the phone's menu 트리나 기록 no longer looks like the date heading under it ("오늘"). The fold, "+" and the filter work as before, and a long filter label beside 트리나 기록 is cut instead of breaking the title onto two lines. On computers and phones, in both looks.
- A thread beside its room can be resized on computers: drag its left edge (the same divider as the file preview's), or focus it and use Left/Right (24 px; Shift for 96 px), Home and End. The thread keeps at least 320 px and the room at least 420 px where the window allows; double-click on the divider returns to the default width (400 px, 440 px in windows from 1280 px, now narrower where the room would get less than 420 px). The width is remembered in each browser, apart from the preview's, as a share of the room and the thread together, so it fits other window sizes. A file preview opened beside a thread now leaves the room 420 px and the thread 320 px where the window allows (before, the thread could slide under the preview). Phones keep the full-screen thread without a divider. The thread header's ✕ now sits 16 px from the right edge, in line with the replies and the reply box. In all five languages.
- Shared project chats. A team project's creator or an admin can turn on **이 프로젝트의 채팅을 프로젝트 사람들과 공유** in the project's settings: new chats there are then seen and continued by everyone who can read the project's folder (owner and admins included), with a shared icon, the creator's name on the project page, the sender's picture and name above every question and "프로젝트 사람 N명이 이 채팅을 볼 수 있어요" under the message box. Each answer runs as the person who asked (their access, AI connection, budget and usage), one at a time ("○○님의 질문에 답하는 중이에요"). Before the AI reads anything some of them cannot open (your own folder, another group's folder), it asks you first: **읽고 공유하기** or **읽지 않기**. Chats you had before stay yours, turning it off keeps the chats already shared, personal projects never share, and shared chats take team files only and are read-only in safe operations mode. Migration 265 (add-only). In all five languages.
- Team rooms and the everyone room now show their whole history to someone who joins later (or joins again), as Slack channels do: the earlier messages, threads, reactions, files and quotes are there, and nothing old counts as unread or notifies. This includes people who joined such a room before this version: they now see what was written there before them. Group chats and 1:1s still start when someone joins. An earlier answer of 트리나, an answer someone shared from their own AI chat, or a message an AI app sent after reading, that used something the new member cannot open, shows as one quiet line ("볼 수 없는 자료를 참고한 답이라 가려졌어요") with nothing else of it; it comes back if their access later allows it. 트리나 keeps a new answer private while it used such an answer, and that preview cannot be posted. In all five languages.
- iPhone app 1.0.2: the app icon's mark is bigger, 70% of the icon instead of 54%, so it no longer sits small in a wide dark frame on the Home Screen.
- iPhone app 1.0.1: a conversation push says who and where, as in the browser: "민준 · #마케팅 › 스레드", a DM "민준", with "대화방에서 나를 언급한 메시지가 있어요." for a mention, instead of "Trina AX / 새 메시지가 있어요". Never the message's text, and the push service in the middle never sees a name: the phone asks its own team for the title, only for a push of this phone on its current team, within 5 seconds, and shows the generic line when it gets no answer (an older team server, no network). Notifications stack per conversation. Signing out (or an ended session) now clears the notifications on screen, as a team change already did. Android stays as it is. Needs the team server with this release and the new push service; iOS Settings › Notifications › Show Previews decides what the lock screen shows.
- The steps under an AI answer no longer show a red mark for the AI's own trial and error. When it guessed a file name that does not exist and then found the right file, or a call failed and the next one worked, that step now has a quiet grey mark whose tooltip says "안 됐지만 트리나가 다른 방법으로 이어 갔어요" (while the answer is still running: "…다른 방법을 찾고 있어요"), and the folded "작업 N단계" line has no red mark. The same holds when the answer later failed for another reason: the red box says what failed, not the step the AI got past. Red stays for what did not get done: a save, a send or a change that failed with no later call of the same kind working (also under an answer that finished), the step a failed answer ended on, and a dev environment command that did not run because it was full. A failed look-up (a read or a search) under an answer that did not fail stays quiet. Opening a step still shows its detail. In all five languages; no API or data change.
- 대화방에 보내기 asks less, and asks better. It no longer warns when the room can open everything it gets: for example when the AI read a quote in your own folder, copied it to a shared folder everyone in the room reads and linked the copy, the answer now goes at once. When something does apply, the dialog asks two things apart: a row for each file the answer links to (모두 열 수 있어요, N명은 못 열어요, 다른 사람은 못 열어요; a file you cannot open yourself is only counted, never named) with a **첨부** switch, on by default, that sends a copy of the file with the message ("첨부하면 N명이 더 보게 돼요"; up to 10 files of 50 MB each), and one line when the answer carries content from material some people in the room cannot see, naming only places you may look yourself. The button reads 첨부해서 보내기 or 보내기 and is the default; 취소, ✕ and Esc go back to the room list with your choice and note kept; "그 사이 못 여는 사람이 늘었어요" when something changed meanwhile. The toast says how many files went. In rooms, a linked file you cannot open now shows a lock and "권한이 없어 열 수 없어요" instead of plain text, and an attached file's download button reads its name and size to screen readers. Older apps keep working. No database migration. In all five languages.
- The team logo shows only inside the page now: at the top left of the computer's sidebar, next to the team name. The browser tab, bookmarks, `/favicon.ico`, the home-screen icon and the installed app (the Dock, Windows' taskbar, a home screen) always show the product's icon, with or without a logo; before, the logo replaced the tab icon, the home-screen icon and the installed app's icon. Admin › Team settings › 팀 로고 says so, and its preview shows the sidebar header in the light and the dark look instead of tab and home-screen sizes; the install pictures in Settings › 앱 show the product's icon. A browser that showed the logo in its tab may keep it until it loads the page again, and an app installed with the logo as its icon keeps it until the browser reads the app again (remove the app and install it again to see the product's icon at once). `GET /api/me` no longer has `team.appIcon` (added after the last release). In all five languages.
- Message boxes: the clip, the text (and its placeholder), the mic and the send button share one line again. In a room's and a thread's box the clip and the mic sat about 3 px below the text and send (2–4 px on phones and in a narrow window); in the AI chat's box on phones "+" sat 1.5 px low. With more lines the buttons stay at the bottom, on the last line, as in ChatGPT and Slack. The recording bar is as tall as the row it stands in for, its ✕ and ✓ on the same line, so the AI chat's box no longer grows (12 px, 14 px on phones) while you talk. On computers and phones, at every text size.
- Trina keeps the team's folders tidy when it saves files, in "트리나에게 묻기" and as @트리나 in a room. Asked to put something "in shared" (or in a group's folder), it looks at what is there first and files it in the subfolder that fits, or makes one named like the others (`shared/Sales/Quotes/<client>/` next to `Past_Proposals`), instead of dropping it at the top. It moves rather than copies unless you ask for a copy, says in one line where the file went, and says so when more people can see it there. If a new file still lands loose at the top of `shared/`, a group's folder or the team root, the server tells the AI right away, with the subfolders there, so it can move it; nothing is blocked. No new setting, no database migration.
- HTML files become PDFs that look like the page. Ask the AI ("견적서.html을 PDF로 만들어 줘") or press **PDF로 저장** in an HTML file's preview: the server prints the page with a headless browser, as a browser prints it, with its colours and backgrounds, merged table cells, the page's own `@page` size and margins (A4 when it names none), its page breaks and Korean fonts. Before, the AI rewrote the page as Markdown tables and lost its design, or told you to print it from Chrome. The preview saves the PDF next to the page (`name (2).pdf` when the name is taken) or downloads it in a folder you can only read; the AI saves it like its other files, and asks first when a picture in the page would let more people see it. Printing loads nothing from the web and runs no scripts in the page: web pictures show as grey boxes, as in the preview. Up to 200 pages. It needs a Chromium on the server (Debian's `chromium`, which the dev environment installs, or `MYCEL_HTML_PDF_BROWSER`) and, on a Linux server, at least 3 GB of memory: each print runs with its own memory and process limits, apart from other people's conversions. A server without either does not offer it.
- The team logo now shows in the computer's sidebar header, next to the team name. Without a logo it stays the product's mark, and the phone's menu (which shows the AI's name there) keeps it; a change by an admin shows without reloading. Also: an orange line along the sidebar's right edge no longer appears after you click an empty spot in the page and press a key; the page area itself draws no focus ring (buttons, links and fields inside keep theirs).
- The Inbox (알림함) shows your mentions (and "@전체") and new replies in threads you follow, one row per thread or room: who wrote it and where ("민준 외 2명 · #마케팅 › 스레드"), the newest message's first line with "@" for a mention, the unread count and the time. A click opens the thread or the room at your first unread message. Messages of 1:1 and group chats stay in 대화. Browser notifications now say who and where in their title, never the message. In all five languages.
- @트리나 in a room now sees threads, as in Slack. Asked in the room, it reads the latest replies of the threads under the recent messages (and knows how many there are and when the last came), and it opens a whole thread when it needs more. It still answers the room only from what everyone there can see: an answer that used a thread someone in the room cannot see (they joined after it started) stays yours until you post it, as with older messages. Asked in a thread, it reads that thread only.
- Team skills page: a team skill that a group or personal skill of the same name replaces no longer vanishes. It stays under 이름이 같아 가려진 스킬 with a quiet note ("Sales 그룹의 같은 이름 스킬이 대신 쓰여요" or "내 개인 스킬이 대신 쓰여요"); the counts above stay honest, and the "/" menu still shows only the one that runs, with "팀 스킬 대신" beside it. Rows show the name you type (`/estimate-docs`, with the title first when a skill has one) instead of words with spaces, and no longer start with the folder path, which is in the row's "…" menu (a subfolder stays as a short hint for admins). The share page's title and subline follow the chosen target ("leads 그룹에 공유할 스킬"). In all five languages.
- AI apps that upload to the team now know where things go, and no longer save skills as plain files. The connector tells the app, when it connects, that `shared/` is for everyone, `groups/<folder>/` for that group only and `users/<your id>/` is yours alone (it names your own folder and your groups' folders), that skills are added with `share_skills` or in the app, and that passwords, keys, certificates and resident registration numbers are never uploaded. The refusals for the real skill folders now point to `share_skills` too. Ordinary files are written as before. No database migration.
- AI apps that upload to the team keep its folders tidy and can save a `SKILL.md` as a plain file again. A `SKILL.md`, or a file under `.claude/skills`, `.codex/skills`, `.agents/skills` or `skills/<name>/<file>.md`, outside the real skill folders is saved (a backup or a draft for review) and the app is told: "Saved as an ordinary file: it is not a team skill. To share it as a skill, use share_skills." No AI loads such a file. Writes into the real skill folders (`skills/`, a group's `skills/`, your own `users/<id>/skills/`) are still refused. The app is also told to look at an area before saving ("shared" or a group means that area, not its top), to pick or make a subfolder named like the others, not to upload a file the drive already has, and to say where each file went. A new file saved loose at the top of `shared/`, a group's folder or the team folder is still saved, with one line naming up to 8 existing subfolders the person can write ("Saved at the top of shared/. … a subfolder usually fits better …"). No database migration.
- Web addresses in people's room messages are links. "https://…", "http://…" and "www.…" (opened as https) in a message, its thread, a quote of it and a message still being sent are now underlined links in the accent colour, beside the @mentions, which stay as they were. Another site opens in a new tab without telling it where you came from (the desktop app and the phone app open it in the browser); a link to this team's own app (a room, a thread, a chat) opens inside the app; a link to a team file opens its preview. Punctuation after an address is not part of it ("(see https://…)", "https://….", "https://…에서", "https://…。"). A long address wraps after its slashes instead of in the middle of a word. Only http and https ever become links. Copy and edit keep the text as written. The phone app opens https addresses only: an http link there copies the address and says so. No link previews. In all five languages.
- Security: a link to this team's own address followed by "//" and another site ("https://<team>//other.example/…") no longer opens that other site in the app's own tab, in AI answers as well as in messages; it is shown as text.
- Send an AI answer to a conversation. Under a finished answer in your own AI chat, next to copy, **대화방에 보내기** picks one of your rooms, DMs or group chats (as the **#** picker lists them), adds an optional one-line note and posts it as your message: your note, then the answer as a quote headed "트리나 답변" with its lists, tables and code. File links in it open for the people who may open those files; the others see the name only. It mentions nobody and does not call the room's AI. An answer longer than one message (10,000 characters) is cut with "…(이하 생략) 전체는 내 트리나 기록에서", and the picker says so first. When some people in the room could not open what the answer used (a folder, your own files, a connected service, another conversation), it asks first: "방 사람 중 N명은 이 답에 쓰인 파일을 열 수 없어요. 그래도 보낼까요?", naming the places but never the people. Such a message cannot be edited (delete it and send again). Not in confidential chats. Phones get a bottom sheet. One database migration (261, additive), which runs by itself. In all five languages.
- The app installed from the browser (on a Mac's Dock, Windows' taskbar or an iPhone's Home Screen) now shows the number of chats and rooms that want you on its icon, the same count as the tab title and the desktop app's Dock badge; it clears at none and when you sign out. When a push notification arrives while no window of the app is open, the icon gets a dot until you open it. Settings › 알림 says where to allow the badge if it does not show (macOS: 시스템 설정 › 알림 › Trina AX › "앱 아이콘에 배지 표시"; Windows: the taskbar's badge setting; iPhone: 설정 › 알림 › Trina AX › 배지). The desktop app and the phone app keep their own badges. In all five languages.
- Sidebar: a thin line at the top of the scrolling part (대화 and 트리나 기록) while it is scrolled, the same as the line above your name, so you can see the list runs under the rows above it. None at the very top; nothing moves. On computers and in the phone's menu.
- The mic sits on the right of every message box, just before send, as in ChatGPT (트리나에게 묻기, a room, a thread); the clip and "+" stay on the left and the engine picker under the box. Same size and taps, same Space to talk and Esc.
- The panel on the right of the chat (file preview, artifact, Browser pane) can be resized: drag the divider between it and the chat, or focus it and use Left/Right (24 px; Shift for 96 px), Home and End. The chat keeps at least 360 px and the panel at least 320 px; double-click on the divider returns to the default width. The width is remembered on each device (as a share of the window, so it fits other window sizes). Phones keep the full-screen sheet without a divider. In all five languages.
- AI apps can read and write your conversations. When you connect Claude, Claude Code, Codex or ChatGPT to the team (Settings › AI 앱), the sign-in page now has **대화 읽기**, ticked, and **대화 쓰기**, unticked. With reading, the app lists your rooms, searches their messages, reads a room or a thread and reads files sent there, exactly as you see them in the app (only rooms you are in, from when you joined, never someone else's private AI preview). With writing, it sends a message as you in one of your rooms or replies in a thread, mentioning people as the app does; it cannot send "@전체", and it cannot send what it read from folders or rooms that some people in that room cannot see. The message looks like one you typed. Nothing is written to the audit log. A connection made before this keeps what you allowed then; Settings › AI 앱 says "대화를 쓰려면 다시 연결하세요" under it. Codex CLI's sign-in works: current Codex versions use their plain sign-in address only when the server says it marks every sign-in answer, which it now says (before, the server refused the address they used instead). ChatGPT's connector sign-in address is accepted too (not tried end to end yet). No database migration. In all five languages.
- Chat rooms: **@전체** notifies everyone in a room, like Slack's @channel. Type **@** and pick **전체** right under 트리나 (it says how many people it reaches; **@all**, **@channel** and **@everyone** find it too). It stands out like a mention, and every member who can see the message gets it as a mention of them: a notice, a push and the sound even in rooms set to 멘션만 and under "1:1·그룹 대화와 나를 멘션한 메시지", but not in a room set to 끄기 or for someone who chose 받지 않음, and quiet hours still hold the push and the sound. In a room of 10 people or more the send asks first ("11명에게 알림이 가요. 보낼까요?"). In the everyone room only owners and admins can use it; a DM has no @전체. Typed or pasted without the list it stays plain text. One database migration (259_b_room_mention_all, one column), which runs by itself; older versions show such a message as a plain one. In all five languages.
- Hosted servers: the free speech-to-text hours can depend on the plan: 50 hours a team a month on Starter, 100 on Team and 200 on Business once the hosting service sets them, instead of 10 on every plan (the free plan keeps 10). The hosting service sets them in the plan file (`speechFreeHours`, a new optional field that `mycel-ctl apply-plan` accepts from this release on; an older kit refuses a plan file that has it, so the hosting service sends it only to servers on this release or later); Team settings › Voice input, the notice when the hours are used up and the AI's note on a video or audio read show the plan's number, in all five languages.
- Conversations get a **⋯** menu everywhere: in a room's header next to the bell (the top bar on phones), on each conversation in the sidebar (on hover, with the keyboard, or with a right-click) and on the conversations page, and on a phone a long press on a conversation opens it as a sheet. It holds **알림 설정** (the bell's levels), **링크 복사**, then **대화방 나가기** for a group chat (as before, with its confirm), **대화 숨기기** for a 1:1 chat, and **숨기기** for a team room or the everyone room (they follow your groups, so they cannot be left). A hidden conversation leaves the sidebar's rows and its "더 보기" count, the 대화 tab's count and the unread count in the tab title and the Dock, and makes no notification or sound; it comes back by itself when something arrives for you: in a 1:1 chat a new message from the other person, anywhere a mention of you, a reply in a thread you follow or the AI's answer to your question. It waits under a quiet **숨긴 대화 N** at the end of the conversations page with **다시 보이기**, and opening it (a link, a notification, the list) shows it again; going Back to it or reloading the page does not. Nobody else is told. One database migration (258_b_room_hidden, one column), which runs by itself; an earlier version can still read the database. New endpoint `PUT /api/rooms/:id/hidden`; `Room` gains `hidden` (optional). In all five languages.
- Every icon button now says what it does in a short tooltip, as the rooms' already did: the top bars, the sidebar (the history's filter, a chat's "…", 대화's +), the AI chat's message box (send, stop, voice, attachments) and answer actions (copy, retry, save, versions), the file browser and file preview toolbars, Settings and Admin (the "…" menus, copy, close and remove buttons), Skills, Scheduled, search, every dialog's close button and the Home rows. It shows after a moment with the mouse or at once with the keyboard, never on touch screens, and no longer pops up when a click or a dialog moves the focus. It replaces the browser's slow hover titles; a button whose words a narrow window hides shows the tooltip only then. A few icons also say what they mean: the lock ("읽기 전용: 볼 수만 있어요"), the pencil ("쓰기 가능: 보고 고칠 수 있어요") and a connector's official mark; a schedule's last-run dot and the usage "+?" mark use the same tooltip. In all five languages.
- Voice input in conversations, and hold Space to talk. Rooms and threads now have the mic too, under the same team switch (Admin › Team settings › Voice input): the words go into the box as text, never sent by themselves, never a voice message. On a computer, hold Space in any message box (트리나에게 묻기, a room, a thread) to talk and let go to turn it into text; Esc cancels, also while it is being turned into text. A short tap still types a space, and typing fast, Korean and Japanese input and Space with Shift or another key are unchanged. On a phone, press and hold the mic to talk, let go to finish, slide away to cancel; a tap still starts a recording that ends with ✓. A recording stops at 5 minutes and keeps what was said (the time left shows in the last 30 seconds). While it turns into text, the server's own progress shows, so a first recording on a server that still downloads its speech model says "음성 인식 모델 내려받는 중 (488 MB, 처음 한 번만) · 23%" instead of looking stuck. While every chat is confidential and voice input goes to an outside service, a room says why instead of recording, as a confidential chat does. Screen readers hear when recording starts, when the words are being turned into text and when they are in the box. No new setting, no database migration. In all five languages.
- Group skills: every group has its own skills in its folder, `groups/<group>/skills/`, seen and used only by the group's members (and their AI): in **Team skills** (one section per group, under its current name, none for a group without skills), in the `/` menu (with the group's name) and in what the AI knows ("[group: 영업팀]"). The owner and admins outside the group do not get them. One name, three skills: your personal skill wins, then your group's, then the team's; two of your groups' skills of one name are both listed, and `/name` takes the group whose name comes first. Any member of the group can add or change its skills; the AI asks on a card first, as for team skills, and every change is recorded with the group's name (also in Skills › 최근 변경). Members can suggest a skill to their group, and the group's members review it. Renaming a group keeps its folder and the section follows the new name; deleting a group takes its skills off its members' lists. Group skills count in usage and popular skills (not in the 7-day team goal). The connector's `skills_list` returns `group` too.
- Share proposals to a group: on a proposal's page (내 스킬 가져오기, or what Claude Code or Codex sent) the owner or admin picks **보낼 곳**: the whole team (the default) or one of their groups; Claude Code and Codex can name the group (`share_skills` `group`). A proposal to a group adds skills only (no team rule, no connections) and never changes a team skill: a skill of the same name as one of the group's is merged into the group's or kept beside it, and one named like a team skill becomes the group's own copy. The group's members hear of it, find it under Team skills › 검토 (they see only what goes to the group, nothing else of the proposer's) and can add it to the group. The setup steps still share with the team. Database migration 260 (additive). In all five languages.
- Push notifications in Microsoft Edge. Edge delivers Web Push through Microsoft's push service (WNS), whose addresses the team server refused until now, so turning push on in Edge always failed and the "새 메시지 알림을 받으려면" line was not shown there. The team server now takes exactly Edge's address shape (`https://<name>.notify.windows.com/w/?token=…`, nothing looser; Google's, Mozilla's and Apple's rules are unchanged), sends to it with the same checks as the others (public addresses only, pinned connections, no redirects), removes a subscription WNS says is gone (404, 410) and tries again later when WNS says too many for now (406). The line now shows in Edge too.
- Push notifications say why they could not be turned on. In a browser, Settings › Notifications › 기기 푸시 알림 no longer answers every failure with "푸시 설정을 저장하지 못했어요. 연결 상태를 확인하고 다시 시도해 주세요." It names the reason: this browser has no Web Push, or the address is not https; the browser's push service is off or out of reach (in Brave, how to turn on "푸시 메시지에 Google 서비스 사용"; a private window); the team server does not take this browser's push service; the browser did not get notifications ready (reload the page); the browser keeps the site from storing data (Firefox's "never remember history", blocked site data); the team server has no usable Web Push key (an admin is told what to fix, a member only to let an admin know; the server now marks this case, so a relay answer while the team server is away reads as no connection, with **다시 시도**); too many devices get push already; the browser's question was closed without an answer; another turn-on is still waiting for the browser's question ("이미 진행 중이에요"); notifications are blocked for the site (now also the row's status line, with how to undo it, and that a private window cannot turn them on); or the team server could not be reached. **다시 시도** shows only where trying again can help (no connection, too many requests, a server fault). An unexpected failure goes to the browser console with its name and message, never the push address or keys. In the desktop app there is no Web Push row any more (Electron has no push service, so the button always failed): **이 컴퓨터 앱 알림** says the app notifies through the computer while its window is not in front, and **테스트 알림** sends one now (the desktop app writes it, at most 3 a minute apart from real notifications) and, when the system refused it, says where to turn the app's notifications on (macOS: 시스템 설정 › 알림 › Trina AX; Windows: 설정 › 시스템 › 알림). Owners and admins still see the phone push line there. A desktop app from before this shows the row without the button. New optional desktop bridge call `testNotify`. In a web browser that has not been asked about notifications yet, one quiet line (at the top on a computer, above the tabs on a phone) says "새 메시지 알림을 받으려면 [알림 켜기] · [나중에]": 알림 켜기 asks the browser and turns push on (a short message confirms it; a failure says why in the line, with the way to Settings › 알림), 나중에 puts it away for 7 days on that device, and the browser's "block" for good. It shows only when the team server offers Web Push and you did not turn push off yourself, and first on the page after the one where it became ready, so nothing moves under your finger. The phone app is unchanged. In all five languages.
- Voice input on your own server: saving the team's OpenRouter key in **Admin › Team settings › 음성 입력** now turns it on by itself (the provider reads OpenRouter while no admin chose one; removing the key turns it off again, and an admin's choice, 끄기 too, always wins). Next to the key, "OpenRouter 요금은 녹음 1시간에 약 $0.1예요" says what it costs. With **이 서버에서** chosen on a server without whisper.cpp or ffmpeg (an office Mac has neither), the mic no longer shows and then fails every recording: it stays hidden and Team settings says which program is missing. In all five languages.
- @트리나 in a room now reads your own messages as yours: paste a long inquiry ending with "이거 바탕으로 견적서 만들어줘", then send "@트리나 ㄱㄱ", and it makes the quote. A short question right after your own messages (nobody else in between, within half an hour) takes them in full as part of the question, and long messages keep their start and their end instead of only the start. Other people's messages are still information only, never instructions.
- @트리나 in a room now sees the files sent with your question: "@트리나 이거 공용 폴더에 넣어줘" with a picture works. Pictures are shown to the AI as pictures and documents and PDFs as text, the same as files attached in your own AI chat, and it can also open a file sent earlier in the room ("아까 올린 PDF 요약해줘") when you can see that message (not one from before you joined). Putting a copy into a team folder asks you on the room's card first, then on the "more people can see this" card when the folder is wider than the room. Never into skills/ or as an instruction file. The files stay where room files are kept.
- Chat rooms: reading a room or a thread in the phone app (and in Chrome, Firefox or Edge on an iPhone) now counts. Until now they marked nothing read when you opened a room from a notice and read it without touching the screen, so your computer kept the room's badge and played the sound for it. A room you read on one device now clears on all the others, and a computer next to the phone stays quiet: the sound waits a moment and does not ring for a message you have just read elsewhere. A page left open across a server update reloads the rooms instead of keeping an old badge.
- Text size, like a phone's: **Settings › Profile › 글자 크기** is a slider from a small 가 to a large 가 with five steps (작게, 기본, 크게, 더 크게, 아주 크게: 90% to 140%), and the line under it reads at the chosen size. The whole app follows when you let go: chats, the sidebar, Settings, Admin and the phone's tabs, in the browser, the desktop app and the phone app; the rows, buttons and gaps around the text grow with it, so nothing is cut, and at 아주 크게 a 390px phone still has nothing sideways. It is saved to your account like the theme, so your other devices get it when you sign in or reload, and kept on this device so the sign-in screen and the first paint use it too. 기본 is the way back. `UserPrefs` gains `textSize` (optional). No database migration. In all five languages.
- Admin › Groups: in a group's member list you come first, marked "나" (You), so adding yourself is the first tick; everyone else keeps the team order. In all five languages.
- Computers: the sidebar shows one row per item again, with its name: **트리나에게 묻기**, **검색**, **프로젝트**, **예약 작업**, **알림함** (with its count), **파일**, **팀 스킬** and, for owners and admins, **관리자**, instead of a row of icons. **대화** and **트리나 기록** always end with a way to the whole list, also when it is short: "더 보기 (N)" or, with nothing hidden, **모든 대화** (the conversations page), and "모두 보기" for the whole AI history. In all five languages.
- Chat rooms on computers: hovering a message shows a small bar at its top-right, as in Slack: ✅ 👀 👍 in one click, **반응 추가** for all eight, **스레드에서 답장**, and **⋮ 더 보기** (copy, and edit or delete for your own message). Tab reaches it too, and Esc closes its menus. It sits under the first message so the header never cuts it, and nothing on the page moves when it shows. Phones keep the long press. Icon buttons in the rooms (the bar, the room header, the message box, the thread panel, the "…" menus) now show a short tooltip saying what they do: after a moment with the mouse, or at once with the keyboard; never on touch screens. In all five languages.
- Connecting the team's AI no longer asks for your password. In Admin › Providers and the **AI 연결하기** dialog, adding a Claude or ChatGPT subscription sign-in, adding an API key (Anthropic, OpenAI) and replacing a token or key go straight through, for the owner and admins alike (the terms checkbox stays, members are still refused, and the owner is still told of an admin's change). Those accounts only ever talk to their own provider, so swapping one gains nothing. The team API engine form still asks for your password, because there you choose the address the team's conversations are sent to.
- Session markers: every AI chat in **트리나 기록** shows at most one mark at the right end of its row. **?** in amber when the AI waits for you (a question, a confirmation, a site permission or a connect card, until you answer it; or an answer that failed or paused at a limit, until you open it), a grey ring while it answers or waits its turn (standing still with reduced motion), and a dot with the title in bold for a new answer you have not opened. Hovering shows "···" in its place, and the preview's first line says why; on a phone the mark sits left of "···". It comes from the chat list, so a reload, the phone and the desktop app agree, and opening a chat clears it everywhere. "모두 보기 · 답 필요 K" counts the hidden chats with a **?** or a dot (amber when one is a **?**, failed and paused answers included), and its filter "답변 중이거나 내 답을 기다림" also lists failed and paused answers. One number counts the chats and rooms that want you, each once: the tab title "(n) Trina" while the tab is hidden and the desktop app's Dock badge on a Mac. Rooms count when the AI waits for your answer there (a **?** pill in place of the room's count) or something unread is for you by your notification level for the room (by default a 1:1 or group chat message, a mention, a reply in a thread you follow). On a phone, ☰ on the 트리나 tab gets a dot when a chat in it wants you (amber for a **?**), and the 대화 tab shows an amber **?** while the AI waits for you in a room (its count is unchanged and comes back after). An answer you stopped yourself, or one to a question asked from Slack (it was answered there), gets no dot. A notification (desktop app, while its window is not focused; its click now opens that chat or room) and on a phone a toast with [열기] come only when a card opens or an answer that ran over a minute ends while no screen shows that chat, after 3 seconds; never for scheduled runs, questions asked from Slack, or all at once after a server restart. A confidential chat's notification says neither its title nor the question. The desktop app also notifies the room messages the Inbox and push notify you of, and closes a team's notifications when you switch teams. Replaces the "답변 필요" text and dots and the intro deck's own dot, toast and notification. `Chat` gains `lastOutcome`, `Room` gains `aiWaits`, `chat.updated` gains `notify` and `room.message` gains `notice` (all optional). No database migration. In all five languages.
- Computers: the sidebar puts people first. The team's name is on top, then one row for **트리나에게 묻기** (a new AI chat) and search and one row of icons for Files, Projects, Scheduled tasks, Team skills, the Inbox (with its count) and Admin (owners and admins), each with its name on hover and on keyboard focus. Then **대화**: the five most recent conversations (and the one on screen), and "더 보기 (N)" for the rest, which shows how many unread messages are behind it ("안 읽음 M", with @ when one mentions you); then **트리나 기록**: the five latest AI chats (and the one on screen), and "모두 보기" for the whole history, which shows how many of the hidden ones need you ("답 필요 K"). So 트리나 기록 stays in sight however many conversations you have. Both open in place, fold, and stay as you left them. A chat whose AI waits for you says "답변 필요", and a chat whose answer finished while you were elsewhere gets a dot until you open it, in every window and on every device. One database migration (when each chat was last seen); it runs by itself when the server starts. In all five languages.
- Phones: four tabs, **트리나 · 대화 · 파일 · 나**, and the app opens on 트리나: the greeting and the message box, then the three latest AI chats. ☰ is only there and holds the AI's history (search, projects, scheduled tasks, team skills, 트리나 기록, 삭제한 기록); 대화 has the rooms, DMs and group chats with ⊕ for a new conversation; 나 starts with the Inbox (its unread count is on the tab) and, for owners and admins, Admin. Pages like Team skills show ‹ and their name in the top bar. The back arrow in a chat, a room or a page now goes back where you came from instead of opening the list again, so Android's back button no longer loops. Removed on phones: the pinned AI row in 대화, the menu button and its red dot on other tabs, the new-chat icon in the top bar, and the New chat, Inbox and Admin rows of the menu. In all five languages.
- People and the AI have their own words everywhere you move around: **대화** for rooms, group chats and DMs (새 대화 to start one), **트리나에게 묻기** for a new AI chat, **트리나 기록** for the AI's past chats (the computer's sidebar, the phone's menu and the 트리나 tab), **삭제한 기록** for deleted ones. No bare "채팅" in the menus any more. In all five languages (English: Conversations, Ask Trina, Trina history, Deleted history).
- Members bring their own skills and files to the team. Under a member's first good answer from the skill their invite opened, the one suggestion line asks "내가 쓰던 스킬이나 파일도 팀에 올릴래요? · 올리기" (✕ closes it for good). On a computer it opens one sheet: pick or drop a `.claude`, `.codex` or `.agents` folder, read in the browser as the owner's 내 스킬 가져오기 reads it (a skill with a key never leaves the computer), tick up to 10 skills ("팀장이 내용을 보고 정해요") and send them as suggestions; a link to the team folder for files when the member can write it. On a phone the line offers only that folder, and nothing without one. The server checks every skill again (keys, scripts, size, names, paths on the member's computer), keeps it outside the team files and runs no AI on it; up to 20 suggestions of a member wait at a time. The owner and admins get one Inbox line per send ("민준님이 스킬 2개를 팀에 올리자고 했어요 · 확인"), and Skills › 검토 groups suggestions by person. Only they approve such a skill: it is saved as `skills/<folder name>/` with its files, the member is told, and the skill shows "민준님이 제안". Approving never changes the skill the invite opens or the 7-day goal names. Database migration 257 (additive).
- Admins can connect the team's AI, not only the owner. In Admin › Providers an admin can add an account (a Claude subscription sign-in or token, an API key, a ChatGPT sign-in), replace a token or key, rename, order, assign, limit, switch off and remove the team's connections (also ones the owner added, and the owner can change ones an admin added), turn on or off members' own connections and shared subscriptions, and edit the team API engine. Adding, replacing and starting a sign-in ask the admin for their own password again, like the owner. When an admin adds, replaces, removes, switches off, gives to other people or limits to zero a connection, turns on shared subscriptions or changes the API engine, the owner gets a short notice (live, and in the inbox if they are away) saying who and what (never the key); the owner's own changes send none. Editing the API engine asks for the admin's own password, an admin can only set a public https address (checked again at every call), calls never follow a redirect, and changing its address never carries the old key over: the key is kept with the whole address it was made for, path included, and a key for the new address has to be entered with it. An admin can switch off or remove a member's own connection (the member is told) but not the owner's or another admin's. Only one AI sign-in runs at a time: when one is in the way, the owner or an admin sees whose it is and can stop it from the Add account dialog (a member whose own sign-in is stopped is told); a team sign-in whose starter stopped being an admin, or whose account was disabled or deleted, does not finish. Saving the edit dialog without changing anything but the name no longer notifies the owner. One database migration (116_a_credential_change_notices, a table for those inbox rows), which runs by itself and does not raise the reader level. A member without AI who taps "tell the owner" now reaches the admins too, and an admin with no AI sees the same "connect AI" button as the owner. Members' own connections are unchanged. Updates, restores, member removal and the team name stay with the owner. New auth level `admin-reauth` in the HTTP API (11 endpoints are now admin instead of owner; see the API reference). In all five languages.
- Chat rooms: a name picked from the **@** list now stands out in the message box at once, in the same colour as on sent messages with a soft background, so you can see who will be notified before you send. **@트리나** stands out the same way where it calls the AI. A mention acts as one piece: Backspace at its end removes all of it, the caret steps over it, and changing a letter inside it turns it back into plain text that notifies nobody. A pasted or typed "@name" that was not picked from the list stays plain text and notifies nobody, as before; what stands out is exactly who is mentioned. Also in a thread's reply box and when editing a message; a draft keeps its mentions when you switch rooms. Korean and Japanese input works as before (the box is still the browser's own text box; the colours are drawn in layers around it). A person you mention whose name begins with an AI name ("@Trina Kim") no longer also calls the AI.
- Admin › Home no longer lists engines nobody set up while another AI is set up or only at its limit: a team on Claude alone, with its one connection at a usage limit, sees only the limit line (once, and none when the "people cannot use AI" line already says it), not "Codex: 로그인 필요" and "API: 설치 안 됨". When no AI is set up at all, one line ("AI가 연결되어 있지 않아요") replaces one line per engine; a broken engine and a not-set-up team default engine keep their lines, and "N건 확인" on the Providers card counts the lines shown. Admin › Providers shows an engine whose team connections only wait for a limit to end as "한도에 닿음" instead of "로그인 필요". `GET /api/admin/engines` has a new status `limited` and `limitedUntil`. In all five languages.
- Conversation notifications like Slack's. **Settings › Notifications** now starts (after the Inbox) with **대화**: what to be notified about (1:1 and group conversations plus mentions of you, the default and what happened before; all new messages in every room you are in; or nothing), replies in threads you take part in (on by default), do-not-disturb hours (off by default; 22:00–08:00 to start, in the team's time zone, optionally all weekend: no push and no sound, the badges stay) and the rooms you turned off, each with its level to change back. Each room's bell is now a menu: default (from settings, showing what it gives that room), all messages, mentions only, or off; mute is "off". A mention of you and a reply in a thread you follow always count, except in a room that is off. The notices, Web Push, phone push and the notification sound all follow the same rule. The page itself lost its boxes: plain sections (대화, 작업·연결 알림, 이 기기) with one row per setting, a short line under each and the switch on the right. Searching Settings for 소리, 방해 금지, 스레드 or 멘션 finds it. One database migration (255_b_room_notify_prefs, additive), which runs by itself; an earlier version can still read the database (it keeps mute and the old switch). In all five languages.
- Admins can delete people now. Until now only the team owner could (**…** menu in Admin › People); an admin could only disable someone or reset their password. An admin deletes a member only, never the owner, another admin or themselves, and types their own password again first. Deleting works as before: sessions end at once, chats and memories are deleted after the retention period, and the personal files go to an archive in the owner's personal folder (or are deleted). The owner's delete is unchanged. The server checks the person's role at the moment of the delete, so a member promoted a second ago cannot be deleted by an admin. Requests that members send from Settings › Profile (**계정 삭제 요청**) now also reach the admins in their inbox, and the texts say "the team owner or an admin". `DELETE /api/admin/users/:id` is admin-level now (it was owner-only). In all five languages.
- Your own AI chat can read your conversations (대화방): say "마케팅 대화방 봐봐", "김민지님이랑 1:1에서 뭐라고 했지" or "그 방에 올린 PDF 요약해줘", and the AI lists your rooms, searches their messages by words, person and date, reads a room or a thread (the newest 50 messages, up to 200) and reads a file sent there (documents and PDFs as text, pictures as pictures; not video or audio). Or type **#** in the message box and pick a room: it goes in as a token, and the AI reads exactly that room (also with a skill: `/회의록 #마케팅`). It sees exactly what you see: only rooms you are in now, from when you joined, threads by their first message, your own private AI previews but nobody else's, no deleted messages; admins see nothing more. Room messages are other people's words, so once a room was read in a chat, every change the AI makes there (saving or moving files, memories, schedules, connected services) waits for your yes on a card, for the rest of that chat. Not in confidential chats or inside rooms. The steps say it in words ("대화방 읽기 · 마케팅"), in all five languages; the admin's audit log names no room or person. One database migration (254, a mark on the chat); it runs by itself when the server starts.
- The desktop app's icon fills its tile like the web app's Dock icon: the mark is about a third bigger, with the same warm-black tile and light edge. Its source is now `desktop/scripts/trina-icon.svg`.
- Admin › Members: when only a limit keeps someone from AI (the team's Claude subscription at its weekly limit, for example), the "No AI" badge says so and until when ("AI 한도 · 11월 8일 오전 12:59까지"), and its hint says it comes back by itself then, or sooner with another connection in Providers. Out of credit, refused, switched off or no connection stays plain "No AI". Admin Home's line says the same when everyone on it only waits for a limit. `GET /api/admin/users?noAi=1` also returns `noAiUntil` (person → end time; `noAi` is unchanged). In all five languages.
- Settings › Apps on a computer: only this computer's desktop installer is in sight. A Mac no longer sees "Download for Windows" as its app; without a Mac app the page says so and points at installing it as an app, and the other computer's installers are one folded line away. Installing as an app now has a picture for each step that follows the browser: Chrome and Edge (the address bar's install icon, Install, then the Dock or the taskbar), Safari on a Mac (File → Add to Dock…, Add), and Firefox says it cannot install and names the browsers that can. The other devices' steps are folded on every screen. In all five languages.
- Settings > Apps: adding the app to a phone's home screen now shows a picture of each step with the place to tap, in your team's own address and icon. The steps follow iOS 26, where Safari's share button is in the ⋯ menu next to the address bar.
- The app icon fills its square: in the Dock and on a home screen the mark is about half again as big (it sat small in a wide black frame), and the tab icon's mark is a little bigger too.
- Chat rooms work like Slack: threads, reactions, and no read receipts. Any message can get replies in a thread (**스레드에서 답장** on a message's hover row, or a long press on a phone): the replies stay in the thread, not in the room, and under the message you see how many there are, who replied and when. On a computer the thread opens beside the room; on a phone it fills the screen. Tick **대화방에도 보내기** to put a reply in the room too. A thread notifies the person who wrote the message, everyone who replied and anyone mentioned (not when they muted the room); for them a thread with new replies counts in the room's badge, shows a dot under the message, and the room's header has a mark that opens it; nobody else is notified. Someone who joined a room later sees no thread of a message from before they joined. **@트리나** in a thread answers in the thread, from that thread only, and keeps one conversation per thread. Reactions: 👍 ✅ 👀 🙏 😂 🎉 ❤️ 🔥 under a message, tap to add or remove yours, hover or long-press to see who; they never notify or count as unread. The number of people who have not read a message is gone; the quoted reply ("답장") is replaced by threads (old quotes still show). One database migration (251_b_room_threads: three columns, a table of who follows which thread and a table of reactions), which runs by itself; it raises the database reader level to 6, so going back to an earlier version afterwards means restoring the backup taken before the update. In all five languages.
- Group chats and room names. **New chat** (the + by 대화방, and the 대화 list on phones) now lets you tick several people, or a group to tick its people at once (who is in the group now; the chat does not follow the group later). One person opens your 1:1 chat as before; two or more make a group chat, with an optional name that appears only then. An unnamed group chat shows the other people's names ("가, 나, 다 외 2명"). You can have several group chats with the same people; one holds up to 100 people. Tap a room's name at the top to see who is in it: there anyone in a group chat can rename it, invite people (they see the chat from then on, never what was said before) or leave (the last one out closes it), and its manager (방장: whoever made it, then whoever joined first) or an owner or admin in it can remove someone. In a 1:1 chat, 초대하기 starts a new group chat with that person ticked; the 1:1 chat stays. Owners and admins can rename a team room or the everyone room they are in; emptying the name brings the group's own name back. Renames, invites, leaving and removals show as a quiet line in the chat; these lines count as nothing unread, send no notice and are never given to @트리나, and @트리나 never sees a room's own name. A message in a group chat notifies like a 1:1 message (mute works as before; the notification setting now says "1:1 and group chats"). One person can make up to 10 new group chats an hour. Group chats, like 1:1 chats, are not in the admins' activity log. One database migration (250_b_group_rooms, two new columns), which runs by itself; it raises the database reader level to 5, so going back to an earlier version afterwards means restoring the backup taken before the update (the update screen says so). In all five languages.
- A short, soft sound when a room message meant for you arrives and you are not looking at that room: every DM or group chat message, a mention of you in a team room or the everyone room, and the AI's answer to your question. Not for your own messages, muted rooms, edits or messages caught up after a reconnect; one sound at most every 3 seconds, from one tab per device. **Settings › Notifications › 알림 소리** turns it off on this device. Browsers play it after your first click or tap on the page; the phone app plays it while open (in the background the phone's notification sounds instead, and an open browser tab makes a Web Push notice silent so you hear one sound). In all five languages.
- Company intro deck, round 2: every content slide now carries one figure drawn by the server in one design language, and covers, openings and closings get backgrounds. One database migration (decks being built slide by slide); it runs by itself when the server starts. In all five languages.
  - Figures from the material's own words: process flows, cycles, hubs, layered stacks, timelines (years, or an order of places), before → after comparisons, funnels, many-channels-into-one, offering grids with 174 Lucide icons, product picture cards, and charts only for numbers written in the material (donuts, bars at true scale, lines, big figures). A figure that implies an order, a loop, a centre or a stack needs a sentence from the material that says so.
  - Backgrounds: the website's best wide picture with a shade only where the type sits (never over lettering in the photo), or a ground drawn from the brand colour (a glow, a mesh, dots, lines or contour lines) that fades away before the type. On the cover and the closing the AI may draw one abstract accent (shapes and lines only, at most two per deck), checked against a strict list of allowed SVG elements.
  - The deck is made in steps: an outline, then one slide at a time, then the server draws every slide, checks contrast and busy areas on the pixels, fixes what it can, and shows the slides to the AI once to fix up to three. The chat shows what is happening ("5장 · 숫자를 도넛 차트로 그리는 중") with the slides filling in one by one, and the panel shows the newest slide; the deck keeps being made on the server while the person uses other menus or closes the page, and when it is done elsewhere, a notice with [열기]. With the 3-minute budget it takes about 2 minutes at about twice the AI usage of the first version.
  - Three time budgets, chosen by owners and admins in Admin › Team settings ("회사 소개서 만들기 시간", 5 minutes by default): 5 and 10 minutes read more of the company's website and search the web for sourced facts (each cited with its outlet and date in the speaker notes) and look at the slides again; 3 minutes uses what was read at setup; the setting says how much more AI each uses.
  - The PowerPoint file keeps diagrams as editable shapes; the PDF and the preview look the same. On a phone the preview pages through the slides at full width; on a computer a strip of slide thumbnails sits under the deck.
  - The website reading at setup keeps up to 12 pictures (was 8), larger ones at 1,920 px.
- Phones 320 px wide: the chat Home's greeting and message box stay on the screen; they ran past its right edge.
- Onboarding: the owner's steps are 회사 소개 → 내 스킬 → 팀 파일 → 도구 연결 → 팀원 초대 → 준비 끝났어요 (with AI 연결 first only while no AI answers; 도구 연결 adds Google Drive or another tool over MCP), and one setup row on every page. Database migrations 249 (share proposals) and 253 (merges that wait, the merge notice, cleared closed proposals), which run by themselves. In all five languages.
  - **설정 이어서 하기 · 2/4** sits in the sidebar above the AI's chat list on every page until the steps are finished, and opens them where they stopped; while no AI answers it reads **AI 연결하기**. ✕ hides it (with Undo); Admin › Home's **시작 설정 다시 보기** brings it back, and Admin › Home's checklist waits until the steps are done. On a phone it is in the ☰ menu, and Home keeps its quiet row under the message box.
  - The end, **준비 끝났어요** (once): **'{skill}' 먼저 써 보기**, the same form a member lands on; the skill is the one the server points the invite and the goal at.
  - The invite names one skill: the first one shared from 내 스킬, else Home (the 7-day goal counts the same one, and open links that still pointed at the deck move to it once). It says "본인 AI 계정이 필요해요" when each person signs in to their own AI. A skill's form starts with the skill's own example, and shows the connect or ask line instead of 실행 while no AI answers.
  - **내 스킬 가져오기** brings the owner's Claude Code or Codex skills to the team: pick or drop a `.claude`, `.codex` or `.agents` folder (Codex prompts count as commands, AGENTS.md becomes a team rule, `config.toml`'s remote MCP servers become connection templates without keys), or paste one line into Claude Code or Codex once the team connector is added (`claude mcp add …` or `codex mcp add …`). Only skills, the rule file and tool addresses are read; a skill with a key or token never leaves the browser; the server checks everything again; nothing reaches the team before [팀에 공유 · N개]. Applying a proposal anywhere counts the step as done; a merge into an existing team skill waits until the steps are finished, then one Inbox notice "합칠 스킬 N개 확인하기" opens one merge chat, which starts merging when it is opened (so its confirmations are there while you look, on a phone too). Hiding the setup row counts as finishing for these merges only after its undo time; a dismissed or expired proposal takes its waiting merges back. The merge chat can now only read the skills to merge and save the team skill (with the usual confirmation); before, it was an ordinary chat with every tool. A proposal that is dismissed or expires keeps no skill names or descriptions.
  - 팀 파일 가져오기 puts the team's documents into the team's folder (or another group's) with the usual who-sees line.
  - From the review and a walk through the steps: invited members land on the shared skill's form with its example (no longer a chat on Home saying "이 방식대로 해 줘"); skills without a title are named as words ("quote maker", not "quote-maker"); 내 스킬 keeps its list when you leave the step and come back, a finished 내 스킬 shows what went in with "다른 폴더도 가져오기" for a second folder, and without AI the skills found are listed as "찾은 스킬"; 회사 소개 asks once ("저장하고 계속" on its review too), its fallback has one main button, a failed reading says whether the website or the pasted text failed and offers 다시 시도, and the typed address survives leaving mid-reading; the goal card names its skill, says "아직 아무도 안 들어왔어요" only from the third day, no longer implies a member failed, uses the right Korean particle, and during setup its 팀원 초대 opens the setup's invite step; a phone owner who left 내 스킬 for a computer sees one line on Home saying so; "AI 연결하기" in the setup row opens "AI 연결" with the same three ways as the first step; hiding the setup row says where to bring it back; a deck that stopped no longer reads "6장 중 6장"; screen readers no longer hear the AI step as put off.
  - Security: a key inside an MCP address's host name (not only its path) keeps that tool out of a share, from Claude Code and Codex too; the Codex scan reads `config.toml` up to 1 MB (was 64 KB, servers after it were dropped silently) and takes the header that carries the key; a skill example with hidden or text-direction characters no longer fills a form.
  - The MCP connector no longer lets an app change a person's own skills (`users/<id>/skills/`) without the in-app confirmation: such writes are refused, as for team skills. New connector tool `share_skills` (proposals only), for Claude Code and Codex.
  - The Mac App Store desktop app opens its folder panel on `~/.claude` with hidden folders shown for this step.
  - From a recheck of the steps: 회사 소개 offers "홈페이지 다시 읽기" only when there is a homepage; after "다른 폴더도 가져오기" the finished 내 스킬 lists every skill shared in the setup, not only the last folder's; a skill without a title is "quote maker" on the member's form, its run button, the team skills list, the chat chip and the answer's receipt too; the setup row and a phone's Home row name the one step left ("남은 일: 팀 파일") and otherwise count only the steps done, so a step put off no longer reads as "4/4"; 내 스킬's phone text and Claude Code line use the product's name ("트리나" before). In all five languages.
- Admin Home no longer lists an engine nobody set up (Codex without a ChatGPT connection, the API engine without settings) as something to fix while another engine answers. It still does for the team's default engine, for a broken engine, and when no engine works.
- Claude sign-in from the browser: **Copy link** next to "Open Claude to approve", for when Claude is signed in in another browser or on another device. In all five languages.
- Connecting Claude to the team server: the sign-in page now lets you choose what else Claude may do, whatever the app asked for. Reading your files is always allowed; **파일 쓰기** (write files) is a checkbox, so claude.ai and Claude Code, which ask for reading only, can now also create, edit and upload files when you tick it. For owners and admins one line says the connection also manages the team's groups. The app is told exactly what you allowed, existing connections keep what they had and can only be narrowed. In all five languages.
- Owners and admins can manage the team's groups with their own Claude through the MCP connector, as in Admin › Groups: five new tools list people and groups, create a group (with its folder), rename it, add and take out people, and delete it. Each change answers with who now opens or loses which folders and what else they get or lose through the group (team connections, templates, team Google Drive shares, AI connections, budgets, engine choice, dev environment, team chat room), and goes through the same checks and audit log entries as the web (marked as coming from the connector). The new `mcp:admin` permission comes with every new connection of an owner or admin, with no checkbox; members never get it; existing connections are unchanged (Settings › AI apps asks an owner or admin to connect again once), and the role is checked on every call (someone made member or disabled loses it on all their connections). In all five languages.
- **Connectors** is one place for everything the AI here uses with your accounts, like Claude's Connectors: MCP and App connectors are no longer two sections (their old addresses lead here), in Settings (just for you) and in Admin (for the whole team). **Yours** lists what you have connected; **Discover** starts with "Top connectors", Google Drive first, then the best-known MCP services, with the rest behind Show all. A card opens the connector's own page: a big logo, Connect, what it does and the tools the AI gets. Google Drive's + goes straight to Google; back from it one line confirms the account (a security step) and it is connected. Admins set up Google once on Drive's page; true zero setup would need a Google sign-in Trina runs itself. In Admin a connector page's Connect adds it with one team key, and its ⌄ makes a template each member connects. Adding a custom connector is one column, Name and Server URL, with type, sign-in, what the AI may change and who may use it under a collapsed Advanced.

## 0.4.0 — 2026-11-09

[Published release and notes](https://github.com/trina-app/trina-ax/releases/tag/v0.4.0)

The stable release of 0.4.0-beta.1 to 0.4.0-beta.10 (below): team chat rooms with the AI, the iPhone app with push, welcome steps for teams that are not developers, Slack, MCP templates, and calmer Admin and Settings pages.

**Upgrade note (from 0.3.0):** the database migrations of the betas run by themselves at the first start; they add tables and columns. The root part changed (`mycel-ctl`, `mycel-launcher`), so a Linux server takes this with a root update: the update timer applies it in the team's update window, or run `sudo mycel-ctl update`. Take a backup first (Admin › System › Backups › Back up now). Two things a 0.3.0 server did differently: the AI makes and fills in Hangul documents only while the HWP plugin is installed and on (Admin › Plugins; 0.4.0-beta.2), and the dev environment is on by default (an admin can turn it off in Admin › Team settings; 0.4.0-beta.10).

- Desktop app icon: the Trina mark is bigger, so it no longer looks lost on the Dock and the taskbar.

## 0.4.0-beta.10 — 2026-11-06

Beta channel only; stable stays on 0.3.0

**Upgrade note:** six database migrations (235 intro decks, 236 dev environment queue, 247 dev environment sites, 333, 334 and 336 MCP integration templates); they run by themselves at the first start and only add tables and columns.
