Update as root as before: the update timer applies it in the team's update window, or run `sudo mycel-ctl update`. The dev environment is on by default from this version; an admin can turn it off in Admin › Team settings.

- Phones: lean first screens. Home shows the greeting, the message box, one row for the 7-day goal, one row for the folders your AI can see (a sheet lists them) and two plain suggestions; settings, 보안·기기, Usage, Members, the file preview and rooms drop repeated labels, frames and duplicate buttons, and everything removed is one tap away.
- A name that already ends in 님 or 씨 (or さん, 様 in Japanese) no longer gets a second one, in the app and in Slack.
- Admin › System reads like a settings page too: plain sections without boxes, in a fixed order (Storage, Version and updates, Health, Connection, Connected folders, Backups, Export; an admin sees the plan, Storage and office updates). Storage is one line with its state, a thin bar and quiet rows for what uses the space; Version and updates and Connection are label / value rows with the action on the right; Health lists what needs attention first and folds the checks that passed into one line with "Show details". Long explanations sit behind "Learn more". Same actions, confirmations and passwords as before; in all five languages.
- Admin › Team settings reads like a settings page: plain sections (General, Time and updates, Keeping data, Members and AI, Voice input), one row per setting with a short line under its name and the control on the right, and the longer explanations behind "Learn more". The paragraph about saving is gone: a save bar ("Unsaved changes · Cancel · Save") appears only when something changed. The team logo is a row with its previews behind "Show previews". Same settings and the same saving as before; in all five languages.
- Admin and Settings are easier to find your way in. **Providers** is one page instead of AI engines plus AI connections, first in the Connections group: each provider row (Claude Code, Codex) shows its accounts in the order they are tried, with **Add account** for the owner, which opens the same connect steps for that provider only. The team's AI settings that were its second tab (AI defaults, who picks the engine and the switch to Codex, members' own AI accounts with shared subscriptions, usage limits) are now groups at the end of **Team settings**, each saving on its own as before. The Admin menu is People · Connections (Providers, MCP, App connectors, AI apps, Plugins) · Activity · Team (Team settings, System), and Admin Home has one Providers card. Settings has named groups, Account (with Memory) · Connections (Providers first) · App, and its AI connections section is now **Providers**, by provider. Every Admin page says "For the whole team" next to its title and every Settings section says "Just for you". Old addresses keep working: `/admin/engines` and `/admin/credentials` open Providers (the second at Claude Code), their old team tab (`?tab=team`) opens Team settings at the AI settings, a usage-limits link opens Team settings at Usage limits, and `/settings/credentials` opens Settings › Providers. A usage-limit warning now links to Team settings. In all five languages.
- App connectors are now simply the apps the AI here uses with your own account, as cards: Google Drive's **Connect** goes straight to Google sign-in, then the card asks you to confirm the account (a security step, kept) and shows it with ⋯ Reconnect and Disconnect. No guide text or setup code on the page. AI apps such as Claude (the other direction) have their own section, Settings › AI apps and Admin › AI apps, with the same Connect steps. Admins can now set Google Drive up in Admin › App connectors: **Set up** takes the Google OAuth client file (or its ID and secret) and members can connect right away, without the installer or a restart; the page shows how many members are connected. A hosted server, one without a public address, or a client the operator set stays as it is (one line says why). New admin-only `GET /api/admin/google-drive` and `PUT /api/admin/google-drive/client`; the client secret is stored only in the server's private client file and never returned or logged. In all five languages.
- App connectors: connecting an AI app is guided. Under AI apps there is one row per app (Claude, Claude Code, other MCP apps) with Connect, which opens numbered steps with this server's connector address and Copy: for Claude, add a custom connector in Claude's Settings › Connectors; for Claude Code, the exact `claude mcp add --transport http …` command and then `/mcp`; other apps get the address and a note that apps signing in through their own website (ChatGPT, for example) cannot connect yet. The dialog says "Connected" when the app appears, and connected apps show when they connected. With the connector off, members see one line and owners and admins get the three ways to turn it on, with Copy. Admin › App connectors shows the same apps. UI only; in all five languages.
- Google Drive is an app connector now. Settings › App connectors is one list with logos: Google Drive first (its + opens the same Google sign-in in a dialog; once linked the row shows the account with Reconnect and Disconnect, and a Drive that needs a new sign-in says so on its row), then AI apps such as Claude (the connector address with Copy, and each connected app with Disconnect; no connected app is one quiet line). Without a Google client on the server Drive says "Not available yet" (admins get the setup guide). The separate Settings › Google Drive section is gone; `/settings/drive` and the notice link open the Drive dialog in App connectors, and searching "drive" in Settings finds it. New Admin › App connectors (under Shared with the team): whether this server offers Google Drive and the MCP connector for AI apps, what members see, the connector address and the setup guides. Both are set in the server setup, so it shows their state instead of a switch. Same sign-in steps and API as before. In all five languages.
- Admin › People laid out like the Claude Console's Members. Members: the title with how many people, one line, "Team invite link" and "Invite to team" on the right, a search box (name, username or email) and a Role filter, then a quiet table of name, username (and email), role and the team skills switch. The owner changes a member's or admin's role right in the row; every other action, and "Folders they see" for anyone, is in the row's "…". The team invite link is made in a popup from its button, not an inline form; invite links are a quiet list under the table, shown only when there are any. Folder permissions: the header stays at the top with Add folder, the by folder / by person switch and the view's own filter; no card around the tree or By person; tighter tree rows that say where a rule comes from only when a parent's rule decides, the whole-team warning as a small chip, and one line per person in the chosen folder. Its rule editor (Change rules) is shorter: one title line with the folder, the basic access as four short choices (only "Same as the folder above" adds what it gives now), people and groups as compact rows, and the result as counts with the people behind "Show people"; the three explanations of how rules work fold behind "How rules work", and every warning stays, in one line each. Groups: the same header with a count and a search box, rows of name, members and the skills switch, with Edit and Delete in "…". Phone width shows stacked rows. In all five languages; no server change.
- Usage looks like the Claude Console. Admin › Usage has filter buttons (Range, Group, Person, Engine, Model, AI connection, Group by), cards with tokens in, tokens out, answers and the estimated cost, a "Token usage" chart with tokens per day stacked by the Group by, and the breakdown table under it; Settings › Usage uses the same cards and period button. The numbers are the same as before. The admin usage report (`GET /api/admin/usage`, admins only) takes these filters and can also return the tokens per day and the filter choices; nothing else on the server changed. In all five languages.
- MCP templates, the rest of the brief: members a template is for get an Inbox notice that opens its Connect (once each: when it is made for them, a rule or group brings it to them, or they join while it is for everyone), and "Reconnect" when a new address or sign-in deleted their key. In a chat, the AI offers the connect card for the member's own key ("Connect your own key for Demo Wiki"); it connects through the template and is refused if the template changed after the card was shown. "Template · N connected" opens who it is for, who connected it and when, and whose key stopped working: names and states, never a key. A scheduled task that needs a template does not run without its owner's own key: its result says "Needs your own key for …", the owner is told, and it never uses anyone else's key. Two database migrations (334, and 336 for the safe-operations policy), which run by themselves. In all five languages.
- Admin › MCP: asking a member to connect a service is now a popup from Add connection's menu ("Ask a member to connect…"), not a card on the page. Sent, the popup closes with a short note; the request is one row in the Connected list ("Requested · Alice", copy its link, withdraw it), and stays there for two weeks once connected, declined or expired (declined or expired: ask again with the same service and member). The page's line under the title is shorter. In all five languages.
- MCP templates: an admin registers a service once, and each member connects it with their own key or sign-in. In Admin › MCP, "Add connection" asks who connects: for the team with one key (as before, the default) or a template. A template has the address, sign-in method, who it is for and a note, never a key. Members it is for see it in Settings › MCP under "From your team" and connect it in the same dialog as an admin's request; it becomes their own connection, used only in their chats with their key, and they can change only their key and write rule or disconnect. The admin sees "Template · N connected", never a key. A new address or sign-in method on the template deletes the members' keys (they connect again); deleting it deletes their connections. One database migration (333), which runs by itself; a server before this build reads such a connection as a plain personal one. In all five languages.
- Onboarding, fifth part: the first result is the company's intro deck (회사 소개서). One database migration (the checked slides of a deck and the hashes of the files given out); it runs by itself when the server starts. In all five languages.
  - Welcome step 3 now makes a designed 16:9 deck of 6–10 slides from the company intro or website, its pictures, logo and brand colour: a cover, the company in one sentence with its base facts, the customer's problems, what the company does, numbers, products, how it works, customers or a customer's words or a case, history or certifications, one full picture slide, and a contact slide. The AI fills short slots for each slide and never designs; the server lays every slide out on a 12-column grid in Pretendard, measuring every line (lines break only between words and keep room for a wider substitute font), and draws it in one of three looks chosen from the brand colour: 밝은 종이 (light paper), 어두운 (dark, the promo video's look) or 브랜드색 (the cover, numbers and contact on the brand colour).
  - Every number, customer and partner name, certification, quote and contact detail must be written in the material or the saved company profile; claims like "최고" or "No.1" only when the material says them. Pictures come only from the team's brand images folder and must be big enough for their place; a slide without a fitting picture uses its designed type-only version. Each slide's speaker notes quote where its numbers and names came from. A deck downloaded and put back into the team folder is never read as material for the next one.
  - The deck opens beside the chat by itself: page through it with the arrow keys, a swipe or a click on either side; in a narrow panel the slides stand in one scrolling column. One [받기] button gives a PowerPoint file (editable text boxes, Pretendard embedded, about 4–6 MB with pictures) and a PDF (drawn with typst, the same on every device). Both are built on the server when asked for (well under a second) and kept until the chat is deleted; the PDF is offered only where the server has typst (every Linux server bundle does). No mark of the product is put on the slides.
  - The first result is now this deck instead of the promo video, and [팀에 공유] invites the team to the "회사 소개서" skill itself, so members' runs (each for their own customers) count toward the 7-day goal. The promo video and card news skills stay installed.
  - Every Linux server bundle now also carries Pretendard 1.3.9 Regular, Bold and ExtraBold (static TTF, OFL-1.1, unchanged) in its `fonts/` folder, with the notice.
- The first-run "팀 만들기" form is in two groups under quiet headings, "팀" (team name, team language) and "내 계정 · 팀장" (내 이름, username, password), so it is clear where the team ends and the owner's account begins. Like joining, it asks for the password once, with a show-password switch, instead of a second "비밀번호 확인" field; the setup API is unchanged. In all five languages.
- The welcome step "AI 연결" (and the owner's no-AI card on Home) shows the ways at once instead of one button with "다른 방법" folded: "Claude 구독 연결" (추천), "ChatGPT 구독 연결" and "API 키로 연결" (Claude or OpenAI key), each opening the existing connect dialog on that way, and one line to the AI engines page for OpenRouter and other providers. In all five languages.
- Coming back resumes the welcome steps: an owner who left them unfinished lands on them (where they stopped) when opening the app on Home or signing in, once per browser tab; "나중에 하기" goes Home and stays there for that tab. Never for admins or members, never when the steps are finished or hidden, and a link to somewhere else (a chat, an invite) opens as before.

## 0.4.0-beta.9 — 2026-11-06

Beta channel only; stable stays on 0.3.0

**Upgrade note:** twelve database migrations (115_a_profiles, 232–234 onboarding, 240–246 team chat rooms, room AI and native push devices, 335_c_slack); they run by themselves at the first start and only add tables and columns. Update as root as before: the update timer applies it in the team's update window, or run `sudo mycel-ctl update`. Native phone push stays off until `push.gatewayUrl` (or `MYCEL_PUSH_GATEWAY`) is set; the Slack connector stays off until an owner connects it.

- Team chat: rooms for every group and for everyone, DMs, replies, mentions, files and photos, read counts and live delivery. Calling @트리나 in a room answers with the permissions of the person who asked; the answer goes to the room only when every member can see everything it could reveal, otherwise the person gets a private preview they can post. Room photos lose location and camera metadata.
- Phones: a phone type scale below 768px (nothing under 13px but badges), the 대화/파일/나 tabs, safe areas filled in the page's own colours, and an in-app question before the notification prompt.
- Push to the phone app and to browsers now reaches servers without IPv6: the addresses are tried IPv4 first.
- Slack answers keep bold next to Korean particles (sent as rich text), and links outside the team show their host.
- Profiles and a people directory: Settings › Profile now has a photo, a job title and a one-line status next to the name, and every member can see the team's names, titles, statuses and photos (never emails, roles or groups). One database migration (115); the photo is cut square in the browser, its camera data is cut out again on the server, and SVG is refused. Nobody can change another person's profile. In all five languages.
- The phone app is ready for TestFlight as "Trina AX" (`app.trina.ax`, iPhone, built on a Mac without Expo's services) and gets native push: after signing in it asks once for notifications and registers the phone with the team; the team server sends through a push gateway with generic text only, and a tap opens the right page. Phone push is off until the server operator sets the gateway (`push.gatewayUrl` in server.json or `MYCEL_PUSH_GATEWAY`; owners see which in Settings › Notifications), and switching team removes the phone from the old team. The app also has an offline / cannot-reach-team screen with retry in Korean and English, camera and microphone for attachments and voice input. People can ask the team owner to delete their account from Settings › Profile › Delete account; the owner gets a notice that opens the member, and removing that person archives their personal files for the owner as usual. Two database migrations (phone devices, deletion requests) run by themselves.
- Slack connector, off by default: people ask the AI with `@app` in a Slack channel or in a DM, and each question runs a normal chat as the person who asked. The owner makes an internal Slack app from the manifest in Admin › Slack and pastes its two tokens and the app's Client ID and Client Secret; it works through Socket Mode, so an office Mac needs no public address. Each person links their own Slack account once with "Sign in with Slack" (or, when Slack's e-mail for that account is their e-mail here, with one click on the link the app sends). In a channel the answer is shown to the asker only, with "Post to channel". One database migration; it runs by itself when the server starts. In all five languages ([docs/SLACK.md](docs/SLACK.md)).
- Onboarding for teams that are not developers, first part. Three database migrations (team invite links, onboarding notices, company profile drafts); they run by themselves when the server starts. `mycel-ctl` learned the hosted trial command, so a Linux server takes this with a root update. In all five languages.
  - No AI is no longer a dead end. When a person has no usable AI connection, the chat error and a line above the message box offer one button: the owner gets "Connect AI" (Admin › AI connections), a member whose team allows personal connections gets "Connect my AI", and admins and other members get "Tell the owner", which sends the owner one notice (asking again does not repeat it). The owner's start checklist puts the AI connection first and links straight to it.
  - Hosted trial AI credit. During a hosted trial the hosting service can give the team an OpenRouter key with a dollar cap and an end date, so the team gets answers before connecting anything. Those answers come from the app's own API engine on Claude Haiku 5.5, never from Claude Code (Anthropic's terms do not allow a key the hosting service pays for in Claude Code), and nobody has to change Admin › AI engines. Any connection the team or a person adds is used first: a team that connects only ChatGPT gets its Claude Code chats answered by Codex, with a line saying so. The server counts the spend itself, holds back what each request may cost so several people at once cannot pass the cap, treats OpenRouter's limit on the key as the backstop, ends an answer cut off at its length with a clear message instead of retrying, and stops with "The trial AI credit is used up" (or "ended"), with the same buttons. Admin › AI connections shows what is used, the cap and the end date. Self-hosted servers are not affected.
  - A marketing skill pack in one click: ad copy, blog and social drafts, campaign plan, SEO brief, competitor brief, brand review and weekly report, in Korean and English (other languages get English). An owner or admin installs it from the empty Team skills page; it also makes four team folders under shared/. Installing again only adds what is missing and never overwrites an edited skill, and a skill whose name another team skill already uses is left out and named.
  - Skills can ask for a short form instead of a blank chat (`metadata.inputs` in SKILL.md). The form opens from Team skills, from the "/" menu and from `/skills?run=<path>`, fills in what the company profile knows, and runs the skill in a new chat. A form written wrongly does not hide the skill; Team skills shows what to fix.
  - Company profile from the company website. An owner or admin enters the website; the AI reads up to four pages of that site only (on shared platforms such as Naver blog or smartstore, only pages under the address typed) and drafts a one-line intro, products and services, customers, tone and words to avoid, each with the page it came from. The person checks, removes what is wrong and saves; if the site cannot be read, pasted text or three questions do the same. Every chat then gets the profile as facts about the company, never as instructions. It is kept in `skills/company-profile.json` and can be edited later in Admin › Team settings.
  - One team invite link to paste in a chat: members only, the groups the admin chose, a number of uses (seats left by default, never above the plan), 7, 14 or 30 days, a limit on failed tries, the owner told about each join, and a ready-made message to copy. The link can be shown again (it is kept encrypted), and making a new one or revoking ends the old one. A link can name a skill, and a member who can read it lands on its form after joining. Joining now asks for name, username and password only (no second password, with a show-password switch), for single invites too.
- Onboarding for teams that are not developers, second part. Four more database migrations (team skill runs and goal notices, closed suggestions, the company website reading log, a team link's starter files and question); they run by themselves when the server starts. `mycel-ctl` learned `status --json --onboarding`, part of the same root update. In all five languages.
  - Welcome steps after creating a team: two steps with a progress bar, the company intro and then a first result (since the third part below, the company's card news). Every team gets a small general skill pack (할 일 정리, 핵심 정리, and 카드뉴스 from the third part) and a group and folder of its own named after the team (`groups/<team>/`, with a notes folder and a brand folder); the step says how many people see files dropped there and that each person's chats stay their own. A marketing team adds the marketing pack with one line above the first step. A step can be put off and picked up later, also on another device; Home shows "Continue getting started (n/2)" until the steps are done or hidden. Without a usable AI the steps show the connect button.
  - While the AI reads the company website, the step lists each page as it is read or skipped, with the reason (did not open, almost no text, outside the site), instead of a spinner.
  - A team link can carry files and a question: a member who joins lands on a new chat with the files attached and the question ready ("내 할 일 정리해 줘"), under a line saying the chat is theirs only. Files the member cannot open are left out when they join. An answer that used a team skill on team files says so in its step line ("팀 스킬 '할 일 정리' · 팀 파일 2개").
  - Skill forms can offer choices as chips and fold extra fields under "더 자세히 정하기" (`metadata.inputs`, `type: "choice"`, `advanced`). The ad copy form shows two fields, product and channel; its answer opens with one preview of the recommended option as it would look in that channel, then three options, each with its own copy button.
  - At most one suggestion under an answer, chosen by what happened: a second run of the same team skill offers to run it every week, a long report offers a PowerPoint. A closed suggestion does not come back for that person.
  - The 7-day team goal. During the team's first 7 days owners and admins see one card at the top of Home ("7일 안에 3명이 같은 스킬 써 보기 · 1/3 · 5일 남음") with one line about where the team is and one button: copy the invite message, or send the skill again to someone who joined but has not run a team skill yet (the line names at most one person and a count). Only answers that finished count; scheduled runs do not. Owners get a notice when someone runs a team skill for the first time in that week. The owner checklist moved to Admin › Home and lists only what is left; Home's frequent skills show once two different skills were used.
  - The counts for the hosting service are opt-in: `mycel-ctl status --json` gives exactly what it gave before, and the onboarding counts (days since the start, pack installed, profile saved, invite made, people who joined, answered and ran a skill; numbers and yes/no only, never a name or a skill) come only with `--onboarding`.
  - Skills have a title people read (`metadata.title` in SKILL.md, such as "광고 문구"). It shows on Team skills, in the "/" menu (which also finds a skill by it), on the skill form, in Home's frequent skills, on the invite link, in its message and on the join page, on the goal card and in the owner's notice; "/ad-copy" still runs the skill, and a question that runs a skill shows the title as a chip. A pack installed before this keeps its files: installing again counts them as unchanged, not edited, and they show the skill's name until someone adds the title line.
  - The invite step is calmer on phones: the message and one button, "Copy message" (the message holds the link); the link, QR code, uses, expiry and replacing the link are under "Manage link". Admin › Members keeps the full card, without a frame on a phone.
- Onboarding, third part: the first result is the company's own card news, and the lead's way of working becomes a team skill. Two database migrations (team skill drafts; the brand pictures, page text and colours a website reading collected); they run by themselves when the server starts. In all five languages.
  - Welcome step 2 asks for a company or team introduction ("회사 소개서나 팀 소개 자료가 있으면 놓아 주세요", up to 10 files) or nothing ("자료 없이 만들게요"). Files go into the team's brand folder (`groups/<team>/브랜드/`). Without files the server writes `회사 소개.md` there from the saved company profile and the text of the website pages it read, each part under its source, with no AI and nothing invented (one already there is kept). The step then opens a chat, "우리 회사 홍보 카드뉴스", that runs the new general skill `/card-news`: five square promo cards in one HTML artifact (cover, the problem, what we do, one number written in the material, the next step with the logo), made with the site's logo, pictures and brand colours. English teams get it as "Promo cards". It replaces the meeting notes run and the ad copy of the earlier step 2.
  - Reading the company website now also collects the brand: the logo, the pictures the pages show (also from a site builder's CDN) and the brand colours, only from what the pages themselves embed and with the web reading's network rules. PNG, JPEG or WebP, at most 8 per reading; the reading log says "로고와 이미지 n개 가져옴". The colours show as swatches on the profile review (each can be removed) and in Admin › Team settings, and every chat's company facts carry them. The pictures go into `브랜드/이미지/` when the first result starts.
  - Too few pictures is fine: the card news never makes, downloads or draws a picture. A card without a fitting photo uses a designed type-only version (the brand colour, the logo or the company name, a large line, one simple shape), never an empty photo slot, and the answer says that photos added to the brand images folder can go in next time. Headlines on the light background stay readable with a light brand colour.
  - Under that first result, "마음에 드시나요? 팀 스킬로 저장하면 다른 분들도 같은 방식으로 만들 수 있어요" [팀 스킬로 저장] makes a team skill from it: the AI drafts it in a chat without tools, only what changes each time becomes a question for teammates (for the card news: the topic and the channel), and the files it uses are named (a dated file as "the newest of its kind" in its folder, a folder of pictures as the whole folder). A card shows the title (changeable), one line, what teammates are asked and the files; nothing of that one run (people, customers, dates, amounts, contact data, links) may stay in it, or the AI is asked once to rewrite it and otherwise there is no card ("다시 정리하기" tries again). The check also knows English names, companies, US dates, dollar amounts and phone numbers. A conversation that used files not everyone on the team can open, content from the web or an outside service, or a confidential one cannot become a team skill. "팀에 공유" saves it under `skills/` as the owner or admin and opens the invite, whose link and message carry the skill.
  - A member who joins sees who made the skill above its form ("{by}님이 만든 팀 스킬이에요"), or lands on a new chat with it when it asks nothing; an answer from it says whose skill it was ("김지훈님의 팀 스킬 '견적서 쓰기' · 팀 파일 1개"). Skills from a starter pack name no maker. The general skills read a template document (a quote, a letter, a report) as a template: "이렇게 읽었어요: 견적서 양식", then its frame and the fields filled in each time.
  - "매주 이 시간에 자동으로 받을까요?" now appears only for recurring work: the weekly report, the competitor brief, the meeting tasks and skills with `recurring: weekly` in their metadata. Team skills made before this (and ad copy) no longer get it unless that line is added to their SKILL.md.
  - The English onboarding reads as English: the welcome, profile, invite, goal, team skill and starter lines were rewritten after a walk as an English team, the website example follows the language (acme.com, acme.co.id, acme.co.jp, acme.es), English names Slack or Teams where Korean names KakaoTalk (Indonesian and Spanish WhatsApp, Japanese LINE), the team skill card names a team folder as "Acme Marketing › Meeting notes", skill summaries start as a sentence, and the English skills use US spelling. A team folder with a space in its name, listed in a team skill, is no longer read as another folder.

- Onboarding: AI connection comes first. When no AI answers the owner yet, the welcome steps open with "AI 연결" (1/3): one line on why, one button "Claude 구독 연결" (the browser sign-in of AI connections: Claude's approval link and the code), and the other ways (ChatGPT, API keys, other AI engines) folded under "다른 방법". "나중에 하기" leaves the steps; the company intro and the first result wait for AI, the invite does not. A team that already has AI (a hosted trial credit, a managed engine, any working connection) sees the two steps as before. While the company website is read, the step shows only "나중에 하기" ("대화로 보기" and "직접 쓸게요" are gone); a reading that fails still offers the pasted text and the three questions. In all five languages.
  - Without AI, Home shows one connect card instead of suggestions that would fail (members: "팀장이 AI를 연결하면 바로 쓸 수 있어요"), a failed answer tells the owner what to do, and every "AI 연결하기" opens a short Claude connection dialog right where you are (the whole team, the provider's terms in one line, the password again as before).
  - The company profile read from the website is saved without showing the form first (one line on the next step offers 고치기), and the team-type line above the first step is gone (Team skills offers the marketing skills).
  - The card news opens beside the chat by itself with a one-line answer, and step lines name the team's folders ("브랜드 이미지 폴더 › 홈페이지-6.jpg") instead of raw paths.
  - Under the first result, [팀에 공유] invites the team to the card news skill itself instead of a copy, so members' runs count toward the 7-day goal; the invite shows the ready message with one "메시지 복사" button without first making a link.
  - The company profile and team skill drafts no longer show in the chat list.
- Onboarding fixes from a walk with a large company's website:
  - The brand colour taken from a logo is the logo's own colour: a wide wordmark of thin letters on white no longer gives a faded blend of the letters and the white.
  - `회사 소개.md` quotes each website page's own words, line by line: no site menu, footer or cookie bar, no empty quote lines, and at most about 2,000 characters a page. Pages written with Windows line breaks no longer give the AI's web reading runs of blank lines either.
  - The card news answer mentions adding photos only when one of the first three cards was made without one, and names that card.
  - The invite message names the team the link leads to, and an owner who is still alone reads "여기 올린 파일은 지금은 나만 봐요 · 팀원을 초대하면 {팀} 모두가 봐요" instead of "{팀} 1명만 봐요".
  - Members see a skill's title and description on Team skills, without its folder path, and a run from a skill's form shows in the chat as the skill and a quiet line of the answers instead of a message they seem to have typed.
  - "이 방식대로 팀 스킬로 만들까요?" [팀 스킬로 만들기] is back: under an owner's or admin's answer that used team files and no team skill (and could become one: not confidential, nothing from the web or an outside service, files the whole team can open), it opens the team skill card, then the invite with the new skill.
- Onboarding, fourth part: the first result is the company's own promo video with music. One database migration (the checked slots of a promo video and its MP4 state); it runs by itself when the server starts.
  - Welcome step 3 makes an 18.5-second vertical (4:5) video from the company intro or website, its pictures, logo and brand colour, in five beats on a 120 BPM grid: the customer's before → after (struck through and slammed in, or swapped in place), the promise over a brand picture, what the company does on its own picture in a floating window, one or two numbers counting into the value written in the material (or the most concrete fact), and an end card with the logo, the site's real offer and its address. Every beat has a camera move, layers of ground, picture, type and light, and seams that zoom through, push or blur instead of fading. The AI fills the text and picks the pictures; the server checks that every line fits, every number on screen is written in the material, pictures come from the team's brand images folder only, and chooses among three looks that fit the brand colour (임팩트, 에디토리얼, 모노). A beat without a fitting picture is made with type and light, never an empty frame or a made-up picture.
  - Music and sound effects (CC0 and public domain only, listed in `resources/promo-video/audio/SOURCES.md`). In the chat the video plays muted on a loop with play, pause and a sound button; with reduced motion the five beats show as stills. On a development Mac with the HyperFrames CLI installed (`bun scripts/promo-video/install-hyperframes.ts`) the panel also offers "영상 파일로 받기" (MP4, with sound). Linux team servers do not offer the MP4 yet.
  - The first result is now this video instead of the card news: it opens beside the chat by itself with a one-line answer, and [팀에 공유] invites the team to the "홍보 영상" skill itself, so members' runs count toward the 7-day goal. The card news skill stays installed for every team.
  - The promo video uses the Pretendard font (OFL-1.1), shipped unchanged with its notice.
- The AI and its sub-agents now answer a file the server reports as "not found or no access" with "I could not find it in the files you can open", without saying or guessing that it exists or that permission is the reason (the server already gives the same answer for a missing file and a closed one, on purpose). Read-only refusals are still reported plainly. On every server.
- `bun run demo:company`, next to `bun run demo`: a development demo of a fictional company, with real AI from the Mac's Claude Code login. The company is a content folder (`--content <dir>` or `MYCEL_DEMO_CONTENT`; format in `scripts/demo/company/README.md`, checked before anything starts); without one it runs the open example, Acme (six people, two teams and a CEO, a closed budget folder). The viewer starts as the new hire and can ask the same question as the team lead and the CEO; showcase prompts replay answers the real AI produced once, with the files made again by the product's tools, and each person has past chats made the same way; the person switch and the replays exist only on this demo server (`MYCEL_DEMO`, refused in production, on the hosted service and in office mode). It can be opened from every network the Mac is on (`--local`: this Mac only). `bun run demo:trina` runs the 트리나 company, whose content is kept outside this repository: it needs `--content <dir>` and says where the content is when it is missing. / 가상 회사의 신입으로 써 보는 데모(기본은 공개 예시 회사 Acme).
- AI connections: a Claude subscription connects from the browser. "Claude 구독 연결" shows Claude's approval link, the person pastes back the code Claude shows, and the server's own Claude Code (`claude setup-token`, run in the engine sandbox on a terminal of its own) makes the token; the terminal paste stays as a folded fallback.
- 대화방 (team chat rooms), first version, without AI: every group gets a room of its members, the team gets one room for everyone, and anyone can open a DM with a teammate from the people list. Messages show how many have not read them yet (as in KakaoTalk), can quote a message, mention a member with @, carry files and images, and can be edited or deleted by their writer; a DM message or a mention is a notice and, with push on, a push that says only that something came. Only the people in a room can read it, the owner and admins included; someone who joins later sees it from then on, and room files are kept outside the team folder. On phones the app has three tabs, 대화 · 파일 · 나, and the 대화 list starts with 트리나 (the AI chat). Two database migrations; they run by themselves when the server starts. In all five languages.
- 트리나 in 대화방: type `@트리나` (first in the @ list) in any room to ask the AI. It answers with the asker's own access, engine and usage, in a conversation of the asker's own for that room, and gets the room's recent messages as other people's words, never as instructions. Only the asker sees its steps and can stop it; the others see that it is answering. The answer goes to the room only when everyone in the room may open every file it used and it used nothing from the web or a connected service; otherwise only the asker sees it, with the folders some members cannot open, and one button posts it (leaving out the files they cannot open). No memories in rooms. One database migration (242); it runs by itself when the server starts. In all five languages.

## 0.4.0-beta.8 — 2026-11-05

Beta channel only; stable stays on 0.3.0

**Upgrade note:** no database migration. Update as root as before: the update timer applies it in the team's update window, or run `sudo mycel-ctl update`. `mycel-ctl` changed, so this is a root update. A server that the broken update left without a working AI launcher heals when this version is installed.

- Hosted servers: the AI launcher could not start after an update. `mycel-ctl update` ran with a strict file mask (umask 077) and made the new version's folders readable only by the server account, so the launcher (another account) was refused with "permission denied", the service stopped after 5 tries and chats stayed in the queue. `mycel-ctl` now sets the folder and file modes itself (folders 755, files 755 or 644) whatever the mask. When the version is already on the server, an update also repairs the modes of its folders and files.

## 0.4.0-beta.7 — 2026-11-05

[Published release and notes](https://github.com/trina-app/trina-ax/releases/tag/v0.4.0-beta.7) · beta channel only; stable stays on 0.3.0

**Upgrade note:** no database migration. It installs as a root update from every earlier version: the update timer applies it in the team's update window, or run `sudo mycel-ctl update`. No root file changed since 0.4.0-beta.6, but the feed's root check also compares with stable 0.3.0, so it marks a root update. Version impact: patch (a bug fix).

- Filling Hangul forms: when added table rows would no longer fit on the page of a table that cannot continue on the next one (most one-page statutory forms), the AI no longer hands over a form whose last lines Hangul cuts off; nothing is saved, and it says how many rows fit and that the rest can go on a separate sheet (별지).

## 0.4.0-beta.6 — 2026-11-05

Same app as 0.4.0-beta.5, which was never published: its release test failed on the GitHub Linux runners.

**Upgrade note:** database migration 222 (state of dev environment runs). The server applies it at start; nothing to do. From 0.4.0-beta.4 this is an app update. From 0.4.0-beta.2 or older it is a root update, as in 0.4.0-beta.3. The srt dev environment is off until an administrator installs it (`scripts/dev-env/srt/install-srt.sh`) and turns it on, so a server that does not use it sees no change.

- Dev environment on srt (beta, off by default): the AI's `box_shell` commands can run on the team server inside srt in place of the podman box. A run can read the folders the person can read, can write only the project folder and the person's own folder, and reaches only GitHub, npm, PyPI and the sites the team adds. Admin › Team settings has a new "Dev environment" section for on/off, who may use it, network, limits and the warning consent. The project modal now keeps the people chosen by name. In all five languages. Design: docs/research/srt-dev-env.md.
- UX audit fixes, batch 2: medium fixes in Admin (engines, usage, activity, members, plugins), Files (list, selection bar, PDF preview), Chat (waiting state, retry, images, phone header), Scheduled and search, Settings and sign-in. Search fields keep the browser clear button where the app has none.
- CI: the srt dev environment attack suite runs on a host like a Debian 13 team server (the Ubuntu 24.04 runners restrict user namespaces with AppArmor, so bwrap could not start as the server account), and it stops when a plain command cannot run through srt, so a broken host cannot pass the attacks. The sandbox settings did not change.

## 0.4.0-beta.4 — 2026-11-04

Same app as 0.4.0-beta.3, which could not be published because GitHub had no macOS runner for the desktop build.

- Release: the Linux server release is published even when the Mac or Windows desktop build fails or gets no runner. The office feed then keeps the previous release, and the GitHub Release says which downloads are missing.

## 0.4.0-beta.3 — 2026-11-04

[Published release and notes](https://github.com/trina-app/trina-ax/releases/tag/v0.4.0-beta.3) · beta channel only; stable stays on 0.3.0

**Upgrade note:** no database migration. The root part changed (`mycel-ctl` for the staged roll-out, `mycel-launcher` for the reaper log), so a Linux server takes this with a root update: the update timer applies it in the team's update window, or run `sudo mycel-ctl update`. Servers on 0.4.0-beta.2 or older ignore the stages of the staged roll-out until they run this version. Nothing for a team to do. Version impact: minor over 0.3.0 (0.x rule, docs/RELEASE-CI.md "Version numbers"), still in the 0.4.0 beta.

- Small UI fixes from a quick UX check. Admin › Members shows each member as a card on a phone, with the row actions in a "…" menu. Settings › Security & devices names each device in short words ("Chrome on macOS"). The sidebar, the phone menu and the search palette have an Inbox with the unread count, and Settings › Notifications shows the inbox before the preferences. Error messages are plain words, not "HTTP 503"; Admin › System shows one message when no card loaded, and Admin › Home says "Could not check everything" when a source did not load. Wide chat tables and tab rows fade at the edge that has more content. Projects cards show 2 plain lines; Scheduled cards say the last result in words; the Files toolbar keeps Upload in view with the preview open. Focus goes back to the member menu after its dialog closes, and screen readers read the Inbox count in words. In all five languages.
- Docker: a Docker image and a compose file to run a self-hosted server (`docker/`, docs/INSTALL-DOCKER.md). It is new and less tested than the Linux install, and it has no per-turn cgroup limits and no root updates. The Linux install on a dedicated VM stays the reference for a production team.
- Linux: the launcher now writes a log line (pid, ppid, session and command name) for each orphan process of the engine account that it stops. Before, it stopped them without a log line.
- Hosted service only (no change for a self-hosted server): a build of the EC2 image (`scripts/image/build-ami.sh`), `install-linux.sh --image-mode` for template builds, and `mycel-link`, the agent that the hosted service uses to run commands on an EC2 team VM. `mycel-link` is only in the EC2 image, not in the Linux kit.
- Admin: a new version of a self-hosted server now shows on Admin > Home for the owner ("Version 0.3.0 · installs automatically tomorrow at 03:00–05:00"), not only inside Admin > System. The server also looks at the release feed again when the last check is more than an hour old (it was a day) and waits a few seconds for the answer, so Admin > System no longer says "up to date" for a day after a release. In all five languages.
- Updates need nothing from the team. Admin > Home says when an automatic update installs (the next opening of the update window, in the team time zone), says the update timer does it when a release needs root, and with automatic updates off has an "Install now" button that asks what Admin > System asks. What an update leaves for an admin to do now shows on Admin > Home with a button and "Not needed" (kept for the team); the first is turning on the HWP plugin, which Hangul writing and filling need since 0.4.0-beta.2. The HWP to-do shows only on a server that was updated across 0.4.0-beta.2 and whose team language is Korean. Home does not promise an automatic install the server will hold back (not this server's turn, a failed earlier try, a window that never opens): it says so and offers the button. Linux self-hosted servers only. In all five languages.
- New stable releases now reach servers in stages instead of all on the same night: about 10% of Linux servers and office Macs take a release by themselves on the first day after it is published, about 50% on the second and all of them from the third, so a bad release reaches few teams. Each server works out its turn from its install id and the release (the app's own updates, the root update timer and office Macs keep the same rule), and installs in its update window once its turn has come; a server whose install id cannot be read is in the last wave, never left out. Nothing waits on the beta channel, for an urgent release, or when an administrator updates by hand (Admin › System › Update, `sudo mycel-ctl update`, the office's "Update now"). While a server waits, Admin › Home says when it installs ("Version 0.5.0 · installs automatically tomorrow at 03:00–05:00, when it is this server's turn", with Install now), `sudo mycel-ctl status` says when its turn comes ("this server's turn comes at …", also `updates.rollout` in `status --detail --json`), the update status API has `turnAt`, and `teamax update status` shows the Mac's turn. Office Macs say it on the new-version banner and in Admin › System's update card ("이 맥 차례인 11월 6일 03:00-05:00(Asia/Seoul)에 자동으로 설치돼요"). Releases published a few hours apart continue the first one's stages, so a burst still reaches every server within two days of its first release. A roll-out a server cannot read never stops an update. Servers on 0.4.0-beta.2 or older ignore the stages and update as before. Maintainers: a line `Rollout: all` in the tag message (or the workflow's `rollout` input) publishes a release to every server at once, for example a fix for a bad release (docs/RELEASE-CI.md "단계적 배포").

## 0.4.0-beta.2 — 2026-11-04

[Published release and notes](https://github.com/trina-app/trina-ax/releases/tag/v0.4.0-beta.2) · beta channel only; stable stays on 0.3.0

**Upgrade note (breaks something 0.3.0 did):** in 0.3.0 and 0.4.0-beta.1 every server's AI could make .hwpx files and fill in .hwp and .hwpx forms. From this version it can only while the HWP plugin is installed and on. A team that uses Hangul writing should have an owner or admin open Admin › Plugins, install "HWP Preview" from the official list and turn it on for the company, after the update (or before it, if it is already installed but off). A self-installed Debian 12 server cannot install the plugin (glibc 2.36), so it loses Hangul writing and filling; reading Hangul documents and downloading them stay. No database migration. It installs as a root update from 0.3.0 and from 0.4.0-beta.1 alike: the update timer applies it in the team's update window, or run `sudo mycel-ctl update`. From 0.4.0-beta.1 no root file changes, but the feed's root check also compares with stable 0.3.0, so it marks a root update. The notes shown in the app, which are signed and cannot change, say "From 0.4.0-beta.1 this is an app update"; that is wrong. Version impact: minor over 0.3.0 (0.x rule, docs/RELEASE-CI.md "Version numbers"), with this note saying what breaks.

- Hangul documents come with the HWP plugin: the AI makes new .hwpx files (make_file) and fills in existing .hwp and .hwpx forms (edit_hwp) only while an admin has installed "HWP Preview" in Admin › Plugins and turned it on for the company. Without it, the AI is not offered edit_hwp, make_file does not mention .hwpx, and a request for one saves nothing and says that an admin can turn the plugin on. Writing does not need the preview itself: a server where the renderer cannot run, or a company that did not allow the AI to see page images, still writes and fills Hangul documents while the plugin is on. Turning the plugin on or off, updating, rolling it back or removing it takes effect at once, without a restart: a chat that goes on gets the change with its next message (one that began with the plugin on may still mention Hangul documents after it is turned off, but can no longer make or fill them). Admin › Plugins says so on the HWP plugin's card and in its install prompt, in all five languages; the plugin package itself did not change. Every team still reads the text of .hwp and .hwpx files, searches them and downloads the original, and write and edit still refuse to save them as plain text.
- Admin › System no longer shows a line about HWP preview above Storage. It was left from before plugins, was shown to every admin whatever language the team uses, and sat outside any card. Admin › Plugins says what the HWP plugin does and, on a server where it cannot run (Debian 12), why.

## 0.4.0-beta.1 — 2026-11-04

[Published release and notes](https://github.com/trina-app/trina-ax/releases/tag/v0.4.0-beta.1) · beta channel only; stable stays on 0.3.0

**Upgrade note:** two database migrations (who wrote a project's instructions, and a project whose folder is in the trash); they run by themselves when the server starts, and 0.3.0 can still read the database. The root part changed (`mycel-ctl`, `mycel-boxd`), so a Linux server takes this with a root update: the update timer applies it in the team's update window, or run `sudo mycel-ctl update`. Taking a backup first is still a good habit (Admin > System > Backups > Back up now). Version impact: minor (new features and visible changes; old links and clients keep working), per `docs/RELEASE-CI.md` "Version numbers".

- Admin › AI engines is in two tabs now, like Claude's settings. **Providers** is one quiet row per engine (Claude Code, Codex, API): its state as a small chip (Connected, Sign-in needed, Not installed, Experimental), one dim line with how it signs in, its models and when it was checked, what is missing in one line under it, and on the right Check again and its on/off switch. The engine's name opens the rest in place: the longer explanation, the version and every model, and for the owner the API engine's address, model and key (`?engine=api` opens that row). **Team provider settings** (`?tab=team`) is rows with the words on the left and the control on the right: the team's default engine and model and answers at the same time, who can pick the engine and the switch to Codex when Claude hits its limit, and usage limits. A section shows Save and Cancel only once something in it changed. Nothing was removed and nothing works differently. In all five languages.
- Admin › Activity log is simpler to read and use. Its title and filters stay at the top while the list scrolls. The filters are one compact row: the kind as chips (All, Sign-ins, Admin, Files, AI tools), the person, the time (any time, today, the last 7 or 30 days, or a custom range in place of the two date fields) and one search box for people, actions and items; a folder path in it ("finance/2026") finds the file changes under that folder, as the old Path field did. The filters are kept in the page's address, so a filtered view reloads and can be shared, and Clear resets them. The list is grouped by day under labels that stay in view (Today, Yesterday, Mon, Nov 2), and each row reads as a sentence: the time, a small icon for the kind, "Olga created an invite link · member", "Bob · AI used read · read hr/salaries.xlsx" with Denied when the AI was refused. The same event by the same person close together is one row ("Bob signed in · 5 times"). A click on a row shows its details: the exact time of each, who (with their id), the action's id, the item, the IP address and the rest of what was recorded. On a phone the kind stays in view and the rest is behind Filters. Nothing changes on the server. In all five languages.
- Search (⌘K / Ctrl+K) now looks like Claude's and finds more than chats. Tabs at the top choose what to look for: everything, chats, projects, files, skills, scheduled tasks or MCP. With nothing typed it shows what needs attention first (an answer still running or waiting, a scheduled task to confirm again, an MCP connection to sign in to again), then recent chats and projects, then actions: new chat, new project, new scheduled task, add MCP and open Files. Typed text is marked in the results. ↑ ↓ move, Enter opens, ← → at the ends of the text change the tab, Alt+Enter (⌥↵ on a Mac) opens a chat's menu (rename, delete), and Esc closes; the hints are at the bottom. Files are still searched by content on the Files screen, only when you choose that row. On a phone, Search fills the screen.
- Scheduled tasks moved from Settings to the sidebar ("Scheduled", after Projects), with pages like Claude's. The list is a grid of quiet cards (one column on a phone): the name, two lines of what it does, and a dim line such as "Paused · Weekdays (Mon–Fri) at 09:00", with a small dot for how the last run went; a search finds a task by name, and "New task" either starts the sentence in a new chat (the AI makes the task on its card, as before) or opens the template form. A task's page has its name in the top line (Edit and Delete in its menu), Run now and an on/off switch ("Check again" for a task made without its card), its last run linked to its chat, and beside it the instruction, when it repeats, the project or folders it runs with and the next and last run. A finished or expired one-time task keeps its switch, turned off and saying why. `/settings/schedules` and the banner and notification links now open `/scheduled`. Nothing changed in how tasks run, and the API is the same; the AI now tells people to find their tasks under Scheduled tasks in the sidebar. In all five languages.
- Sidebar, account menu and Settings, like Claude's: the sidebar is 280 px wide with quieter 32 px rows and smaller icons. The profile line at the bottom is one compact line, and pressing it opens a menu above it: who is signed in, Settings, Usage (admins: Admin › Usage), Appearance (light, dark or the system's, chosen right there), Deleted chats (moved there from the bottom of the sidebar) and Sign out; arrow keys, Home, End and Esc work in it. Settings is now a dialog over the page it was opened from, with a search that filters its sections, the sections in groups on the left and the open one on the right; Profile is laid out as rows with the setting on the left and its control on the right. `/settings/...` addresses still work (over Home when opened directly), Esc or ✕ goes back to the page, the keyboard stays inside the dialog and comes back to where it was. On a phone Settings fills the screen: the list, then a section with a way back. Admin stays its own page. Pages such as Team skills, Projects and Trash now keep their content in a column centred beside the sidebar on wide windows. A title row that stays at the top is filled with the surface it sits on (the Settings dialog's panel, or the page), so it shows no box of its own. A confirm or edit dialog opened from Settings sits above it, and Esc in a field that uses Esc itself (editing a memory) no longer closes Settings. The sidebar's rows are roomier (36 px, inset hover). "Recents ▾" folds the list away, and a button beside it opens filter and sort, like Claude's: Status (active, answering or waiting for you, in a project or not, confidential), Last activity (today, 7 or 30 days), Group by (none, date, project) and Sort by (last activity, date created, title), each a submenu with the keyboard too; what is not at its default shows as a dot and a dim label with Reset, and all of it is remembered in this browser. Resting the pointer on a recent chat shows a quick view beside the sidebar (the start of the last answer or its first file, the title and when); not on touch screens. The app is now exactly the window: long pages no longer scroll the whole app.
- Usage: everyone now has their own usage in Settings › Usage, opened from Usage in the account menu, like Claude's. It shows how full the usage limits that apply to you are (your own, your group's, the team's), each with a thin bar, "N% used" and when it resets, and a plain sentence on top ("Limit reached. You can use the AI again from …" once a blocking limit is used up); then your answers per engine and model today, this week or this month, and your answers, tokens and estimated cost. Only your own: a group's or the team's limit shows how full it is, not who used it. Admin › Usage keeps everyone's, now calmer: a title row with Download CSV that stays at the top, the grouping and dates on one line, quiet rows with thin share bars, and the long notes on one line with "More". New: `GET /api/me/usage` (signed-in people, their own usage only). In all five languages.
- Hosted team VMs with the dev environment: `scripts/dev-env/install-trial.sh` no longer stops on every hosted server. A Trina Cloud team has its own VM, so the box can run there when the team's plan includes it: a root-owned `root/dev-env.json` with the plan's box caps (`maxRunning`, `cpus`, `memoryMiB`, `pids`, `diskGiB`, and `network` `open` or `offline`). Without that file the script still stops. The VM must have the plan's memory plus the box's. `mycel-boxd` has a new `--limits-file`: it refuses a box over the file's caps, and only `offline` unless the file allows `open`. With the flag and no file, boxes are not available. A self-hosted server does not change.
- Dev environment: Node in the box now sees the CPUs the box has. Before, the box got a lower CPU weight, and Node 20 (libuv 1.50) multiplied its CPU count by that weight, so `os.availableParallelism()` was 1 with 3 CPUs and tools such as Remotion ran on one CPU. The weight did not give the team server more CPU either, so it is gone. Each box is made again once at its next start; its files and caches stay.
- Dev environment (experiment): box state is now on the disk of `/var/lib/mycel`, the team data disk on a VM with one, and no longer on the OS disk. `install-trial.sh` runs `mycel-boxd` with `--state-dir /var/lib/mycel/boxd` (box volumes) and puts the podman storage of the box account in `/var/lib/mycel/boxd-storage`. On a test VM the box added about 2.7 GiB to a 12 GiB OS disk, outside the plan storage and outside the disk watchdog. A re-run of `install-trial.sh` moves an older install: it stops `mycel-boxd` and every box, copies the box volumes, builds the image again and then removes the old folders. The unit now has `RequiresMountsFor`, so boxd waits for the data disk. The disk watchdog now reads the disk of the team folder, of the box volumes and of the podman storage, and the disk nearest its floor counts. `mycel-ctl update` does not move anything: run `install-trial.sh` again after the update. `uninstall-trial.sh` removes both the old and the new paths.
- External services is now called MCP, in Settings, in Admin, on Admin Home, in the activity log, notices and help texts and in what the AI tells you when a connection needs fixing ("Settings > MCP"). The addresses stay `/settings/integrations` and `/admin/integrations`, so old links still open it, and plain API connections are still added there. Page title rows now stay at the top while the page scrolls: MCP (title, Connected / Discover, search, Add), Team skills, Projects, a project's top line and Trash, with a hairline under a title row once the page has scrolled. Settings keeps its title and menu in place and scrolls only the page beside them, like Admin. A control that gets keyboard focus is never hidden under a row that stays at the top.
- Docs: a documentation site for developers and AI agents, in the style of Claude's docs (`docs/site/`, `bun run docs:build`, preview with `bun run docs:serve`). It has an intro, a quickstart, guides for using and running Trina AX, a developer guide, pages for AI coding agents and runtime agents (including the Team AX MCP connector and the SKILL.md format), an HTTP API reference generated from the API contract, a CLI reference, search, light and dark themes, and `llms.txt`, `llms-full.txt` and a Markdown copy of every page. Nothing in the app changed.
- Projects are folders with instructions now. In Files, a folder's menu has "Make it a project" (where you can write) or "Open project", and project folders show the project icon; the project dialog then has the folder chosen and its name filled in, an empty name takes the folder's name, and "Existing folder" saves only once a folder is chosen (it no longer starts on the whole team folder). A folder holds one project: making a second project on it, an admin pointing another project at it, sharing a copy or moving a folder onto a path a project still names is refused with the project's name, and "Open project" next to the message (projects that already shared a folder stay as they are). A shared copy no longer lands on the folder of an older project whose folder is in the trash. A project on a folder that was not admin-only yet needs write access to the folder it is in. The chat header's project before the title is the project's own name, follows a rename, and is left out for a project you can no longer see; the title menu has "Move to project…" ("Change project…") to move the chat into a project you can see or out of it, on phones too. Fixed: when an admin put a restricted folder in the trash, its project's name and instructions showed to everyone who could read the folder above (in the list, on its page, in their AI); while a project's folder is in the trash, or after it was purged from there, only admins and the owner see the project (their project page, its edit dialog and the chat header say so), no chat gets it or can be put in it, nobody uses its dev environment, and editing it keeps it so until an admin restores the folder or chooses one that is there (the dialog then says how many people will see it); when the folder is restored, also under a "(restored)" name, the project follows it. Remember protection: a project's instructions count as outside content for everyone except the one person who wrote every version, so after an admin edits your instructions the AI asks before it remembers (existing projects use the audit log, and count as edited when it cannot tell). The Korean text of a new team project folder no longer says admins cannot see it. In all five languages.

## 0.3.0 — 2026-11-04

[Published release and notes](https://github.com/trina-app/trina-ax/releases/tag/v0.3.0)

**Upgrade note:** no database migration. The root part is the same as 0.2.21 (the feed says `rootSince: 0.2.21`), so a server on 0.2.21 takes this as an app update. A server still on 0.2.19 or older needs one root update, because 0.2.21 added a root file: run `sudo mycel-ctl update`, or let the update timer do it in the update window. The tag's release notes say "0.2.19 or newer takes this as an app update"; that is right only from 0.2.21. Taking a backup first is still a good habit (Admin > System > Backups > Back up now).

- Chat: the chat screen now looks like Claude's, with less around the words. An answer is plain text with no picture beside it. Copy, retry and the other actions stay in view under the newest answer, and show on older ones when you point at them or reach them with Tab; on a phone or tablet they always show. A file sent with a message is a small card above it, with its name, its type (TXT, PDF) and its size. The chat's name at the top opens Rename and Delete, after the name of its project when it is in one. The message box is like Claude's too, in a chat, on the new-chat page and on a project's page: one line that grows, with a quiet send key inside it on the right (Stop while an answer runs), and under it attach (+) and voice on the left, the engine on the right, and the "AI can make mistakes" note between them. In a chat that has started it says "Reply". Nothing else changed in how the chat works. In all five languages.
- Admin › Folder permissions › By person: "View or change rules" now opens the same edit panel as By folder, with the folder chosen in the tree; closing or saving it goes back to the person, the focus on that row ("Back to By person" says so; a folder that is not there offers the same way back). The old table of rules in a dialog is gone. With changes not saved, the By folder / By person tabs, the admin menu and other links in the app ask before dropping them, as closing the panel does. For skills and its folders, the tree and the panel say who can change the team's skills ("Who can edit: the owner and admins, Sales: 3 people, Bob"), and a folder inside skills says who can edit in skills but not there. Admin › Members: the "Manages team skills" switch now shows on, and why, for a person whom a group's rule ("On through the group Sales") or everyone's ("On because skills is open to everyone") already lets change skills; it is not switched there, with a link to Folder permissions. Turning off a person's own rule says when another rule still lets them edit. Turning off the last skill manager of skills keeps an "admins: write" rule there (skills always keeps a rule; nobody's access changes). A connected folder no longer keeps "everyone reads and writes" when it was clicked before the panel knew the folder was connected. What anyone may open is unchanged. In all five languages.
- Projects: a project's page now looks like Claude's, with much less on it. The top line says where you are, and Edit, Delete and Share are in a menu on the project's name. Under the title is the first paragraph of the instructions. A box starts a chat in the project right there, and your chats in it are one line each with their date. Dev environment, Instructions and Files are one line each beside them, with their state (stopped or running, how many files), and a click opens the rest in place: the box, the preview port, the activity, the instructions and the file list. Something typed in the box is the same draft as in "New chat in this project". On a phone the box comes first, then these lines, then the chats. In all five languages.
- Admin › Folder permissions › By folder: a folder's rules now change in a panel beside the tree ("Change rules"; the whole screen on a phone) instead of a table of rules in a dialog. It asks one thing first, the basic access for everyone not named: follow the folder above (it says what that gives now, for example "Now from shared: Everyone · Read & write"), only the people named, everyone reads, or everyone reads and writes. The top of the team folder has no folder above, and a connected folder, a one-way copy, does not offer "everyone reads and writes". Then people and groups, found by name in one search, each with read, read and write, or no access, so "everyone reads, but not Bob" stays possible; switched-off people say so. While you edit, the server works out what saving would change, with the same permission function that decides access: who loses access first (read and write → read counts too), who gains, and the folders below with rules of their own that change with it, plus warnings when members' AI would lose the team's files or instructions, when everyone could change the team's skills, or when the top of the team folder opens to everyone. The Save button says it: "Save (2 people lose access)". Old "All members" and "All admins" rules are saved as the "Everyone" rule that means the same (the admins rule never had an effect), and the panel says nobody's access changes. If another admin, or the Members screen's skill manager switch, changed the folder's rules while you edited, the save is refused, the panel says so, keeps your changes and works the result out again against their rules. Leaving with changes not saved asks first. "Follow the folder above" with nobody named saves the folder with no rules; "Remove all rules" is now only for cleaning up rows of folders that are gone or hidden, and empty rows no project uses. New: `POST /api/admin/access/edit-preview` (admins and the owner; nothing is saved), `Folder.rulesHash`, and an optional `expectedHash` on `PUT /api/admin/folders/:id/rules` and `POST /api/admin/folders` (409 `rules_changed` when the rules are not those any more). What anyone may open is unchanged. In all five languages.
- Admin › Folder permissions › By folder is a tree of the team folder now, read from the top. Before, it listed only the folders with rules, flat, with their rules as badges: no folder above them, nothing on folders without rules, and not that the same folder comes from different rules for different people. Each row is one folder with who can open it ("Sales: 3 people · Read & write, Everyone · Read", "Owner and admins only") and where that comes from ("This folder's rules", "From shared", or by whom when it differs). Folders without rules of their own are dimmed as inherited, a lock marks a folder only part of the team can open, and a folder that only the owner and admins open by no rule at all just says so. A warning shows only for risks the server works out: a folder every member can change, the same under skills (the instructions every AI of the team follows), and a rule for everyone on the top of the team folder, which opens group and project folders to people outside them. The tree opens on the top folders and the way down to folders with rules, group, project and connected folders; other folders open one level at a time, and long levels come in pages ("N more"), so nothing is cut off. users/ is one row (how many personal folders, only their owner sees each, nobody can change that) and is never listed. Choose a folder to see its rules and each person's access and why, in the same words as By person, and to change its rules there or "Remove all rules" (the folder itself stays). "Only folders with rules" shows just those folders and the way to them, all open, a page at a time, to find a deep one. Rules of folders that are no longer on disk (or sit below a folder that became a link), or whose names the tree never shows, are folded under the tree, to edit or remove. A team folder that serves `Skills/` is shown as the team skills. The keyboard follows the tree pattern: one Tab stop, the arrow keys move, open and close, typing jumps to a name, Enter chooses. On a phone the chosen folder takes the screen, with a way back to its row. A folder in the address (`?folder=`) is looked up, also past the first page of its level and while another page of it is on its way, and opened and chosen; one that is not there says so, with a way back to the tree, and the By folder tab or the menu link always goes back to the tree. New: `GET /api/admin/access/tree` (admins and the owner only), one level with who sees each folder, from where, and its risks (`rules=1`: the folders with rules), never through a link on disk; with 2000 people and 2500 folders with rules, one level answers in about 10 ms and the opened top in about 100 ms. What anyone may open is unchanged. In all five languages.
- The tabs of Folder permissions, Files, Team skills and External services now name the part of the page they show, for screen readers. By person, narrowed to users, now says the person's own folder is there, and for someone new the top of the team folder is "on the way to the personal folder they will have once they join".
- Chats: ask the AI to connect a service (Higgsfield, Notion, GitHub, any MCP server or HTTP API, or your Google Drive) and it finds the service's address, fills in the connection and shows a card. You paste the key into the card or sign in; the key never goes to the AI, and nothing is connected until you press the card's button. The card shows where the key goes and, for an HTTP API, the notes saved with it. Before, a connection was made only in Settings.
- Message box, answers to the AI, edited and queued messages: text that looks like a key or password is held with a warning before it is sent. Before, a pasted key went to the AI provider and stayed in the chat.
- Dropdowns: the arrow now sits a little in from the right edge, on every dropdown and in both themes. Before, the browser drew it on the very edge, touching the border.
- Admin › Folder permissions has a "By person" view: choose a person and see every team folder they and their AI can see, with read and write, and why, in words: a rule on that folder for whom, a rule inherited from which folder above, the rules there and above that are not for this person, "no rule, so only the owner and admins can open it", or a folder whose name only shows because a folder below it can be seen (with what decided the folder itself, so an explicit "No access" stays in sight). One sentence on top counts the folders they see and can write to, and names the folder when the list is narrowed to one. Folders they cannot see are folded under "N folders they cannot see". Folders are grouped as team, group, project, connected, skills and their own personal folder. A member who can write in skills, and so changes what every AI of the team follows, is marked. "Someone new" shows the same for a role and groups before you invite anyone, and a disabled person shows what they would get if enabled again. For the owner and admins one line says that rules change nothing for them. Nothing changes on this screen: a folder's rule editor opens from its row, and for a folder that is not on disk it says so and makes nothing. Admin › Members has "Folders they see" on each person, and the invite and add-member dialogs fold out "Folders this person will see", whose full view opens in a new tab so the invite being written stays. The choice is kept in the address, so links and Back work; a folder in the address that is no path says so and keeps a way out, and the folder view starts fresh. Long lists come in pages, and saving rules after opening more pages keeps them. The view switch of Folder permissions, Files, Team skills and External services now follows the tabs keyboard pattern: one Tab stop, the arrow keys, Home and End choose. The answers come from the server's permission function itself (`GET /api/admin/access/people/:id`, `POST /api/admin/access/new-person`, admins and the owner only), so the screen says what the server does; nothing under other people's personal folders is shown. Fixed on the server: with a rule on the top of the team folder, "someone new" failed (it named a personal folder the person does not have yet); what anyone may open is unchanged. An answer the screen cannot read is said in words, not as developer text. In all five languages.
- Hangul documents: the AI can now fill in an existing .hwp or .hwpx (a company form, a statutory form, a template with blanks) with the new edit_hwp tool, and the result stays in the same format. It fills placeholders such as {{이름}} and empty table cells, replaces exact text in a paragraph, and adds table rows shaped like an existing row. Only those parts change: fonts, styles, tables, pictures and everything else in the file stay byte for byte (in a .hwp, every stream but the changed body section). The result is a new version of the same file (the previous one stays in the file history, the change is yours) or, when asked, a new file; if someone else saved the file in the meantime, a conflict copy. When a change cannot be applied exactly (text not found, found more than once, across differently formatted text, a field or a control, a merged cell in the way), nothing is saved and the AI is told why. Documents with macros (scripts), OLE objects, a signature, links to local files or network shares, distribution-only (배포용), password-protected or change-tracking .hwp documents are not changed. Each result is read back before it is saved. Open important documents in Hangul before you send them.
- Hangul documents: the AI can now make a new .hwpx file with make_file, from Markdown or a CSV. Headings become Hangul outline levels (개요 1-6), and paragraphs, bold, italic, bullet and numbered lists, simple tables and page breaks come along. A team can keep its own template .hwpx in `skills/` for its fonts, paragraph styles and page setup. A document with images, links, footnotes, code or anything else a .hwpx cannot hold yet is not saved, and the AI is told what to leave out. Each file is read back before it is saved and must give the document's text and headings. This runs on every server, Debian 12 included, with no plugin. The AI still cannot write a .hwp or .hwpx as plain text, and a .hwp cannot be made. Hangul's layout is not certified yet: open important documents in Hangul before you send them.
- Admin › Folder permissions, Groups and AI engines › Usage limits: a deleted row now leaves the list at once. Before, the delete worked but the row stayed until the page was opened again, because the empty answer of a delete was taken for a failure. A refused delete keeps the row and says why. The keyboard focus then goes on instead of back to the top of the page: to the next row's delete where a confirm dialog comes first (folders, groups), and to the next row's Edit or the list's title where the delete happens at once (usage limits, and invites on Members), so a held Enter deletes one row, not the whole list. Such a delete also runs one at a time. The delete buttons say which row they delete, on Members and for your own AI connections too. Removing a skills manager in several folders no longer says "turned off" when one of them failed.
- Admin › Folder permissions: the texts now say what really happens. Before you remove all rules of a folder, the dialog shows who gains or loses which access, from the same "who sees what" as saving rules: access is decided per person, by the nearest rule above that names them, so the folder above is not the answer for everyone. While it asks, the delete button shows it is busy; when the check fails or takes more than 5 seconds, the dialog says how access is decided. The edit dialog no longer says that the owner can write everywhere: other people's personal folders and the system folder are hidden from the owner and admins too. In all five languages.
