- Admin › Folder permissions: shared, skills and a group's folder can no longer be removed from the list, and shared and skills can no longer be saved with no rule. With no rule left on shared or skills, everyone but the owner and admins followed the team folder's own rules, which are none by default, so the folder was easily hidden from the team by mistake; a group's folder lost its link to the group for good, and with it the protection that only admins move, rename or delete it. These rows now say why instead of showing the delete button, and the rule editor says so and does not save when the last rule of shared or skills is removed. To keep shared or skills to the owner and admins, give it the rule "Everyone: No access", as the message says. On a Mac, a rename that changes only the case (Shared) keeps the protection; on Linux, where Shared is another folder, it stays an ordinary one. When the server refuses a save, its reason now shows in the dialog. Deleting a group still removes its rules, and its folder row can go after that. Rows of folders in the trash are no longer listed or editable; they come back when the folder is restored. `GET /api/admin/folders` has `groupId` and `keep` (`team`, `group` or null) on each folder. In all five languages.
- Admin and Settings menus, the folder picker, tabs and the sheet tabs of a spreadsheet: the keyboard focus ring is whole now. Before, the menu's scrolling cut off its left and right sides.
- Admin: a Home page comes first. It lists what needs attention (an engine to sign in, people who cannot use AI, an AI connection or a service to fix, a disk filling up), then one card per area. The menu is grouped (People, Shared with the team, Activity, Settings) and stays in place while the page scrolls; on a phone it is a strip under the title. Long explanations on admin cards are one line with "More", and the AI engine cards show their state as a chip.
- Uploads: a file now shows its progress while it goes up, in a chat, on the file screen and in a folder upload. Before, a file up to 8 MB stayed at 0% until the upload ended, so on a phone connection it looked stuck, and a larger file moved only after each 8 MB. A file that is being prepared now says "Preparing", and when all its bytes are sent it says "Finishing" while the server answers. The number shows only while bytes go out, so it no longer stays at 0% or 100%. On a large file, the chat chip and the file list no longer go up to 100% during preparation and fall back to 0, and the number and a folder's bytes stay where they are when a part is sent again after a lost connection. A screen reader hears the file's name once and the end of the upload, not each step or number, and a failed upload is announced with the file's name. A folder upload says its failures and how it ended. In all five languages.
- Uploads, in every browser: a file up to 8 MB, and each 8 MB part of a larger file, now goes up as a copy in memory, three at a time on a page, so the rest of a long list waits as "Preparing" and can be removed there. A large file holds its place for one part at a time, so other files get in between. An upload with no byte out for 20 seconds lets the next one go ahead and goes on, so a stopped upload does not hold up the others, and at most six go at a time. Safari and iOS from 26.5 have a bug ([WebKit 319985](https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=319985)) that can send a file with no bytes on a page with a service worker, and such an upload failed with "The request is malformed". All browsers on an iPhone have it, and telling browsers apart is not safe enough, so all take the same way. A voice recording is copied too but does not wait in line, so uploads never hold up the message box.
- Sign-out: folder uploads now stop, as file uploads and chat attachments do. Before, the next person in the same tab saw the last person's folder and file paths, the rest of the folder went up with the next person's session, and "Try again" sent it in that person's name. When a session ends by itself, a large file stays remembered: pick it again after signing in and it goes on from the chunks the server has.
- Uploads: a file that reaches the server with another size than the sender read is now refused before it is stored. Before, a file that lost its bytes on the way was saved with 0 bytes, showed as attached, and with `onExists: replace` it replaced the file that was there. The page also compares the size in the server's answer. An empty file such as `.gitkeep` still goes up. `POST /api/uploads` and `POST /api/files/upload` take an optional `size` in decimal digits, and a sender that does not name one is served as before. In all five languages.
- File screen: an upload no longer redraws the whole file list at each step. With 200 rows and a CPU slowed four times, the page's own script during an upload fell from about 47% of the main thread to about 3%. An upload's row now stays when you open another page and come back, and it goes at sign-out. An upload that ends no longer brings back the folder it started in when you have gone to another one.
- Reading a spreadsheet, a Hangul file or a Word or PowerPoint file waits less behind background work. These reads share two office readers with the document text the server remakes in the background (after an update, for files a search left for later, and for search by meaning). Background work could hold both, at its lower priority, so on a busy server a read of a small file could wait about a minute. Now background work never takes both readers, and people go first in the line. A read can still wait while someone else's work has the other reader. Background work gets its turn after 8 of people's jobs or 30 seconds, whichever comes first, and then takes the next reader that comes free, so it cannot wait without end while people keep working. The same turn now holds in the line of the text cache, where background extraction could wait without end behind a long run of greps and uploads. Kinds of background work that overlap now run one after the other, so they finish later, also on a quiet server. No root update is needed.
- External services: a large answer that arrives in many small pieces uses much less CPU on the server. With chunked framing the server read the whole answer so far again with every network piece, so the cost grew with the square of the pieces: about 0.5 s of CPU for a 1 MB tool list that came in 1.4 KB pieces, 13 s for the largest list a check can read in its 90 seconds, and 4 s for a 20 MB image. Now it looks only when a piece may have ended a message, and these cost 0.25 s, 0.8 s and 0.25 s. "Check again" still takes as long as the service needs to send its list.
- Search by meaning (`MYCEL_SEMANTIC=on`): when a search comes while background indexing has a document in line for an office reader, that document now moves up, so with a reader free the search finds it at once. When both readers are busy, or the conversion is long, the search still waits for its 3 seconds; it then says how many changed files are not indexed yet. Before, it said none.
- External services: a tidier screen with two tabs. Connected shows one line per connection, with its details a click away; what needs attention (why it fails, signing in, "Run without asking") stays on the line. Discover lists 39 services with their logos to add by name: choose one, say who may use it, and sign in. "Add connection" still takes any MCP server or API by its address.
- Team skills: much less text. The title, tabs and a search now share one row, and each skill is one line (name, folder and description), the most used first with its count on the right. Click a row to start a chat with the skill; "…" also opens its file. Personal skills have their own section. The new-chat home keeps its "Frequently used skills" card, and a link in a notice now also changes the tab when the page is already open.

## 0.2.21 — 2026-11-03

[Published release and notes](https://github.com/trina-app/trina-ax/releases/tag/v0.2.21)

The tag v0.2.20 was not published: its macOS build got no runner.

**Upgrade note:** take a backup first (Admin > System > Backups > Back up now). The update needs one root update on every Linux server, because the kit has a new root file. One additive database migration; going back to 0.2.19 needs no database restore. On a server with the dev environment, run `sudo /opt/trina-ax/scripts/dev-env/build-image.sh` after the update.

- External services, requests to members: a name with only control or invisible characters is now refused, under the name field. Before, such a request was saved and showed with no name in the list and in the member's notice. In all five languages.
- Office Mac, Admin › Team settings › "Automatic update window": when the field is empty and a time set earlier with `teamax update time` is in use, the field now says so, with the window and its time zone. Before, the field looked empty and its hint said 03:00-05:00, which was not what ran. Nothing is written into the field. A window saved there applies instead, and the old time applies again if the field is emptied. Linux and hosted servers do not change. `GET /api/admin/office-update` has `windowSource` (`team`, `legacy` or `default`). In all five languages.
- Admin › System, update cards: the release notes are small text now, the same size as the rest of the card. Before, they showed at the size of a chat answer.
- Office Mac, the "new version" banner: it now shows the team's update window and time zone, for example "03:00-05:00 (Asia/Seoul)", the same as the update card. Before, it said "tonight at 03:00" with no zone, which was wrong for a window in the daytime and for anyone in another zone. A window that never opens no longer says that the update installs by itself. In all five languages.
- Office Mac, Admin › System › automatic updates: the last check and the last update now show in the team's time zone, like the next check, and each time names its zone. Before, they were in the browser's zone with no name, so one card could mix two zones. `teamax update status` prints the last check, the last update and the Windows app the same way. In all five languages.
- Admin > System > Plan: the plan card no longer shows memory and CPU. It shows answers at the same time, people, storage and backup days.
- AI connections: a token or key that is already connected now says where it is. Before, adding it answered only "This token or key is already connected", and an owner had to sign in as the member to find and delete a personal copy. The owner and admins now see the name of the team connection, or "<name>'s personal connection '<label>'": they hold the token they pasted. A member who adds a token that someone else on the team uses sees only "already connected on this team", never whose; their own connection is named. No part of the token or key is in the answer, not even the last 4 characters. A duplicate is still refused: one subscription's limit would be counted twice, and a copy would stay behind after the token changes.
- AI connections: "Move it to the team". When the owner adds a team connection with a token that is a member's personal connection, the dialog offers to move it. The owner types the password again. The personal connection is removed and the team connection is made in one step: there are never two copies, and if anything fails (a wrong password, the provider refuses the token) the personal connection stays as it was. The token's use this month, its last use and a provider limit it is waiting out carry over to the team connection, so one subscription is still counted once. When the team connection is for other people only, the dialog warns that the member may be left without AI. The member gets a notice if they have the app open (it says so when the team connection is not for them), and Admin › Activity log shows "Moved a personal AI connection to the team". API: `POST /api/admin/credentials` takes `moveFrom`, and its 409 has `params` (`reason: duplicate_credential`, `where`; for the owner and admins also `credentialId`, `label`, `ownerName`, `movable`). A ChatGPT sign-in that is already connected still says only "already connected", with no place and no move (not covered yet).
- Admin › Members shows "No AI" next to people who cannot use AI now: they have no personal AI connection and no team connection they may use. The rules are the ones a chat uses: subscription sharing, the groups and people a connection is for, switched off, expired, refused, the monthly block limit, and the engines that answer that person (an engine whose program is missing on the server answers nobody). A provider's limit that ends within the hour still counts, because that person only waits; a longer one (a weekly cap, a Codex plan limit until its reset) and "out of credit" or a spend limit, whatever the time, show "No AI". The owner's start checklist on the home page names these people with a link to AI connections, and stays until everyone can use AI. `GET /api/admin/users?noAi=1` has `noAi`; without the flag, or on a server that does not use managed AI connections, it is left out. In all five languages. No database migration.
- Linux servers: the install kit now has `mycel-boxd`, the root helper of the dev environment, at `root/mycel-boxd` (root-owned, like `mycel-sandbox`). The dev environment is still off by default: the file does nothing without `MYCEL_DEV_ENV=1` and the `mycel-boxd.service` unit. A hosted server gets the file and no unit. Because this is a new root file, the next release needs the root update once (`needsRoot`, and a new `rootSince` at that release): `mycel-update.timer` or `sudo mycel-ctl update` installs it.
- Linux servers with the dev environment: `mycel-ctl update` now restarts `mycel-boxd.service` when an update changes `root/mycel-boxd`, before the team server restarts, and at no other time. The restart stops the boxes that run; each starts again at its next use. A service that an administrator stopped stays stopped. If the restart fails, if boxd does not answer after it, or if boxd says that boxes are not available, the update goes on and its result says so. Do one step once: the update to the next release runs the older `mycel-ctl`, which does not know this restart, so run the new `scripts/dev-env/install-trial.sh` after it. The script sees that the service runs a program other than `root/mycel-boxd` and restarts it. Later updates need no step.
- `sudo mycel-ctl update` without `--json` now prints the update's notes, so they also reach the update timer's journal.
- Hosted teams and plan files: a team on a plan smaller than its disk now keeps to the plan's storage limit. A disk cannot shrink, so a team moved to a smaller plan (a refund, for example) kept the larger disk and could write until that disk was almost full. The plan's size is now the total in Admin › System, in diagnostics and in every write check, and the 80% notice uses it too. When the team goes over the limit, the owner and admins are told once, with the end date of a 7-day grace period. After the grace period, new saves, uploads and AI writes are refused until the use is under the limit. Read, download and delete keep working, and no data is deleted. Deleted files stay in the trash and still count, so the messages say to empty the trash. A hard limit of 2x the plan applies at all times, also during the grace period. When the use drops under the limit, or after an upgrade, writes work again with no action by an operator. Operators still see the real disk size. A full local backup counts in the use, so it now stops when the plan leaves less than 2 GB of room, as on a real disk of that size. Diagnostics show this, and a team over the limit, as a warning, not a failure. A server with no plan file does not change.
- Diagnostics: "disk size differs from the plan" now shows only when the disk is smaller than the plan, which means that a disk grow failed. A disk larger than the plan is normal after a move to a smaller plan.
- Dev environment (experiment): a box can no longer fill the server disk. The disk size of a box limits only its volume (`node_modules`, `.venv`, caches), and files in the project folder are on the server disk. The box now stops when the server disk has less than 2 GiB or 5% free, the larger of the two, and it does not start again until there is 1 GiB more than that. The people who may use the box and the admins are told why. Before each command, `box_shell` applies the same storage rule as AI writes. This is not a project quota: one project can still use all the space down to that limit.
- Dev environment (experiment), upgrade step: run `sudo /opt/trina-ax/scripts/dev-env/build-image.sh` once after this update. A box now has no `fallocate` and no ioctl of type `X`, because these calls can take all the disk space between two checks of the disk floor. Tools go on with normal writes. The update does not rebuild the profile, and a box takes the new one at its next start.
- Dev environment (experiment): long commands. `box_shell` has a new `job` mode for a render or a large build: the call returns at once and the chat does not wait. The person who started the job gets a notice with the exit code, the end of the log and a link to the chat when it ends, and the next `box_shell` call in that project gives the AI the result. The AI does not go on by itself. The project page shows running jobs with their time and last log line. Dev servers with `background` work as before, with no notice. One additive database migration (219); the schema reader level stays 4.
- Dev environment (experiment): a running render no longer stops at the idle time. A box now counts as active while it uses CPU, for at most 6 hours past the idle time, so an endless loop stops too. An idle dev server still stops.
- Hosted teams and plan files: copying a file or a folder, restoring a file version, `save_url` and connected-folder sync now respect the storage check. Before, they could write past it. A connected-folder sync that has no room leaves the file for the next sync.
- File screen: a whole folder can now go up, with no SSH. Use "Upload folder" (a computer with a big screen), or drop a folder on the page or on a folder row. The subfolders stay and are made in the open folder, so a project folder works. Three files go up at a time, through the same upload as one file: large files use chunked sessions up to 20 GB per file, and they go on from the chunks the server has after a dropped connection. One row shows files done, bytes and failures. One failed file does not stop the others, and "Try again" sends the failed files again. When the storage or the server disk has no room, the upload stops with one message, and "Try again" goes on after you free space. A folder of the same name is used as it is, and a file of the same name gets a new name, as for one file. System files such as `.DS_Store` and `Thumbs.db` stay out. In all five languages.
- Skills: a `SKILL.md` whose name is not valid (for example `name: acme-견적`) now gets a clear reason. The warning on the Skills page and on the file screen names the rule: lowercase a to z, 0 to 9 and hyphens, the same as the folder name. Before, the warning said only that the name was not valid. When the AI saves such a file in `skills/`, the confirmation card shows the reason before the save, and the AI gets the reason after a save in `skills/` or in `users/<id>/skills/`, so it can tell you and fix the file.
- Uploads: a "storage full" answer (507) to a large file now ends the upload at once. Before, the browser asked again for about 15 seconds.
- External services: an admin can now ask a member to connect a service as the member's own connection, without seeing the member's key and without signing in as them. Use this for a service that logs every call under the person whose key it is. In Admin › External services › "Requests to members", pick the member and fill in the service as for a team connection: kind, name, address, key or sign-in, the key's header, and usage notes for an API. The member gets an inbox entry and a banner that name the service and the host of its address, and the admin can copy a link to send. The link opens only for that member, signed in, and holds no key. In Settings › External services the member connects it with their own key or signs in, and it becomes their personal connection, made and checked like one they add themselves. Because the admin chooses the address, the connect screen warns, with the host in bold, that the key or sign-in goes to that host, and to go on only if it really is the service's address. The member can also decline. The admin sees whether each request is waiting, connected, declined, withdrawn or expired (after 7 days), and can withdraw a waiting one. The admin never sees the key or the member's connection. Admin › Activity log records requests made, connected, declined and withdrawn. In all five languages. Two additive database migrations (220 and 331); the schema reader level stays 4.

## 0.2.19 — 2026-11-02

[Published release and notes](https://github.com/trina-app/trina-ax/releases/tag/v0.2.19).

**Upgrade note:** take a backup first (Admin > System > Backups > Back up now). No database migrations in this release (the schema reader level stays 4, and 0.2.18 still opens the database). Linux servers need the root update once: mycel-ctl and the update timer's units changed. The old timer installs it at its old time (04:30 plus up to 90 minutes on the server's clock, 13:30–15:00 in Korea on a UTC server) and stops answers still running after 10 minutes; to pick the moment, run `sudo mycel-ctl update` at a quiet time before then. From this release on the timer keeps the team's update window.

- Security, office Mac with this Mac's own Claude login: two ways an AI answer could get an MCP server past the `~/.claude.json` cleanup are closed (both older than this release). A file that starts with a byte-order mark was skipped, though Claude Code reads it, and a very deeply nested file could not be written back, so the planted server stayed and nothing was logged. The server now reads the file as Claude Code does. A file it cannot read or write back is moved whole to `~/TeamAX/config/claude-mcp-backups/moved-…` (only the Mac user can read it), an empty config `{}` takes its place, and Admin › Activity log says so. A `.mcp.json` approval switch set to `1` or `"yes"` instead of `true` is removed too, because Claude Code accepts those, and so is the file's top-level `env` (with a copy, which `teamax claude-mcp restore` lists and puts back): Claude Code copies it into the environment of every command it runs, so a `BASH_ENV` or `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` planted there would act outside the sandbox. The engine sandbox no longer lets a turn lock these files (`chflags uchg`, an ACL), and the server unlocks one that was locked before. A cleanup that fails gets its own Activity log entry instead of "removed".
- Office Mac with this Mac's own Claude login (installed without `--claude-token-file`): the server no longer removes MCP settings without a copy or a word. In this mode it empties the MCP settings of the Mac user's `~/.claude.json` (user and project MCP servers, `.mcp.json` approvals, and the copies the Claude CLI keeps of the file) at every start and after every AI answer, so that an MCP server an AI turn might plant there cannot run outside the sandbox. On a Mac someone also used for their own Claude Code this deleted their MCP setups silently (on a test Mac: 2 user servers and the servers of 4 projects), and `--check-only` did not say so. Now everything it removes is first saved to `~/TeamAX/config/claude-mcp-backups/` (only the Mac user can read it, the AI engine cannot; Admin › Activity log names the copy). `office-install.sh` and `--check-only` list what would be removed, by name, and a new install stops until you choose: `--claude-token-file` keeps them (the server never touches `~/.claude.json` in that mode), `--remove-claude-mcp` lets them go, and the installer then saves the copy before the server first starts (it stops if it cannot). `teamax configure --claude-auth local-login` asks the same. `teamax claude-mcp check | backups | restore <copy>` shows them, lists the copies and merges one back without replacing anything, once the server no longer removes them; restore first lists every server in full (command, arguments, env, address) and needs `--yes`, since a copy may hold a server an AI turn planted. Installed offices and automatic updates go on without a question and keep copies from now on.
- Linux servers: the root update (`mycel-update.timer`) now keeps the team's update window. It ran at 04:30 plus up to 90 minutes in the server's own time zone, which is UTC on most Linux servers (13:30–15:00 in Korea), ignored Admin › Team settings › "Automatic update window", and after 10 minutes interrupted the answers still running. The timer now checks every 15 minutes and installs a root release only inside the team's update window in the team's time zone (03:00–05:00 when empty) while no answer is running. It waits for a quiet moment instead of cutting answers off (at most an hour per check, then the next check or the next night); a root release that found no quiet moment for three days is installed in the next window after waiting at most 10 minutes. A check outside the window ends at once, without network. A release that does not start (rolled back) or whose update fails is not downloaded and restarted again at every check: the timer tries it again after 20 hours (the next day's window), then after 44 and 92 hours if it keeps failing, at most once a week; a failure before the root files are in (a download cut short, for example) waits an hour, doubling up to 20 hours. `sudo mycel-ctl status` says until when (Admin › Diagnostics too, from this version on), `sudo mycel-ctl update` tries at once, and a newer release ends the wait. The server's own updater now also leaves a version the server rolled back for 7 days, as documented; it missed the roll-back record before. When the app was rolled back to a version that cannot tell the timer the team's window (0.2.18), the timer reads it with the newer version's own program instead of falling back to 03:00–05:00 on the server's UTC clock. This release changes root files: the old timer installs it, so a server's update to this version still comes at the old time.
- Linux servers: "automatic updates off" and "unsigned versions allowed" no longer stick without anyone seeing them. `sudo mycel-ctl auto-update on|off` and `sudo mycel-ctl allow-unsigned on|off` change them; the units are rendered again each time (and on every root update), so `root/install.json` and the units agree. The installer has `--auto-update` and `--require-signed`, and repair and update keep the update timer as it is now (a plain repair used to turn off a timer that had been turned on by hand). `sudo mycel-ctl status` and Admin › Diagnostics show both, with a warning while automatic updates are off or unsigned versions are accepted, in all five languages; the "needs administrator rights" line no longer says to wait for the timer on a server whose timer is off. A repair of an install that accepts unsigned versions works from the installed copy again when that copy is a signed release.
- Linux servers: a server that skipped a release with root changes now gets them. A release's `needsRoot` compared only with the releases before it, so a server two releases behind could run a new app on old root programs. The feed now names `rootSince`, the oldest release with the same root files; the update timer and the server's own updater compare it with `root/VERSION`. Servers on 0.2.18 or older ignore the field and go by `needsRoot` alone, so `needsRoot` now stays on for every release whose `rootSince` is newer than 0.2.18: such a server never puts a later app-only release on its old root files (after which it refused every release); its update timer installs the root files and the app together. Servers on this version or later still take app-only releases by themselves.
- `sudo mycel-ctl verify-bundle /var/lib/mycel/app/current` checks the version the link points to; it reported every file as missing.
- Office Mac: automatic updates keep the team's update window too. Admin › Team settings › "Automatic update window" did nothing on an office Mac, which updated at 03:00 on the Mac's own clock. The update agent now wakes every 15 minutes and goes on only inside the team's window in the team's time zone (03:00–05:00 when empty), the same setting as a Linux server's; `teamax update time 02:30` (or `02:00-05:00`) now changes that team setting. A time set with `teamax update time` before still counts while the team setting is empty. Admin › System and `teamax update status` show the window with its time zone and the next check in that zone. "Update channel" is gone from Team settings: no server read it (the channel is `teamax update channel` on an office Mac and `server.json` on Linux), and the update window is hidden on a hosted server, which its hosting service updates.
- Office Mac: the nightly update no longer cuts answers off. It waited at most 10 minutes for running answers and then installed, and did not wait at all when the server had not written its answer count lately. Now it waits until the update window closes (at most an hour per run) and leaves the update for the next check; a count the server has not written lately counts as running while the server answers. After three days without a quiet moment it waits at most 10 minutes, as "Update now" does. An empty `update/download/` folder is no longer left behind after an update.
- Grep on very large documents is fast again. To show where each line is ("[p.12]", "[시트 '매출' A12:F12]", "[슬라이드 3]"), the server used to read a matching document's whole text a second time. Now it reads only up to the matching lines, starting from a small index it keeps next to the text of documents of 1 MB or more. A grep over two 50 MB documents took 0.5–0.7 s and about 600 MB of extra server memory; it now takes 35–50 ms and no extra memory, the same as a grep without places (measured on a Mac). Documents whose text was made before this version have no index until they change; they are read in pieces, about 0.1–0.15 s and no extra memory. The labels are the same as before.
- Remaking document text in the background (after an update, for files a search left for later, and for search by meaning) now gives way to people's work. Its converters run at lower CPU and disk priority: on Linux `nice` 10 with the lowest normal disk priority, on a Mac the "utility" level (`nice` has no effect on macOS). A person's grep, uploads and previews go first when the server is busy; an idle server still does the work at full speed. Because a background converter may have to wait, it gets three times as long before it is stopped (its CPU-time limit on Linux is unchanged). No root update is needed.
- A document whose text could not be read in time is no longer left out of grep and search until it changes. When a converter ran out of time or was killed for memory (a busy Mac, where a background converter can wait a long time for the CPU, or a busy server), the server used to keep an empty text for the document. Now it keeps nothing and reads the document again after 2 minutes, then after 8; the last try runs at normal priority with the normal limits. A file that makes its converter crash or use up its CPU time is still kept as "no text" at once. Only after three stopped tries is the document kept as "no text" until it changes, as a file without text always was. Meanwhile grep says how many documents it could not search yet ("search again in a few minutes"), and so does the file screen's content search, in all five languages. A docx or pptx whose headers, comments or notes could not be read in time is read again the same way, instead of being kept with a line saying they were not read.
- External services: "Check again" works for a service whose tool list comes slowly. Higgsfield sends its 1.07 MB list at 17-38 KB/s, so the 30-second limit of a request cut it every time ("The service did not answer within 30 seconds") and the connection kept an older, shorter list (100 of 185 tools). The list is now read for as long as it keeps coming: it fails only when the service sends nothing for 30 seconds, or when the whole list is not in after 90 seconds ("The service did not send its whole tool list within 90 seconds."). Tool calls keep their 30 seconds, and a chat never waits for a check (it uses the saved list).
- External services: a check that fails for now no longer shows a working connection as "Error". When an MCP server with saved tools does not answer in time, cannot be reached for a moment, or answers HTTP 408, 429 or 5xx, the connection is "Connected", keeps its saved tools, and Settings says "Last check failed: … The AI keeps using the tool list from …" (toast: "Could not check the connection."). This also fixes a connection already showing "Error" for such a reason, like a Higgsfield connection the 30 seconds cut. A call that goes through now clears an error a failed check or call left, but never a sign-in that is needed; before, the connection showed "Error" while its tools kept working. In all five languages.
- Chat steps show a short look at a connected service tool's arguments, like Claude Code's tool inputs: "Higgsfield · generate_image (get_cost: true, model: "z_image", prompt: "…")". Flags and numbers come first, so a free price check and the paid run it was for are told apart. Stored keys are hidden. A value whose name says password, token, key, session or cookie (also `pwd`, `jwt`, `apiKey`, `Authorization`, `mfa_code`) shows as "•••", numbers too; counts such as `max_tokens: 4096`, names like `token_type` and plain flags stay readable. A URL's password, an Authorization value, a JWT or a secret query parameter inside any text is hidden too.
- Windows desktop apps on a Linux team server update again. The Windows app updates only from its own team server (`/downloads/latest.yml` and the installer it names), and only an office Mac put those there, so on a Linux server the Windows app never updated. Now a Linux server puts the Windows installer and `latest.yml` of the release it runs into its downloads folder 15 minutes after it starts (an update restarts it) and, while its automatic updates are on, every 6 hours after that. They come from the signed office manifest on the same feed; the signature and both files' size and SHA-256 are checked, a release newer than the server waits until the server has it, one installer is kept (older ones are removed), and nothing is downloaded when the disk would fall under the team folder's write reserve. Windows apps already installed need no reinstall: they find the new version at their next check (every 4 hours) and install it when they quit. Settings › Apps lists the installer too, its hint no longer tells a Linux server's owner to run a Mac command, and Admin › System › Diagnostics has a "Windows app" line that says what the last run did, in all five languages. Now, or on a server without internet: `admin.js desktop-refresh [--from /full/path] [--force]` (docs/INSTALL-LINUX.en.md "Windows app"). No root update is needed.
- A tab that is open while the server updates no longer goes blank. Clicking a page, a side panel or a viewer whose file the update removed now reloads the page once, for the new version, with a spinner meanwhile. It reloads at most once in 5 minutes per tab. It does not reload while something would be lost (a draft, a message that is still being sent or shows "not sent", an answer still streaming, an upload, including one on the file screen, or a recording) or while the server cannot be reached; it waits the same way as the reload after the server reports a new version. A recording counts while it is being transcribed too. The page then shows "This part of the page could not be loaded" with a Reload button, and when a message, an upload or a recording is not finished it says to finish it first, in all five languages. Reloading with that button, or with "Reload" on the new-version notice, puts the text of a message that was not sent back into its chat's input box. The installed web app's service worker keeps one cache per build and deletes the others when a new build starts, so its cache no longer grows by about 15 MB with every release.
- Update cards and the banner (Admin › System, office Mac and server): release notes show their first paragraph, rendered, with "Full release notes" for the rest; before, the whole notes appeared as raw Markdown, several screens long. The office Mac's "Version … is available" banner disappears once the server runs the new version (the page then says "A new version is ready. Reload"), and it follows a changed update time or a pause within 5 minutes, when the tab comes back into view, or at once from the update card. Admin › System › Diagnostics no longer says an office Mac updates by running office-install.sh from a newer zip: it updates itself. In all five languages.
- Windows desktop apps on hosted teams update too. A hosted team server now also puts the Windows installer and `latest.yml` into its downloads folder, 15 minutes after each start (the hosted service's update restarts it), from the signed feed of its own channel and never newer than itself; it does not look again every 6 hours. A server that the channel has passed (a hosted team waiting for its rollout, a self-installed server that has not updated yet) takes its own release's Windows app: from this release on, the feed keeps each release's signed office manifest (`office/<version>/<channel>.json`). For servers on an older release than that, the app follows once they run 0.2.19 or later. Settings › Apps tells the owner of a hosted team that the app appears by itself and that Admin › System › Diagnostics ("Windows app") says why when it does not; on a hosted team that line names no server command, in all five languages.

## 0.2.18 — 2026-11-01

[Published release and notes](https://github.com/trina-app/trina-ax/releases/tag/v0.2.18).

**Upgrade note:** two small database migrations (329 and 330, additions only: 329 adds a column to `chat_seen_urls`, 330 two columns and a trigger to `integrations`; the previous version still opens the database; the schema reader level stays 4). A connection whose tools an older server cut (Settings > External services says "100 of 150 tools") gets all of them when someone with the right to manage it presses "Check again".

- External services: a service with many tools works whole. A connection now keeps every tool its service lists (up to 1,000), so a service like Higgsfield, whose MCP server offers 186 tools (about 1 MB), no longer loses the ones after the first 46 (the old 256,000-character limit) or 100. Settings shows "186 tools" with a switch for each. When one answer cannot carry all of a person's connection tools (200 tools or 50,000 characters of definitions, Claude Code's line of about 10% of the context), the connections that do not fit are not sent tool by tool, like Claude Code's deferred MCP tools: the AI's instructions list their names, it looks one up with `find_tools` (by words or by name: its description and input schema) and runs it with `call_tool`, which checks the arguments against the tool's schema first (a bounded check, so a service's schema cannot stall the server) and then works exactly like the tool itself (who may use the connection, the write card, scheduled runs, external data). This works the same in Claude Code, Codex, the API engine and sub-agents, and the model proxy still allows only the tools of the turn. A tool turned off is not listed, not found and not run. For a Higgsfield-sized connection each model request carries about 1,500 characters of tool definitions instead of about 252,000, and the per-answer limit no longer leaves a connection with no tools. Steps say "Find tools "…"" and the tool's own name, in all five languages.
- External services: checking a connection no longer lets its server send the team server hundreds of megabytes, and chats no longer reread every saved tool list. "Check again" reads a server's tool list only until the list the team server keeps is full (1,000 tools or 4,000,000 characters), at most 20 pages and 16 MiB in all; before, a server that kept sending pages could make one check download about 800 MiB (100 pages of 8 MiB). When pages are left unread, Settings and the AI say "1,000 of more than 1,000 tools" instead of a number the server does not know, in all five languages. Each chat turn used to read and parse the saved tool list of every connection the person may use (up to 4 MB each, for up to 20 personal and 50 team connections); the server now keeps the parsed lists in memory (at most 64 MiB) and reads a list again only after it changed. Who may use a connection and which of its tools are turned off are still checked with every turn and every call.
- Copying an answer keeps where a file link points. A link to a team file at a page, a sheet (and its rows) or a slide pasted as a link to the start of the file, because the copy dropped `&page=2`, `&sheet=…`, `&range=…` and `&slide=…`; now the pasted link keeps every part of the address the link in the app had.
- Voice input in confidential chats no longer goes to OpenRouter. The server did not know which chat a recording was for, so a confidential chat's voice input went to the team's speech provider like any other (the sound of files never did). Now the message box tells the server the chat, and the server refuses to send a confidential chat's recording out; the message box says so before you start recording ("기밀 대화에서는 녹음을 바깥 음성 서비스(OpenRouter)로 보내지 않아서 음성 입력을 쓸 수 없어요."), in all five languages. With "On this server" voice input still works there, since nothing leaves the server. A new chat counts as confidential only while every chat is (Safe operations 1A).
- Scheduled tasks: pressing "Run now" just before a task's time no longer starts its timed run at the same moment. The timed run now waits until the run that is going has finished, then runs once (Admin › Activity log shows "Scheduled task waited"); it is not skipped, so a one-time task still runs at its time.
- PDFs the AI makes are set in the language they are written in. Every PDF used Korean settings and Korean fonts first, so Japanese and Chinese documents got Korean-style character shapes. Now a Japanese document uses Japanese fonts first, a Chinese one Chinese fonts (simplified or traditional), and one without Korean, Japanese or Chinese text follows the team language.
- Korean: the office Mac's menu bar app, its installer's messages in Terminal and the README in the office kit now use the same polite, plain style as the rest of the app (해요체 instead of 합니다체). The install guide quoted a chat message that no longer exists ("팀의 Claude 구독이 아직 연결되지 않았습니다"); it now shows what the chat says today ("이 엔진에 쓸 AI 연결이 없어요. 관리자에게 알려 주세요.").
- Skills: when the AI looks for a skill with words that are in none of them (for example Korean words for a skill described in English), it is now shown the skills you use most instead of nothing, so it can still pick the right one from their descriptions. A skill's own folder or file name now counts as much as its name (a skill named "quote" in a folder called "견적서" is found first for "견적서"). Only skills you can open are ever shown, as before.
- Linux servers: the root update (`mycel-update.timer`, and `sudo mycel-ctl update` without `--version`) no longer installs a release older than the app that runs. A server on a pre-release build such as 0.2.18-pre.1, whose feed still named 0.2.17, would have been taken back to 0.2.17 by the timer (the app's own updater already ignored the older feed); it now keeps its version and logs `the feed's release 0.2.17 is older than the installed 0.2.18-pre.1…, not installed`. The order is the app updater's (semver: a release is newer than its pre-releases, `pre.1` before `qa4`, `0.2.10` after `0.2.9`). The same version is skipped without a download unless the root files are older than it, which then catch up. Going back on purpose still works: `sudo mycel-ctl update --version 0.2.17` (newer root files stay). mycel-ctl also reads versions exactly like the server in a few unusual forms (`1.0.0--1`, `1.2a.0`, 20-digit numbers), checked on one shared table by the Go and TypeScript tests.
- Web research asks much less, closer to Claude Code. The AI now opens up to 30 search results and links of pages it read per answer without asking, and 150 per chat (was 8 and 30). A research answer that reads 25 search-result pages and follows 5 of their links used to ask 22 times; now it asks none (measured on 4 such answers in one chat: 90 questions before, 0 now). A scheduled task's run keeps 8 per run, since nobody is there to watch it. Operators can still set the numbers with `MYCEL_WEB_LINK_BUDGET` ("30,150", and a third number for scheduled runs).
- Safety, which is what makes that possible: a search result or a link on a page that contains what the AI had sent to a tool earlier in the chat (a site can repeat what it was sent, with your data in it) now always asks, however many links are left, and the question says why. A redirect to such an address asks too, for pages the AI reads as it already did for files it saves. Before, only addresses from connected services were checked this way. Addresses the AI writes itself still ask once per site, and the other rules (long addresses, the office network, confidential chats) are unchanged. In all five languages.
- Dev environment (experiment): the code is in this release, off by default. It needs `MYCEL_DEV_ENV=1` and the root helper `mycel-boxd`, which the installer does not add. Without them nothing changes.

## 0.2.17 — 2026-11-01

[Published release and notes](https://github.com/trina-app/trina-ax/releases/tag/v0.2.17).

**Upgrade note:** take a backup first (Admin > System > Backups > Back up now). Five small database migrations (217, 218, 325, 327 and 328, additions only: the previous version still opens the database; the schema reader level stays 4). 325 adds two tables for save_url (addresses connected services returned in a chat, and the marks of what the AI sent, for the echo check). 328 keeps the tools turned off per connection (`integrations.disabled_tools`); if you go back to 0.2.16, it offers every tool of a connection again until you update. After the update the server makes its text of Office, PDF and Hangul files again in the background, newest first and at most half its time; grep keeps using the old text until a file's new text is ready (roughly 1–3 hours for 10,000 documents on a 2-CPU server). Where search by meaning is turned on (`MYCEL_SEMANTIC=on`), its pieces of those files are made again the same way, while the old ones keep answering. Linux servers need the root update once for this release: mycel-ctl has the new tenant command `hosted-speech-key-set` (the update timer installs it, or run `sudo mycel-ctl update`). One new team setting, "Turn video sound into text" (Admin > Team settings > Voice input): it starts at its default, on when voice input runs on this server (off on a server with 2 CPU cores or fewer, where it is slow) and off when it uses OpenRouter. On a hosted server, once the hosting service has handed it its speech key, voice input (OpenRouter) and "Turn video sound into text" start on, with 10 free hours of sound a month; an admin's own choice still wins. Search by meaning is now off unless `MYCEL_SEMANTIC=on` is set. Linux servers use the distribution's `ffmpeg` package (the installer already installs it and stops without it). Keep the server's security updates on: ffmpeg's decoders read files people upload.

- Hosted servers (every plan, the free one too): speech-to-text is included, one pool of 10 hours of sound a team a month (Korea time) shared by voice input and the AI's video and audio reads. The hosting service hands each team's server its own OpenRouter key (the new tenant command `hosted-speech-key-set`; mycel-ctl and `admin.js hosted-speech-key`), kept encrypted like the team's own key and never shown. With it, voice input is on by default (OpenRouter) and "Turn video sound into text" defaults on; an admin's choice, off too, still wins. The sound goes through OpenRouter to DeepInfra in the US, or Groq in the US when DeepInfra is down, with zero data retention. Admin › Team settings › Voice input shows "This month: N of 10 free transcription hours used"; past 10 hours voice input and video reads say so and stop until the 1st. A team's own OpenRouter key comes first and is not counted. A server that is not hosted removes the hosting key at start. In all five languages.
- Voice input and video sound through OpenRouter: when the provider fails (5xx) or does not answer in time, the request is sent once more (OpenRouter may send it to another provider), after the same checks as new sound (in a read: the switch, the provider, a confidential lock, the person's access to the file); only both failing is shown.
- Chat: the AI reads video and audio files, like a Claude Code user with ffmpeg and whisper. Reading a video (mp4, mov, m4v, webm, mkv, avi, 3gp) gives evenly spaced frames as images with their times and a timed transcript of the sound between them; an audio file (mp3, m4a, wav, aac, flac, ogg, opus, amr, aiff, caf, wma) gives the transcript. The AI can look at a part (`time`, for example "1:00-2:30"), ask for fewer, bigger frames for small text on a screen, or skip the sound. A read covers up to 10 minutes of sound next to frames and 30 minutes without them, and says what to read next (a 1-hour meeting takes two reads with OpenRouter; this server's speech model works in 30-second pieces and keeps each one it finished, as many a read as its speed allows, at most 2 minutes of new sound a read on a hosted server). Files up to 1 GB. The step reads "Watch video · …" / "Listen to audio · …". Linux servers only: an office Mac says it cannot read video yet.
- Privacy: the sound of a video or audio file is turned into text by the team's speech provider only while "Turn video sound into text" is on. With OpenRouter, turning it on sends the sound of team files (meeting recordings, calls) to OpenRouter and its provider, which the setting says; a new provider starts from its own default again. Confidential chats never send sound out (a transcript the server already made may be used there). Before each new piece of sound the setting, the provider, the chat's confidentiality and the person's access to the file are checked again, so turning the setting off or taking access away stops a read that is already running. This server's speech model is never downloaded by a read. Transcripts are kept by content on the server for up to 30 days (200 MB, not in backups), so reading the same file again costs nothing, and each new transcript is an activity record without its words. In all five languages.
- Video and audio sound on this server (whisper.cpp, the "On this server" speech provider): a read makes its frames first and gives the sound the time left, so a slow transcript never leaves a video without its frames. The sound goes in 30-second pieces, as many as fit in the read's 5 minutes at the speed the server measured on itself (voice input measures it too; one unusually slow run cannot stop later reads); the result says how far the transcript got, and reading again goes on from there (finished pieces are kept). Silent parts (gaps with no sound at all; quiet speech still counts as sound) are not transcribed and lines over them are dropped (whisper made words up there), each part keeps its own language, and when whisper is unsure of a few seconds of speech it tries again told the file's language (else the team's), keeping that only when it is not a translation (never reported as the language heard). whisper.cpp uses 2 threads on a 2-core server. On a server with 2 CPU cores or fewer "Turn video sound into text" starts off; the read and the setting say why, and an admin can turn it on. The setting's text follows the server (piece length, a hosted server's 2 minutes a read, the measured speed). A hosted server still transcribes at most 2 minutes of new sound a read. In all five languages.
- Voice input goes first: while someone dictates, a video read's transcript waits (a piece that was running stops and is made again after it), so dictation no longer takes minutes next to a video read on a small server. A read waits for a long voice input job at most 45 seconds, then says voice input is using the transcription and that reading again goes on.
- Admin > Team settings: "Send images and PDF pages to the AI" is now "Send images, PDF pages and video frames to the AI" (the same setting); video frames follow it, the API engine and the per-answer image limit like images. Picking another speech provider in the form and back no longer turns "Turn video sound into text" back on; only a save that changes the provider starts it again from that provider's default.
- Security: video decoding runs in the converter isolation with allow-lists of formats and decoders (by decoder name, checked on Debian 12's ffmpeg 5.1 and Debian 13's 7.1), one thread, time and memory limits; frames come on ffmpeg's output and are drawn again by the image normalizer. A playlist or concat file disguised as a video never opens another file. Only a video's length, size, codecs and frame rate reach the AI, never its title, comment or subtitle text, and the transcript is marked as file content, not instructions.
- Chat: under each answer, "참고한 파일 N개" (files referenced) lists the team files the AI opened while writing it: what it read, and what it saw in search or grep results (files it only found by name are not listed). Open it to see each file with the pages, sheet ranges, slides or sections it looked at; a click opens the file next to the chat at that place. Files the AI saw only in search results, and files only a sub-task read, say so. The server records the list itself (it is not the AI's claim), and checks it again for you every time you open it: a file you can no longer open (access taken away, deleted, moved) shows only as "볼 수 없는 파일 N개", without its name. The list is shown only on screen, never in notifications or exports. In all five languages.
- The AI sees where in a document something is: grep results show the page, sheet row or slide next to each line ("[p.12]", "[시트 '매출' A12:F12]", "[슬라이드 3]"), reading a spreadsheet keeps the real row numbers (a line says where blank rows were left out), a presentation shows where each slide starts (hidden slides counted, as PowerPoint numbers them), and Word and Hangul headings show as headings. The AI can link the place in its answer. Scanned PDFs read on a Mac keep their page breaks. Where search by meaning is on, its results show "매출보고서.pdf · p.12", "시트 '매출' A1:F40", "슬라이드 3" or "§ 환불 정책" with a ready-made link, and its pieces no longer run across two pages or slides.
- File previews open at a place: the AI's links and the list under an answer open a PDF at the page, a spreadsheet at the sheet with the rows marked, a presentation at the slide.
- Korean: the AI's image notes and voice input errors now use the same polite, plain style as the rest of the app (해요체 instead of 합니다체), like the team server's other messages in 0.2.16. The meaning is unchanged.
- Search by meaning (the AI's `search` tool) is off by default. In a test with a folder of about 2,900 files it put unrelated passages ahead of the right document for most questions, used up to about 1.9 GB of memory while indexing and kept most CPU cores busy for minutes; the AI finds documents with grep and file names, as before. `MYCEL_SEMANTIC=on` turns it back on. An index and model already downloaded stay on disk (about 120 MB plus the index) and are used again when it is turned on.
- Team skills: every person now sees the skills they can use. A member's Team skills page used to show no skill at all (only their own suggestions; "Frequently used skills" appears only once a skill has been used). The page now opens on "Available skills", the same list as the "/" menu: team skills in folders the person can read and their own personal skills, each with its description and file, "Use in chat" and "View file". Nothing there can be changed; reviewing suggestions and recent changes stay with the people who manage skills and admins. In all five languages.
- Skill picker ("/" in the message box): every matching skill is listed, not only the first eight, and the list scrolls. The sixth row was cut in half and the arrow keys moved to rows you could not see; now the chosen row stays in sight, also on a phone.
- Counts say "1 person", "1 use", "1 time", "1 file", "1 unread notification" in English and Spanish instead of "1 people", "1 uses" and so on: on the Skills page and the new-chat home, the "more people will see this" questions, notifications, Admin › System, the desktop browser's "more questions waiting", and the team server's cards when the AI saves where more people can see it or moves or copies files ("1 item" instead of "1 item(s)", "Move it?" for one file). Japanese, Korean and Indonesian do not change a word with the number and read as before.
- Desktop app: the browser's question before the AI uses a site now says what the AI is doing in plain words, such as "Open browser · https://…" or "Open page · https://…", the same words as the step under the answer. It used to show the internal tool name ("preview_start").
- Skills: the AI now knows every skill you can use, not only the first 50. Before, skills after the 50th (by name, often plugin skills and skills in later folders) were left out of its list without a word, so it never picked them on its own; typing "/name" still worked. Now each skill is one short line (its name and what it is for), like in Claude Code, and the AI loads the full steps with a new skill tool when it uses one ("Use skill · …" under the answer). When a team has more skills than fit, every name stays; your own skills and the ones you use most keep their description, the AI can search the rest ("Find skills …"), and the server points it to the skills that match your message. Skills in folders you cannot open are never named, found or loaded, by the AI or its helpers.
- Skills: a chat that goes on is told which skills were added, changed or removed since it started, so a skill made during a conversation can be used in it.
- Skills: the Team skills page says how many skills the AI sees with their description and by name only, when not all of them fit. The server now reads up to 1,000 skill files per folder (was 200) and tells the people who manage team skills when there are more. Team rules: when there are more than 10 rule files, the AI is told how many it does not get and the managers see a note (only the first 10 are followed, as before).
- External services: a service that offers more tools than the AI can use shows "100 of 150 tools" in Settings, and the AI is told so (at most 100 tools per service and 200 in one answer, as before).
- Speech through OpenRouter (voice input and the AI's video and audio reads): a long recording no longer comes back in the wrong language. The speech model picks one language for a whole request from its first 30 seconds, so a 13-minute Korean meeting whose first voice it took for Japanese came back translated into Japanese. Now a recording or piece of a minute or more that it heard in an unlikely language (not the team's language or English, words it was unsure of, or a language other parts of the file were not in) is sent once more told the file's language or the team's, and the answer the model is surer of is kept; a Japanese meeting stays Japanese. On that meeting the Korean text went from almost all wrong to about 1 character in 5 (as one voice recording, 1 in 12), and its timestamps are right again. A language it was told is not shown as the language heard. Confidential chats still send nothing.
- Speech through OpenRouter: quiet recordings are raised before they are sent (a room microphone at -45 dBFS: about 1 error in 10 characters before, under 1 in 100 now), never far enough to make the model invent words over room noise.
- Speech through OpenRouter: the sound is sent as FLAC, which is lossless, so the text is exactly what it was, at about 40–50% of the upload (10 minutes: 7–9.5 MB instead of 19 MB), which keeps long pieces further inside OpenRouter's one-minute limit on a slow connection. A recording's title and comment tags are no longer sent with it.
- Speech through OpenRouter: the request asks for DeepInfra, and for Groq when DeepInfra is down, and no other provider (with zero data retention and no data collection, as before); OpenRouter does not apply this to speech yet, so a key's own provider settings on OpenRouter decide for now. The answer's language is read the same way from every provider ("Korean" from Groq is `ko`). Each activity record of a video or audio transcript now also says which provider served each request (looked up at OpenRouter a few seconds after the read; never the words).
- Self-hosted servers: Claude Code can answer with another company's models through an Anthropic-compatible gateway (OpenRouter; the Anthropic-compatible APIs of DeepSeek, Kimi, GLM): `engine.anthropicGateway` in server.json, managed mode only, no admin screen. Only the model ids set there reach the gateway (never a `claude-*` name); Claude Code's opus / sonnet / haiku aliases and its side requests use them, and Admin › AI engines and the team default list them. The gateway's key is added in Admin › AI connections as "API key for <name>" (an OpenRouter key is checked on its `/api/v1/key`). While it is set, Claude subscription connections and Claude API keys added for Anthropic are off on the server: they are kept, and nothing is sent with them. Costs use what OpenRouter reports it charged, or the prices in server.json. Conversations go to the gateway provider and the model's vendor. [Configuration](docs/CONFIGURATION.md#anthropic-compatible-gateway). In all five languages.
- Copying an answer keeps its formatting, like Claude's copy. "Copy answer" now puts two versions on the clipboard: formatted text and, as before, the answer's Markdown. Pasting into Gmail, Outlook, Word or Google Docs gives headings, bold, lists, links, code blocks and real tables with borders; pasting into a plain text field gives the Markdown. Images from other sites become links and never load where you paste them; links to team files point to the file on the team server, so whoever opens one signs in first. Math and diagrams are pasted as their source. Works over plain http on an office network and in the desktop app too.
- Settings › Memory: add a memory yourself and correct one in place, not only delete. "Add memory" opens a field at the top of the list; the pencil on a memory opens the same field with its text. The rules are the AI's own: one line of up to 300 characters (line breaks and runs of spaces become one space), not empty; Enter saves and Esc leaves it as it was. A memory you correct counts as yours from then on, and its source chat link stays. The audit log records that someone added, changed or deleted one of their own memories, never the text. Memories still keep no earlier versions. In all five languages.
- Settings › Scheduled tasks: "Run now" runs a task once right away, in a new chat ("Open chat" in the note takes you there), with the same rules as a run at its time: nobody is asked anything, only the addresses and connected services its card allowed are used without asking, your AI usage limit and your folder access apply. The schedule and on/off stay as they are, and a paused task can be run now too. A task nobody confirmed, one that is running, or a used-up limit is refused with the reason. The pencil edits a task: its title, instruction, schedule and the project it runs in. Before anything changes you see the same card as when the AI creates a scheduled task (the whole instruction, the addresses it opens, an address with a long query listed apart because runs never open it, the connected services it may send to), and only an answer on that card saves. A template's time and folders can be changed too, checked like when it was made. A paused task now says that it does not run at its times. Pausing, resuming, editing and running now are in the audit log (never the title or the instruction). In all five languages.
- External services: turn single tools of an MCP connection on or off, like the tool switches of Claude's connectors. Open a connection's tools (Settings › External services for your own, Admin › External services for the team's): each tool has a switch. A tool you turn off is not offered to the AI at all and a call of it is refused; the AI is told how many are off. Tools the service adds later are on; a tool you turned off stays off when the service drops it and brings it back. People who use a team connection see which tools are off; only admins change them, and only you change your own connections. In all five languages.
- The AI can save files from the web into the team folder with a new tool, save_url, like Claude Code's `curl -o`: PNG, JPEG, GIF and WebP images, PDF, MP4, MOV and WebM video, MP3, WAV, M4A, AAC, FLAC and Ogg (Vorbis, Opus) audio (what image, video and voice services such as fal, Replicate and ElevenLabs return). The type is judged by the file's first bytes, never by what the site says, and the extension is corrected to match; SVG, HTML, archives and programs are refused, and images are also decoded in the isolated converter as a check (the original bytes are saved). The address rules are web_fetch's (every redirect checked, the card for an address the conversation never showed), and the save is like make_file's (your permissions checked again, never under `skills/`, a version or a conflict copy, the wider-audience card, the activity record with the full address and SHA-256). The download streams to disk: there is no size limit, but a file over 1 GB asks first with its size and the server's free disk ("이 파일은 3.2 GB예요. 서버 디스크 여유는 40 GB예요. 받을까요?", in all five languages), a scheduled run skips such a file with a note, and a download that would leave less than 10% of the disk free (at least 5 GB, at most 20 GB) is refused, up front or while it streams. A download also has a total time budget of 30 minutes (the wait for a card does not count), so a site that sends one byte at a time cannot keep it alive forever; and a non-image whose size the site never declares (the file ends only when the connection closes, so a cut-off cannot be told from a complete file) is saved with a note that completeness could not be verified.
- Images from connected services (an image generator over MCP, such as Higgsfield): the addresses in a service's answer and its resource links open in save_url without a card, within the same budget as search results (8 per answer, 30 per chat), unless the AI had sent that address or a long random-looking part of it to a tool earlier in the chat (a service may repeat what it was sent; then it asks, with its own card text). This also holds when the service gave it back with another query, wrapped in its own redirect address, or with its case changed (including a random-looking part the AI sent as plain text and the service put into a web address in another case, such as a `<data>.attacker` subdomain), however long the AI's input was, and a redirect to such an address asks too (a link shortener cannot pass it on). An image, audio clip or file the service sends inside its answer is kept with the chat for 24 hours as an `mcp-file:` handle that save_url saves without asking the service again (at most 200 MB a chat, 1 GB a person and 4 GB across the server, the oldest dropped first), and the AI sees the image when "Send images, PDF pages and video frames to the AI" is on. A tool's answer may now be up to 25 MB (it was 1 MB, so a large inline image failed the call); other requests keep 1 MB.
- HTML previews show team images. `<img src="images/hero.png">` in `landing/index.html` now shows `landing/images/hero.png` in the file preview (and team paths in HTML and SVG artifacts), fetched with the viewer's own permissions and put into the page before it goes into the sandboxed frame, whose policy is unchanged. A file preview only takes files that everyone who can open the page can open too. Images from the web stay blocked: they show as a grey placeholder and a note says "바깥 이미지 N개는 미리보기에서 막혀요 — AI에게 파일로 저장해 달라고 하세요" (in all five languages). Images a React artifact's script creates are not covered yet.
- The AI is told how: save an image with save_url next to the page, right away (result links expire), and reference it by a relative path.
- Security: generate_video now downloads the finished video the way web_fetch opens pages. Before, only the first address was checked and the redirects that followed went through a plain `fetch`: a result address that redirected to a private, office-network or cloud-metadata address (such as `169.254.169.254`) was followed. Now every hop must be https, every redirect's address is checked again from the start (public internet addresses only; never private, office-network, metadata, this server or the office's own exit address), the connection goes to exactly the address that was checked, and the video streams to disk instead of being held in memory. The 200 MB limit is unchanged.

## 0.2.16 — 2026-11-01

[Published release and notes](https://github.com/trina-app/trina-ax/releases/tag/v0.2.16).

**Upgrade note:** no database migrations in this release (the schema reader level stays 4, and 0.2.15 still opens the database). Linux servers need the root update once for this release: its root programs are the first built without the commit stamp, so they differ from 0.2.15's; the update timer installs it, or run `sudo mycel-ctl update`. Later releases that do not change these programs need no root step. Nothing to change in settings.

- Linux servers (and Macs set up with `install-macos.sh`) no longer fill their disk with old versions. Every update added a whole version folder under `app/versions` (about 540 MB unpacked) and nothing removed the old ones: at one release a day that is about 16 GB a month, enough to fill a hosted Starter server within months. Now the server account removes them by itself, 10 minutes after the server starts and then every hour: every version folder except the current one, the previous one (Admin › System › Update › Roll back, and where the bootstrap goes when the current one no longer verifies), the newest one downloaded but not applied yet, and any changed in the last 24 hours; also `.staging-*` and `*.partial` folders an interrupted update left (once a day old) and engine folders `app/engines/<name>/<sha12>` that `current` no longer points to (a day after the installer changed the engine). Never while an update is pending, never following a link, never into a mount point, no root step. A folder is first renamed to `.prune-<hex>` and then removed, so a removal cut short leaves no half version; such a leftover goes a day later. A folder that changes while it is being taken out and cannot get its name back is kept as `.prune-hold-<hex>` (never removed) and named in Diagnostics. A folder the server account cannot remove (owned by another account, such as root's copy from an installer that was stopped) is named in Diagnostics for root to remove. `app/` stays at about 1–1.5 GB. The first run after the update to 0.2.16 removes what has collected (in a test: 9 versions, 4.5 GB, about 40,000 files in 5 seconds). `admin.js prune-versions --dry-run` lists what it would remove and `admin.js prune-versions` removes it now. Admin › System › Diagnostics shows the kept versions and their size ("Kept versions", in all five languages). Office Macs keep `app`, `app.previous` and `app.failed` as before.
- `mycel-ctl update` removes the work folder an earlier update left in `.install-tmp` when it was killed (a download and an unpacked bundle, about 600 MB).
- Office Macs: when several releases in a row fail to install, only the last one's download and unpacked bundle stay for a retry (before, each failed release's stayed until an update worked).
- Release builds use exactly Go 1.27.3, the Go that built 0.2.15 (`GO_VERSION` in both workflows, the same as `versions.json`), so a new Go patch or runner image no longer changes the root programs and makes a release need the root update. `docs/RELEASE-CI.md` says how to move it.
- PDFs the AI makes on a Linux server now show Korean, Japanese and Chinese. Before, these characters came out as empty boxes, because a Linux server has no font for them (only DejaVu) and typst fell back to its own font without them. The Linux server kit now carries the Noto Sans CJK fonts (2.004, Regular and Bold, OFL-1.1) in the version folder and make_file gives them to typst; headings and bold text are bold. Installed servers get the fonts with their normal update: no root step, no package to install. PDFs made before need to be made again. The kit grows by about 32 MB (40 MB unpacked). The fonts' notice and licence are in `licenses/noto-sans-cjk/`. Office Macs are unchanged: they use the Mac's own fonts. Word files were never affected.
- Linux server updates: the root programs (mycel-ctl, the launcher, the sandbox helper, the bootstrap, mycel-safeops) no longer change with every release. They carried the commit they were built from, so every release so far needed the root update ("Administrator rights needed"), even when these programs had not changed. Now a release that does not change them needs no root step; the first release with this change still needs it once, since its programs are the first without the commit.
- HWP preview: when the HWP Preview plugin is installed but turned off, an admin is now told to turn it on in Admin › Plugins (before: "Admins can add it"). In all five languages.
- AI image reads: when a converter replaced its work folder, the AI is told the file could not be read (damaged), not that it was too heavy.
- Look and contrast: the office update banner and the hosting notices now write their text in the darker accent colour (5.2:1 on their tint in light, was 3.3:1), and so do the links in Admin (Engines, System, AI connections, Team settings). Bordered buttons (such as "Always allow", "Try again" and "Restore") have an edge at least 3:1 against the page instead of a faint hairline. Switches: the off track is darker (at least 3.3:1, was about 2:1) and in dark the knob turns warm black on the orange when on (6.6:1, a white one was 2.9:1; in light it stays white, 3.7:1), and the keyboard focus ring sits outside the track so it also shows when the switch is on. The danger button's border is stronger (3.5:1 or more, was about 2:1). The quiet delete buttons (Delete on a project, turning off a skill manager's folder) are red again, as intended (they showed grey); a plain "no" stays neutral, like Claude Code's: "Deny" on the in-app browser's site card and "Reject" on a team skill review; the delete icon of an external service turns red on hover. Light and dark.
- The app's font is now named as what it is: the system face (Apple SD Gothic Neo and SF on Macs and iPhones, Segoe UI with Malgun Gothic on Windows, Noto Sans KR on Linux). Pretendard was listed first but never shipped, so nothing changes unless Pretendard is installed on your computer. AI-made pages (artifacts) use the same default.
- The phone's browser bar and the installed app's title bar use Trina's page colour in light and dark (`theme-color`), not the older green-grey theme's.
- The mobile app's default accent is Trina's orange, with a dark label on its Connect button so it stays readable; the team address screen uses Trina's colours and a field edge you can see.
- Desktop app: the welcome screen and the browser toolbar use the same colours as the web app (the focus ring is the accent, dark-mode error red, warning tint), bordered buttons and fields have a 3:1 edge, and orange text is the darker accent text. The office Mac's helper page (the certificate install steps) uses Trina's colours instead of the older green.
- Korean: messages from the team server (sign-in, files, system and update status, safety checks, the office Mac's helper and command line, the relay's pages) and a few app texts now use the same polite, plain style as the rest of the app (해요체 instead of 합니다체). The meaning is unchanged. Error notes and steps saved in earlier answers still change language with the screen.
- The icon script paints full-bleed app icons on the logo's own dark tile colour instead of the old green; the committed icons already looked like that, so they are unchanged.

## 0.2.15 — 2026-11-01

[Published release and notes](https://github.com/trina-app/trina-ax/releases/tag/v0.2.15).

**Upgrade note:** no database migrations in this release (the schema reader level stays 4, and 0.2.14 still opens the database). Nothing to change in settings.

- Files: drag rows onto a folder to move them there, like Finder or Google Drive. Dragging a selected row moves the whole selection; dragging any other row moves just that row and leaves the selection as it was. Folders can be dragged too, but never into themselves or their own subfolders. A breadcrumb also takes the drop to move items up. While you drag, the breadcrumbs show in place of the selection bar and the dragged rows are dimmed. A folder you can drop on is highlighted and says "Move here". A read-only or navigation-only folder says "Can't move here" and takes nothing. The move follows the same rules as the "Move" action: per-item permission checks, read-only items skipped and counted, one question for the batch if the move hides items from other people, renaming on a name clash, failures listed per item, and the same toast. The "Move" action stays the keyboard way to do it. Computer files dropped on a folder row are uploaded into that folder, and the upload list shows where each one goes. Dragging a file into a chat's message box still attaches it. In all five languages.
- Text fields: the edges of inputs, selects and text areas (the message box too) now have at least 3:1 contrast against the page in light and dark (WCAG 1.4.11; they were 1.28:1). A new `--c-field-border` token is used only for field edges, so cards and dividers keep their lighter lines. A focused field gets a 2px accent edge, at least 3:1 against the page. The chat's message box stays quiet, as in Claude: focused, its edge only turns darker.
- Security: what a converter leaves in its work folder (ffmpeg, whisper.cpp, heif-dec, pandoc, typst, the HWP renderer) is now used only when it is a plain file the converter itself wrote there, and a run whose work folder no longer is the one the server made is refused. Before, a converter taken over by a hostile file could make the server use another file it can read as the converter's result: sent to OpenRouter for transcription, or shown as a transcript, an attachment thumbnail, a HEIC or HWP preview, or the text of a new document; make_file could also write outside its work folder. The attachment thumbnail now comes straight from ffmpeg's output, and make_file writes only under names nothing in the work folder has taken.

## 0.2.14 — 2026-10-31

[Published release and notes](https://github.com/trina-app/trina-ax/releases/tag/v0.2.14).
